> Revision 16 of the LIVE-INT PLAN — **Product-narrowed; rev 12 through rev 16 are contract alignments inside the locked bar**. Revision 1 (PR #39/#40/#41) locked Founder product decisions P1–P3 and closed Codex LIVE-01..LIVE-10; revision 2 (PR #42, `main` @ `75ae1fa`) closed Codex REV1-01..REV1-12; revision 3 (PR #43, `main` @ `789c8a2`) closed Codex REV2-01..REV2-11 — durable slot reservations, pre-mutation operation identity with idempotent calendar inserts, ownership generations for takeover, a single cancel-vs-reschedule policy, claim generations on the delivery ledger, `expectedRevision` on notify, a durable create idempotency key, the booking id/token split, the `/b/{token}` controls, and the legacy one-off link entry point; revision 4 (PR #44, `main` @ `c4f14a4`) closed Codex REV3-01..REV3-10 — calendar repair as an explicit owned operation with a complete `calendar_state` transition table, `latest_action='confirm'` on the calendar-failed create path, key retention on non-terminal create responses, an immutable create fingerprint, reload recovery of an unresolved submission, a retry-notification control on the cancelled panel, a reschedule slot increment that actually yields overlapping moves, consumed-link replay, and conservative treatment of managed calendar events the store cannot account for. Revision 5 (PR #45, `main` @ `d3d8ee1`) closed the five Codex REV4 findings (REV4-01..REV4-05) raised against revision 4: the rev-4 **event-id fence is withdrawn** — Google documents neither lifetime retention of a cancelled event's id nor guaranteed id-collision detection at creation, so exclusion by placeholder was never a guarantee — and is replaced by **intended-id retention and reap** (an id whose insert may still execute is kept on the row; anything that lands under it is counted as busy and deleted by the next call that observes it), **ETag-conditioned mutations** of existing events with **version invalidation on takeover** (a paused patch, delete, or compensation can no longer land after a takeover), a durable per-operation **attempt ledger** (a later definite 4xx never clears an earlier ambiguous attempt; cancel resolves outstanding inserts even on a calendar-failed row), notify **takeover of a stale calendar repair**, and delivery status computed against the **required recipient set** (missing ledger rows are `pending` and are claimed by notify). Revision 6 (PR #46, `main` @ `baeb6d0`) closed the six Codex REV5 findings (REV5-01..REV5-06) raised against revision 5: **observed event existence is separated from completion of individual insert requests** — an `events.get` completes an op but never resolves an attempt, every unresolved insert attempt is retained on the row across a *successful* T2 and across cancel (including attempts on the live id), and one deletion retires one attempt, not the id (REV5-01); ids are in exactly one of two states, **live or retired**, retired is absorbing (no id is ever promoted back to live), and only retired ids are reap-eligible, so no reaper — sequential or paused — can delete a repaired event (REV5-02); the product guarantee is **narrowed honestly**: a late-landed insert is never offered as free and never persists, but interval occupancy is not held while an insert may still execute, so a transient overlap on the host's Google Calendar is possible and documented (REV5-03); **cleanup is distinguished from repair** — notify on a cancelled row may reap retired ids but never inserts or patches (REV5-04); the absolute "stale content is never sent after a cancel" claim is **withdrawn** — an already-claimed send may reach Gmail after a later revision's emails, the ledger stays generation-correct, and README says so (REV5-05); and **application-owned root slugs are reserved** during owner creation and public-path construction with an explicit sign-in error (REV5-06). Revision 7 (PR #47, `main` @ `9476bcd`) closed the five Codex REV6 findings (REV6-01..REV6-05) raised against revision 6: **every insert is attributed** — the insert body carries `marloAttemptId`, a reaped event retires exactly the attempt that created it, and retirement is idempotent by `attemptId` and shared by reapers and issuing workers, so neither a delayed insert response nor two concurrent reapers can retire an attempt that may still execute (REV6-01); the reap **traverses retired ids fairly** (least-recently-inspected first, `inspectedAt` written under the lock before any Google call) and a cancel's T2 precondition covers only the taken-over op's intended ids — the retired-id reap is bounded best-effort cleanup, never a precondition, so a row with more than five retained ids is cancellable in one attempt (REV6-02); Google **reads are separated from mutations** — an external-availability read (`events.list`) precedes create/reschedule T1 outside any transaction, the locked T1 re-checks the idempotency key and managed occupancy, an externally busy slot is refused without a row, and AC-11 forbids calendar *mutations*, not reads, before T1 commits (REV6-03); the duplicate-email bound is **stated honestly** — claim generations bound claim *acquisition* (one per row per stale window, only on a caller's retry) and `attempts` bounds the number of copies, but delivery timing is not bounded and stalled generations can deliver clustered duplicates; the rev-2 "at most one duplicate per stale window" is withdrawn and no issuance mechanism is pretended (REV6-04); and README no longer says the most recent email describes the current state — arrival order establishes nothing, every email links to the booking page, and the booking page is the authoritative status (REV6-05). Revision 8 (PR #48, `main` @ `71e0e6d`) closed the four Codex REV7 findings (REV7-01..REV7-04) raised against revision 7: a superseded worker's **response reconciliation is separated from its cleanup eligibility** — one locked read decides both, and the worker deletes its own attributed event only when that read shows the id **retired and its attempt retained**; an id that is live (the winner adopted it, and may since have moved it) or still intended by an open op is never deleted by a superseded worker, whatever the row's interval says, and the observed-etag retry after 412 lives only inside that eligibility, so the delayed-insert-response → adoption → subsequent-move interleaving can no longer delete the winner's live event (REV7-01); the create path performs a **locked idempotency lookup L0 before the R0 external read** — an existing key validates its fingerprint and returns or resumes with zero `events.list` calls, so a completed or non-stale pending create replays during a Calendar outage, and only a missing key performs R0 followed by T1's mandatory second lookup (REV7-02); a reschedule that takes over and **completes** a stale reschedule distinguishes **same-target recovery** (the completed move *is* the request → 200 with that outcome, no second operation) from a different requested move (→ 409 `booking_changed`, the reconciliation stands) and from an abandoned op (→ proceed as a new op), so a retry after death between the patch and T2 is never answered `slot_unavailable` (REV7-03); and the delivery-claim bound is **stated precisely**: the 2-minute window governs takeover of a `claimed` row (send in progress or outcome unresolved), a `failed` row (Gmail definitely refused; ambiguous outcomes leave the row `claimed`) is re-claimable immediately, `attempts` remains the exact bound on copies because a failed generation delivered nothing, no rate limit on failed retries is invented, and README, both stores, and the ACs say the same thing (REV7-04). Revision 9 (PR #49, `main` @ `d40cd24`) closed the three Codex REV8 findings (REV8-01..REV8-03) raised against revision 8: a terminal create rejection is a **durable fence** — `create_rejections (owner_id, idempotency_key, create_fingerprint, reason)` is written in the same locked T1 transaction that rejects with 409 `slot_unavailable`/`session_full`, L0 and T1's mandatory second lookup both check it, and an earlier same-key request paused between R0 and T1 can never insert a row after the client has cleared the key and booked elsewhere; the client drops responses for a key that is no longer its unresolved record (REV8-01); a superseded worker whose target the winner reached answers with the **calling route's success status** — 201 with the C3 envelope for create and create replay, 200 for reschedule/cancel/notify — so a delayed create insert response reaches the shipped client as the 201 it requires (REV8-02); and the token page's `pending` state has a defined exit when the single automatic retry is also answered `operation_in_progress`: `stalled`, with explicit **Try again** (same request, same `expectedRevision`, reconciled by the existing `conflict` handling) and **Reload** actions, so the page never remains disabled with nothing in flight (REV8-03). Revision 10 (PR #50, `main` @ `c42effb`) closed the three Codex REV9 findings (REV9-01..REV9-03) raised against revision 9: a superseded **create** worker reconciles success from the row's **immutable create identity** — `(owner_id, idempotency_key, create_fingerprint)` plus the app-generated `id`, all written once in T1 — and from **completed business creation** (`pending_op` no longer names the create's `opId`), never from `latest_action`, so a delayed create insert response answers **201 with the row's current state** even after the takeover's booking was rescheduled or cancelled in the meantime (exactly what a C9 replay returns), while cleanup eligibility stays the separate C6.3a(2) decision; a takeover still running answers the non-terminal `operation_in_progress` (REV9-01); the **collective boundary is defined instead of implied** — in memory mode a collective booking takes the same ordered per-host mutexes as single-host ops for **every** assigned host and its occupancy is counted for every assigned host by the one `hostOccupancy` implementation, so a collective booking and a single-host booking for a shared host serialize and conflict; in pg/live mode every collective operation is refused 501 `collective_not_supported` before any side effect, because the durable schema has no host-assignment set and a half-persisted collective would lose its secondary hosts' occupancy after a fresh-isolate read (C6.9, AC-24; durable collectives remain a Non-goal, REV9-02); and `/api/health` gets an **executable contract** (C13): in pg mode exactly one bounded `schema_migrations` readiness query (2 s deadline) decides `schema` — `current` / `behind` / `missing` / `unknown` — on every request including the first request of a fresh isolate, with no cached observation, no Google/Gmail call, and `ok`/HTTP 503 derived from it; memory mode performs zero I/O; offline proof runs against the pg fake (REV9-03). **This revision (rev 11) is a Product narrowing, not a Codex round.** Founder (Yalla) and VP Product lock the product bar (`## Product bar lock (rev 11)`): **group (capacity-N) sessions and collective bookings are dropped from LIVE-INT** — the live path is single-host, one attendee per slot; in pg/live mode a `group` or `collective` event type is never seeded or materialized and, if resolved anyway, is refused 501 `group_not_supported` / `collective_not_supported` **after** the read-only catalog resolution and **before** any booking side effect (C6.9, AC-24); in memory mode both kinds remain the existing fixtures under their existing tests, outside the C6 contract and never under live Calendar or email — REV10-01 and REV10-03 are closed by that scope-out, not by new mechanism. The already-narrowed concurrency and email guarantees are **frozen as the product bar**: transient calendar overlap after a late insert (REV5-03), an already-claimed or stale email after a cancel (REV5-05, REV6-05), and **Calendar HTTP 429 as an ambiguous outcome everywhere** — one shared classification (C6.0) used by C6.2–C6.8 and both adapters, the attempt and `pending_op` retained through takeover and cancel (AC-25; REV10-02 closed by unification) — and **no deeper concurrency guarantee is added without a Founder reopen**. **Revision 12 (PR against `main` @ `22ef8e9`) closes Codex REV11-01..02 by contract alignment only — no new mechanism, no new guarantee, Product bar untouched:** AC-11(n) and its ordering variants now assert what C6.3a(1), C9, and AC-11(r-iii) already required — a delayed create-replay worker whose booking was completed and then cancelled by a takeover answers **201** with the current C3 envelope of the cancelled booking (its immutable create identity matches and its create op is cleared), never 409 `operation_superseded`, while every attribution / retained-attempt / zero-delete assertion of (n) is kept (REV11-01); and the C9/C6.4 **L0 replay of a completed create is exempt from the mandatory C6.3a reap**, so it stays a **zero-Google-call** path even when the row is cancelled and `unresolved_inserts` retains reap-eligible ids — those ids are reaped by the designated observing calls (reschedule, cancel, notify, `GET /api/bookings/{id}`, `/b/{token}`, and C7's `events.list` classification), never by the replay (REV11-02). **Revision 13 (PR against `main` @ `b62fbac`) closes Codex REV12-01 by contract alignment only — no new mechanism, no new guarantee, Product bar untouched:** the AC-12(m) **same-target variant** now asserts what C6.3, C6.3a(1), and AC-18(iii) already required — when B completes A's move to T and the booking sits `confirmed` at A's requested `start = T` with `revision` 2, A's delayed response reconciles as **200** with the current booking and the `delivery` envelope of the completing claim, never 409 `operation_superseded`; every zero-delete / zero-mutation / F-live assertion of the variant is kept, and `operation_superseded` remains the answer only for the main variant, where the booking was subsequently moved to U (REV12-01). **Revision 14 (PR against `main` @ `de6955f`) closes Codex REV13-01..02 by contract alignment only — no new product scope, no new guarantee, Product bar untouched:** the C6.3a retained-id traversal is ordered by a **monotonic per-row cursor** — `bookings.reap_cursor`, stamped as each entry's `inspectSeq` under the same host lock that selects the batch — instead of by `inspectedAt = now()`, so the reap advances strictly however the clock behaves (frozen fake clock, coarse granularity, backwards step) and `inspectedAt` survives as a diagnostic that orders nothing; AC-11(p) now asserts the coverage guarantee that actually holds — the **union of any two consecutive observing calls** inspects every eligible id while `|eligible| ≤ 2 × REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST` — disjointness of two concurrent batches is claimed only at `|eligible| ≥ 10` (two five-id batches drawn from six ids cannot be disjoint; the overlap is harmless because `retireAttempt` is idempotent by `attemptId`), and a new fixed-clock case (p-ii) runs six absent ids through six calls and then lands F6 late (REV13-01); and the **pending-create read** is defined instead of left unexpressible — `delivery.calendar` gains `pending` so the C3 envelope mirrors *every* state of `calendar_state`, including the one create T1 commits, it is returned only by the read surfaces (`GET /api/bookings/{id}`, `/b/{token}`) and never by a create 201, and it renders as a status line with no control, no new `controlsState`, and no polling; AC-23(a) proves that row of the C6.8 table like the others (REV13-02). **Revision 15 (PR against `main` @ `4dbf4bf`) closes Codex REV14-01 by contract alignment only — no new product scope, no new guarantee, Product bar untouched:** the shared create-success predicate now distinguishes a create that was **finalized** by someone else from one that a C6.7 cancel merely **replaced** while taking over a stale create whose event was absent — C6.3a(1)(ii) and the C6.4 L0 / C9 replay both test the durable, monotonic **nullness** of `latest_action` (C6.4a; never its value, so REV9-01's objection to `latest_action='confirm'` and the (r-ii)/(r-iii) 201s stand), and the replaced-but-unfinished case is answered the **non-terminal** 409 `operation_in_progress` instead of a 201 whose `delivery.calendar` could only read `'pending'` (forbidden by C3) over a `(revision, NULL)` pair C5 cannot claim; the inherited create is **carried durably** on the row (`bookings.unfinished_create`, written by exactly the one cancel T1 that replaces a create without completing it, cleared by whichever transaction finalizes it — including cancel T2, so the REV9-01/REV11-01 cancelled-booking replay is unchanged); **cancel T2′ recovery** restores that create as an immediately-takeable `pending_op` (original `startedAt`, bumped `gen`) instead of clearing the row's last-operation state, so the row can never be parked `confirmed`/`pending`/`latest_action=NULL`/`pending_op=NULL` — unreadable as a 201, unclaimable by notify, and unreachable by C6.8 repair; three invariants (CF-1 `calendar_state='pending' ⇔ latest_action IS NULL`, CF-2 `pending_op IS NULL ⇒ latest_action IS NOT NULL`, CF-3) are stated and asserted, C5 states the refusal for a row with no notifiable pair, the C6.8 table gains its **cancel T2′** row, and AC-11 gains **(r-v)** (cancel takeover of a stale create with the event absent, the replay and the delayed create response both answered 409 `operation_in_progress`, then the cancelled-booking 201) and **(r-vi)** (cancel T2′ restoring a resumable create, the replay finalizing it, notify then delivering) (REV14-01). **Revision 16 (PR against `main` @ `ac4f42e`) closes Codex REV15-01..03 by contract alignment only — no new product scope, no new mechanism, no new guarantee, Product bar untouched:** the notify route gets **one normative ordering** and the two things it does are finally named apart — **N-1 lifecycle recovery** (C6.4 `resumeCreate`, the stale-`calendar_repair` takeover of REV4-04), then **N-2 repair/reap**, then **N-3 the notification claim** — so a *claim* is never a *recovery*: rev 15 stated the NULL-pair `stale_revision` refusal without saying it runs after recovery, which left the restored create of C6.7's cancel T2′ simultaneously owed a refusal (C5) and a takeover (C6.8, Approach §5, AC-11(g)); the refusal now applies **only** to a claim that reaches N-3 still carrying `latest_action IS NULL`, which CF-2 plus N-1 make unreachable on any committed row — a **defensive invariant check** logged `notify_no_pair` and asserted suite-wide never to fire — while an unfinished create gets N-1's answer instead (409 `operation_in_progress` under a live owner, or the finalized booking's emails once notify itself takes the stale create over and completes it); AC-11(r-vi) is split into **(r-vi-a)** the create replay arriving first (the rev-15 sequence, unchanged) and **(r-vi-b)** a notify arriving first, which now recovers rather than refuses — one `events.insert` under the same id, create T2 issuing the `(1, 'confirm')` claim, N-3 finding both rows already `sent` and adding no copies, 200 — with the cancel-in-progress window still answered 409 `operation_in_progress` (REV15-01). The rev-15 finalization predicate becomes the **single shared implementation** `createFinalized(row, op)` behind C6.3a(1)(ii), the C6.4 **L0** lookup, and the C9 replay — immutable identity **and** `latest_action IS NOT NULL` **and** the create op cleared — replacing Approach §4's stale `createIdentityMatches` + “never `latest_action`” wording and C9's remaining success text, both of which still prescribed the rev-9 predicate that rev 15 had already replaced and would have answered AC-11(r-v) with a 201 whose `delivery.calendar` could only read `'pending'`; the rev-9 prohibition is **narrowed to what it always meant** — no predicate may compare `latest_action` against a **particular value** (`= 'confirm'`, which a later reschedule or cancel legitimately falsifies), while testing its **monotonic nullness** is required — and C9's replay is re-expressed in three ordered branches (open create op → resume; finalized → 201; unfinished under a non-create op → non-terminal 409 `operation_in_progress`), with CF-2 excluding the fourth (REV15-02). And the C2 `Idempotency-Key` requirement is **scoped to the live product's event kind**: the header is checked **after** catalog resolution and **after** the C6.9 kind gate on every create entry point, so a resolved `group`/`collective` kind never reaches it — 501 in pg/live mode, and in memory mode the existing fixture path with no key, no fingerprint, and no C9 record, exactly the preserved fixture semantics C6.9 promises — which is what keeps `tests/group-route.test.ts` and `tests/collective-route.test.ts` (whose helpers send only `content-type`) passing **byte-for-byte unmodified**, proved on both sides by AC-24(f); a `one_on_one` create with no header is still 400 `idempotency_key_required` (REV15-03). It keeps P1–P3 and every LIVE-xx/REV1-xx..REV9-xx closure that the Product bar does not supersede (only REV9-02's memory-mode collective boundary is superseded, by the scope-out), and supersedes the merged PLAN in full. Everything live is still behind env flags; `npm test` never leaves the machine once dependencies are installed (see AC-6 / REV1-12 for the exact offline contract).

# PLAN — LIVE-INT (rev 16, Product-narrowed): durable bookings, live Google Calendar, real emails, per-owner URLs, token reschedule

Spec owner: Shahar + VP of Product · Product review: Shahar + Grok · Plan review: Codex (LIVE-01..10 in rev 1; REV1-01..12 in rev 2; REV2-01..11 in rev 3; REV3-01..10 in rev 4; REV4-01..05 in rev 5; REV5-01..06 in rev 6; REV6-01..05 in rev 7; REV7-01..04 in rev 8; REV8-01..03 in rev 9; REV9-01..03 in rev 10; REV10-01..03 closed by the Product bar in rev 11; REV11-01..02 in rev 12, REV12-01 in rev 13, REV13-01..02 in rev 14, REV14-01 in rev 15, and REV15-01..03 in rev 16 by contract alignment, dispositions below) · Status: REVISED / PRODUCT-NARROWED — Founder GO 21 Sep 2026 ET; product locks P1–P3 verified by Founder and unchanged in rev 11 through rev 16; Product bar locked by Founder (Yalla) + VP Product 21 Sep 2026 ET and **unchanged by rev 16**

BUILDER: claude

## Goal

When a guest books a host on production, the booking is real end to end and survives the guest changing their mind:

1. **Per-owner public URL (P1).** The booking page lives at `/{ownerSlug}/{eventSlug}` and every piece of page chrome and copy that names the host uses that host's `firstName` (title, hero, confirmation, email subject/body). `/demo/intro-30` remains only as the fixture-backed demo route; it is not the product URL. Event types are **owner-scoped**: two owners may both offer `intro-30`, and owner identity travels through the page, the form store, the API client, the availability route, the booking route, and the store (REV1-01).
2. **Durable booking row.** The booking persists in Neon (Postgres) across serverless isolates, and so do the owner, event-type, and schedule records it references, under **stable identifiers** — a fresh isolate can read a booking and resolve its event type and schedule (REV1-02). A booking has two distinct identifiers: an opaque `id` (the API path segment) and a separate secret `token` (the `/b/{token}` capability and the bearer credential for every `/api/bookings/{id}/*` call); the two are never equal and the id alone grants nothing (REV2-08). `GET /api/bookings/{id}` and `/b/{token}` return the same confirmed row regardless of which isolate serves the request. A create request carries a client-retained **idempotency key**, so a retry after a lost response returns the original booking and token instead of a second row (REV2-09).
3. **Live Google Calendar + real emails on book (P3).** Booking creates a real event on the host's connected primary calendar (`events.insert` with the host's stored OAuth refresh token) and sends a real confirmation email through the Gmail API to **both** the invitee and the owner, with a minimal `.ics`.
4. **Guest reschedule / cancel via `/b/{token}` (P2, P3 parity).** The guest's change path is the token page, not "reopen the event link and book again": the page carries explicit **Reschedule** and **Cancel** controls, its own authenticated availability that excludes the booking's current occupancy, and pending / conflict / error states wired to the real handlers (REV2-10). Reschedule moves the live calendar event (patch; or insert when the event is gone) and sends reschedule emails to invitee and owner. Cancel removes the live event and sends cancel emails. Calendar occupancy mutations happen **regardless** of the event type's legacy `notificationMode` (`calendar_invitation | email_confirmation`), which now only selects whether Google also sends its own attendee invitations (REV1-05).
5. **Honest failure semantics.** A booking whose DB write succeeded but whose email failed is still a booking: the API returns HTTP 201 with the booking token and `delivery.email: "failed" | "pending"`, and an idempotent, revision-aware retry route re-attempts delivery (REV1-07). Calendar failures and Gmail failures are distinguished in the response and in logs. Every lifecycle operation (create, reschedule, cancel) has a defined transaction order, durable operation state, compensation, and retry response (REV1-09), and never destroys a calendar event on the strength of an *unknown* commit outcome (REV1-10). Rev 3 makes that operation state complete: the operation's identity, its **ownership generation**, and the **intended calendar event id** are persisted before any Google mutation, so an accepted-but-lost insert or a process death is reconciled by the next call rather than duplicated or orphaned (REV2-03), a superseded worker reconciles the winner instead of undoing it (REV2-04), an unknown commit is only ever interpreted after the original transaction is known to have finished (REV2-02), and every conflict query sees pending reservations (REV2-01). Rev 5 keeps only the guarantees Google's contract actually establishes: every Google mutation is recorded as a durable **attempt** before it is issued and stays *unresolved* until this app observes its outcome (REV4-03); an intended id whose insert may still execute is **retained** on the row, and any event that lands under it is counted as busy and deleted by the next call that observes it rather than assumed excluded (REV4-01) — retention is per *insert attempt*, survives a successful completion, and is never cleared by observing or deleting an event, because neither proves which request executed (REV5-01) — but every insert carries its own `attemptId` into the event it creates, so a deletion retires exactly the attempt that provably executed and never one that may still (REV6-01); the guarantee this buys is stated honestly: never offered as free, never a booking, never persistent — but interval occupancy is not held for an insert that may still execute, so a late landing can transiently overlap a later booking on the host's Google Calendar until it is reaped (REV5-03); patches, deletes, and compensations of an existing event are **ETag-conditioned** and a takeover invalidates the predecessor's version before it proceeds, so a paused mutation cannot land after a takeover (REV4-02).

All live implementations sit behind interfaces whose default implementations are the existing in-memory / fixture / mock ones, so `PROOF_CMD` (`npm test`) stays green offline and never needs `DATABASE_URL`, Google, Gmail, or the network. Live code paths activate only when `DATABASE_URL`, `LIVE_CALENDAR=1`, and `LIVE_EMAIL=1` are set in the host environment **and** the database schema is current (AC-17).

## Product bar lock (rev 11) — Founder (Yalla) + VP Product, binding

This section is the scope fence for LIVE-INT. It overrides any contract, AC, or disposition below that reads wider, and no inspector finding reopens it: a finding that would widen it is dispositioned `out-of-scope` by the judge and logged for a later PLAN (Round budget).

1. **Dropped from LIVE-INT: group (capacity-N) sessions and collective bookings.** The live path — durable Neon store, live Google Calendar, real email — serves **single-host, one-attendee-per-slot** (`kind='one_on_one'`) event types only. In pg/live mode (`DATABASE_URL` set) a `group` or `collective` event type is never seeded or materialized (C1, C8) and, should one be resolved anyway, is refused **501 `group_not_supported` / `collective_not_supported`** after the read-only catalog resolution and before any booking side effect (C6.9, AC-24). In memory mode both kinds remain what they are today — fixtures under `tests/group*.test.ts` and `tests/collective*.test.ts` with their existing code paths — **outside** the C6 lifecycle contract and never under live Calendar or email (memory mode has no live adapters, AC-6). No capacity-sharing occupancy rule, group reschedule-grid rule, host-assignment schema, or multi-host lock order is specified or proved for the live path.
2. **Frozen guarantees (the product bar).** These are the already-narrowed statements of rev 5–rev 10, now fixed as the bar for this slice:
   - **Transient calendar overlap is accepted:** a late-landing insert may briefly overlap a later booking on the host's Google Calendar; it is reaped by the next observing call, is never offered as free, never becomes a booking, and occupancy is not held for it (C6.3a, C7, REV5-03).
   - **A stale email after a cancel is accepted:** an already-claimed send may reach Gmail after a later revision's emails; the ledger stays generation-correct and the booking page is the authoritative status (C5, REV5-05, REV6-05).
   - **Calendar HTTP 429 is ambiguous, everywhere:** never a definite rejection; the attempt stays `unresolved`, `pending_op` is retained, and the outcome is decided by `events.get` on the next attempt — through takeover and through cancel (C6.0, AC-25, REV10-02).
   - **No deeper concurrency rounds.** No new concurrency, ordering, or exactly-once guarantee is added to this PLAN beyond the frozen bar unless the Founder reopens it. Review of later revisions is confined to the executability of what is written here.
3. **In scope, unchanged (P1–P3):** single-host booking at `/{ownerSlug}/{eventSlug}` with host-named chrome (`/demo/intro-30` fixture-only); guest change via `/b/{token}` reschedule/cancel with live calendar + email parity; durable Neon store, live Google Calendar create/update/cancel, real invitee + owner email — all behind env; the proof is `npm test`, offline on mocks; the builder seat is `claude`.

## Non-goals

- Chrome / Gmail extension
- Host dashboard (listing/managing bookings as the host) — hosts see bookings on their Google Calendar and in their inbox; that is the dashboard for this slice
- Cursor cloud / any non-Vercel deploy target
- MJML / HTML email polish beyond the minimum plain-text body + `.ics` needed to send a real confirmation / reschedule / cancel
- Multi-calendar selection UI, calendar-picker, or writing to any calendar other than the host's primary
- Host self-serve creation/editing of event types (event types remain fixture-defined per owner and are materialized for an owner at sign-in; slugs are data, not a CRUD surface)
- Payments, SMS, reminders, buffer rules, round-robin, and **group (capacity-N) sessions and collective bookings on the live path** — Product bar lock (rev 11): both kinds are dropped from LIVE-INT; in pg/live mode they are neither seeded nor materialized and are refused 501 `group_not_supported` / `collective_not_supported` after catalog resolution and before any booking side effect (C6.9); in memory mode they remain the existing fixtures under their existing tests, outside the C6 contract. No capacity-sharing occupancy rule, host-assignment table, migration, pg group/collective path, or memory-mode multi-host lock order is built or proved (the rev-10 memory-mode collective boundary of REV9-02 is withdrawn as scope, not as a bug)
- Any concurrency, ordering, or delivery guarantee beyond the frozen bar (Product bar lock §2) — holding occupancy for a possibly-executing insert, ordering email issuance against lifecycle changes, or treating Calendar 429 as anything but ambiguous; closed for this slice unless the Founder reopens them
- A background job runner / queue for email retry or for lifecycle reconciliation — retry and reconciliation are idempotent HTTP routes the guest or owner can hit from the confirmation page; no cron, no queue
- Exactly-once email delivery — at-least-once with a documented, bounded duplicate window (AC-13 / REV1-07)
- Durable (Postgres) single-use links or one-off meetings — the legacy `/api/links/{token}/*` entry point stays fixture-only and is refused in pg mode (C10 / REV2-11)
- A background reconciler for stuck `pending_op` rows — takeover happens on the next call that reads the row (C6.3); the reap of a late-landed event (C6.3a) likewise runs only inside calls that already observe the event or read the row, bounded per request and traversed fairly so no retained id is starved (REV6-02)
- Holding a slot's occupancy for as long as an insert *might* still execute at Google — Google bounds nothing about delayed execution, so such a hold would be unbounded; a late landing is reaped, not prevented (C6.3a, REV5-03)
- Serializing email issuance with lifecycle changes (a durable send queue, or a lock held across Gmail) — an already-claimed send may arrive after a later revision's emails (C5, REV5-05)
- A wall-clock bound on *when* a claimed email is delivered — claim generations bound how often a claim can be re-acquired and `attempts` bounds how many copies can ever exist, but a worker that stalled after claiming delivers its copy whenever it resumes, so delayed copies can cluster (C5, REV6-04)
- A rate limit on retrying a **definitely failed** send — a `failed` ledger row is re-claimable immediately by the next caller; the 2-minute stale window governs only the takeover of a `claimed` row whose outcome is unresolved (C5, REV7-04)
- A health endpoint that verifies Google or Gmail connectivity — `/api/health` reports `calendar`/`email` as *configuration*, and its only I/O is the bounded `schema_migrations` readiness query of C13 (REV9-03)
- Changing the proof harness shape: `PROOF_CMD` remains `npm test` (typecheck + `next build` + unit tests via `scripts/proof.cjs`); the builder never edits `.github/`

## Interface contracts (normative; ACs reference these)

### C1. Owner-scoped identity (REV1-01, REV1-02)

- Every event type belongs to exactly one owner. Uniqueness in `lib/availability/event-type.ts` becomes `(ownerId, slug)`, not global `slug`. `createEventType` takes `ownerId`; existing fixture/test callers default to the demo owner.
- Stable identifiers are deterministic strings derived from slugs, identical in memory and pg mode: owner `own_{ownerSlug}`, event type `evt_{ownerSlug}__{eventSlug}`, schedule `sch_{ownerSlug}__{scheduleKey}`. Re-seeding fixtures in a fresh isolate reproduces the same ids, so a booking's persisted `eventTypeId`/`scheduleId` always resolves. Sign-in materialization and the migration seed write **only `kind='one_on_one'` event types** (Product bar lock; C6.9).
- Owner records are created/updated on host sign-in: the OAuth callback upserts `owners` (slug = validated local-part of the Google email **and not a reserved root slug** — `RESERVED_ROOT_SLUGS` in `lib/owners.ts` = `api`, `b`, `host`, `signin`, `tokens`, `components`, the app's own root path segments, kept complete by the filesystem test of AC-2; a reserved local-part fails the sign-in with `owner_slug_reserved` before any `owners`/`host_tokens` write, REV5-06 — `firstName` = profile `given_name`, `email`, `calendarId = 'primary'`) and materializes that owner's fixture-defined event types and schedule with the ids above. The `demo` owner is seeded by migration and by `ensureDemoFixtures()`.

### C2. HTTP routes (REV1-01, REV1-06, REV15-03)

| Purpose | Route | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Booking page | `GET /{ownerSlug}/{eventSlug}` | `app/(public)/[slug]/[event]` where `[slug]` **is** the owner slug; a reserved `[slug]` (C1) is 404 before any store read; `/demo/intro-30` pins `owner=demo, fixture=true` |
| Availability | `GET /api/owners/{ownerSlug}/event-types/{eventSlug}/available-times?…` | owner-scoped; 404 `owner_not_found` / `event_type_not_found`; in pg/live mode a resolved `group`/`collective` kind → 501 `group_not_supported` / `collective_not_supported` after the catalog read, before any lock, store write, or Google call (C6.9) |
| Book | `POST /api/owners/{ownerSlug}/event-types/{eventSlug}/bookings` | body unchanged: `{ start, invitee: { name, email }, notes? }`; **required** header `Idempotency-Key: <uuid>` per C9 (missing/malformed → 400 `idempotency_key_required`). **Where the check runs (REV15-03):** the header requirement is evaluated **after** the read-only catalog resolution and **after** the C6.9 kind gate, never before — so a resolved `group`/`collective` kind is answered by C6.9 (501 in pg/live mode; the existing memory-mode fixture path in memory mode) without the header ever being consulted |
| Legacy | `GET /api/event-types/{slug}/available-times`, `POST /api/event-types/{slug}/bookings` | resolve `slug` **only** within the `demo` owner; any slug not owned by `demo` → 404 `owner_required`. Kept for fixtures/tests; not linked from any page. `POST` requires the same `Idempotency-Key` header **for `one_on_one` event types only**, checked in the same position (after catalog resolution, after the C6.9 kind gate). **A resolved `group`/`collective` kind is exempt (REV15-03):** those rows are memory-mode fixtures explicitly outside the C6/C9 contract (C6.9), they book through their existing fixture path with no key, no `create_fingerprint`, and no C9 record, and the exemption is what keeps `tests/group-route.test.ts` and `tests/collective-route.test.ts` — whose request helpers send only `content-type` — green **without modification** (AC-24(a)(f)) |
| Legacy one-off link | `GET /api/links/{token}/available-times`, `POST /api/links/{token}/bookings` | existing single-use-link entry point (`lib/availability/single-use-link.ts`, `bookSingleUseLink`). **Memory mode:** kept for fixtures/tests but routed through the shared C6 create path (host lock, reservation-aware conflict check, two-phase create, C9 key = the link token); a `POST` on a **consumed** link with the identical payload replays the original booking (201) through the C9 lookup, a different payload is 410 (C10, REV3-09). **Pg/live mode (`DATABASE_URL` set):** links and one-off meetings are not durable records; both routes return 501 `links_not_supported` **before** any store, calendar, or email side effect (C10) |
| Read | `GET /api/bookings/{id}` (token-authenticated: header `Authorization: Bearer {token}`), `GET /b/{token}` | `{id}` is the booking id, never the token; `/b/{token}` looks the row up by the `token` column (C11). Missing bearer → 401 `token_required`; wrong token or unknown id → 404 `booking_not_found` |
| Booking availability | `GET /api/bookings/{id}/available-times?timeMin&timeMax` | token-authenticated (bearer); the reschedule picker's source: same occupancy computation as the owner-scoped availability route **minus this booking's own occupancy** (own interval, own reservation, own managed event), but candidate starts are generated at the **reschedule slot increment** (C12, REV3-08: `RESCHEDULE_SLOT_INCREMENT_MIN = 15`, independent of `durationMinutes`), so the overlapping moves of AC-8(d) are actually generated, not merely un-blocked |
| Reschedule / cancel | `POST /api/bookings/{id}/reschedule`, `POST /api/bookings/{id}/cancel` | token-authenticated (bearer); body `{ start, expectedRevision }` / `{ expectedRevision }`; `expectedRevision` is the revision the client last saw, mismatch → 409 `booking_changed` |
| Notify retry | `POST /api/bookings/{id}/notify` | token-authenticated (bearer); body `{ action?: "confirm" \| "reschedule" \| "cancel", expectedRevision?: number }` — see C5 for the two request forms (revision-specific vs. explicit retry-latest). On a **confirmed** booking whose `calendar_state='failed'` it also begins the owned **calendar repair** operation (C6.8) — or takes over and resumes a **stale** one (REV4-04) — before the email retry; on a **cancelled** booking it retries the cancel emails and runs only the C6.3a **reap** of that row's retired ids (a bounded, fair batch: get, and `If-Match` delete of anything present, retiring the attributed attempt — cleanup, never insertion or repair; REV3-02, REV5-04, REV6-01/02) |
| Health | `GET /api/health` | C13 / AC-15: one bounded `schema_migrations` readiness query in pg mode, zero I/O in memory mode, never Google/Gmail; HTTP 503 when `ok` is false (REV9-03) |

### C3. Booking response envelope (REV1-06)

The shipped client (`lib/api/client.ts`) expects `{ booking: { … start … } }` on 201 and recognises 409 codes `slot_unavailable` and `session_full`. Rev 2 keeps that envelope and **adds** fields; it does not introduce `slot_taken`/`slot_full` (those names from rev 1 are withdrawn):

```json
201 {
  "booking": { "id", "token", "ownerSlug", "eventSlug", "start", "end", "status", "revision", "hostFirstName", … },
  "delivery": {
    "email":    "sent" | "failed" | "pending",   // over the required recipient set {invitee, owner} for the current (revision, latest_action); a missing ledger row is "pending" (C5, REV4-05)
    "calendar": "created" | "failed" | "skipped" | "deleted" | "pending",   // mirrors bookings.calendar_state (C6.8) — every state of that column, "pending" included; "deleted" only on a cancelled booking; "pending" only on a read envelope (REV13-02)
    "errors":   { "email"?: "gmail_send_failed" | "host_not_connected", "calendar"?: "calendar_insert_failed" | "host_not_connected" }
  }
}
409 { "error": "slot_unavailable" }   // overlap for this host (AC-4); on create, terminal AND durably fenced for (key, fingerprint) in the rejecting T1 (C6.4, REV8-01)
409 { "error": "session_full" }       // memory-mode group fixture only (existing wire code kept for the shipped client); unreachable in pg/live mode, where kind='group' is refused 501 (C6.9); where produced, fenced exactly like slot_unavailable (REV8-01)
409 { "error": "booking_changed" }    // versioned update lost / expectedRevision mismatch (AC-18)
409 { "error": "operation_in_progress", "retryAfterSeconds": n }  // another op owns pending_op (C6); also a same-key create replay while the original create is still active (C9) — NOT terminal for the client's key
409 { "error": "operation_superseded" } // this worker's op was taken over; winner's state reported in `booking` (C6)
409 { "error": "stale_revision" }     // notify for an older (revision, action) (C5)
400 { "error": "idempotency_key_required" }  // C9
422 { "error": "idempotency_key_reused" }    // C9: same key, payload differs from the immutable create fingerprint
410 { "error": "<SingleUseLinkConsumedError message>" }  // C10: consumed link with a *different* payload (existing wire status; identical payload replays 201)
501 { "error": "links_not_supported" }       // C10: one-off link in pg mode
501 { "error": "collective_not_supported" }  // C6.9: collective event type in pg/live mode; after the read-only catalog resolution, before any booking side effect; non-terminal for the client's key (C9)
501 { "error": "group_not_supported" }       // C6.9: group (capacity-N) event type in pg/live mode; same rule (Product bar lock, rev 11)
503 { "error": "availability_unknown" }        // AC-7
503 { "error": "booking_outcome_unknown", "retryAfterSeconds": n }  // AC-11
```

`booking.id` and `booking.token` are **both** present and always unequal (C11). A create replayed with the same `Idempotency-Key` returns the **same** `booking` (same `id`, same `token`) with the current `delivery` and status 201 (C9). `PublicBooking` in `lib/api/types.ts` gains `id`, `ownerSlug`, `eventSlug`, `revision`, `delivery`; `mapBooking` maps `token` from `booking.token` (no longer from `booking.id`) and returns `null` when either `id` or `token` is missing. The client's `createBooking` posts to the owner-scoped route with the form's idempotency key and maps `delivery` through; the form's success path navigates on any 201 regardless of `delivery.email`.

**Pending-create reads (REV13-02).** `calendar_state='pending'` is a real persisted state — create T1 commits it before the insert is resolved (C6.8) — so the envelope must be able to express it, and `delivery.calendar: 'pending'` means exactly *this booking's calendar event is still being created and its outcome is not yet known*. It is returned **only** by the read surfaces, `GET /api/bookings/{id}` (bearer) and `/b/{token}`, on a row whose create T1 committed and whose calendar outcome is still unresolved (the create op is open, or its worker died leaving `pending_op` retained). It never appears on a create **201**, which is issued only after create T2 (`created`) or T2′ (`failed`) has resolved the column, and never on a reschedule/cancel/repair 200, whose T2/T2′ resolve it too. Rendering is a **status line only — no new control, no new state, no polling**: `PublicBooking`/`mapBooking` carry the value through unchanged; `/b/{token}` renders "Calendar invite is still being created." beside the existing `delivery.email` line, keeps `controlsState = 'idle'` with Reschedule, Cancel, and Resend **enabled** (those routes already refuse or take over an open create op by the C6 rules — the page must not second-guess them), and offers **no** repair or retry-calendar control, because repair is reachable only from `calendar_state='failed'` (C6.8). A reload re-reads the row; nothing polls. `'pending'` is never an error: it never populates `delivery.errors`, which stays reserved for definite failures. **The read/201 split is a consequence, not a second rule (REV14-01):** `calendar_state='pending'` holds on exactly the rows whose creation has not been finalized (`latest_action IS NULL` — C6.4a CF-1), and a create **201** is issued only for a **finalized** creation (C6.3a(1)(ii)), so the two can never collide; a create worker whose `pending_op` was merely **replaced** by an in-progress cancel (C6.7) is therefore answered the non-terminal 409 `operation_in_progress`, never a 201 it could only fill with `calendar: 'pending'`.

### C4. Notification policy (REV1-05)

- **Recipients are a product constant, not a mode:** confirm, reschedule, and cancel each email **both** invitee and owner (P3). No `none | invitee | owner | both` mode exists or is introduced.
- **`notificationMode` (existing `calendar_invitation | email_confirmation`) selects only the Google attendee behaviour:** `calendar_invitation` → the calendar event lists the invitee as an attendee and `events.insert/patch/delete` are called with `sendUpdates=all` (Google sends its native invite/update/cancellation in addition to Marlo's emails); `email_confirmation` → no attendees, `sendUpdates=none`. Default for fixture event types is `email_confirmation`.
- **Calendar occupancy (insert on book, patch on reschedule, delete on cancel) is unconditional** in both modes and in both mock and live adapters.
- **Every email links to the booking page (REV6-05):** each of the six bodies (confirm / reschedule / cancel × invitee / owner) carries the absolute `/b/{token}` URL, built from the issuing request's origin (`bookingPageUrl(origin, token)`; no new env var — notify retries are requests too), followed by one sentence: *"The booking page always shows the current status of this booking."* The README's authoritative-status pointer (C5) must be reachable from the very email that prompted it. The owner's copy carries the same link (the host may reschedule or cancel through it; there is no host dashboard — Non-goals).
- `dispatchBookingNotification` and `tests/notification.test.ts` are updated to this policy; the mock email adapter records `{ action, revision, recipient }` per send.

### C5. Delivery ledger (REV1-07, REV7-04, REV14-01, REV15-01)

`notification_deliveries (booking_id, revision, action, recipient) PK` with `state ∈ {claimed, sent, failed}`, `claimed_at`, `attempts`. One row per (revision, action, recipient); `revision` is the booking row's monotonically increasing `revision` column. Initial delivery and retries share one **atomic claim**:

```sql
INSERT INTO notification_deliveries (booking_id, revision, action, recipient, state, claimed_at, attempts)
VALUES ($1,$2,$3,$4,'claimed',now(),1)
ON CONFLICT (booking_id, revision, action, recipient) DO UPDATE
  SET state='claimed', claimed_at=now(), attempts=notification_deliveries.attempts+1
  WHERE notification_deliveries.state='failed'
     OR (notification_deliveries.state='claimed' AND notification_deliveries.claimed_at < now() - interval '2 minutes')
RETURNING attempts;
```

Only a caller that gets a row back sends. **Two re-claim conditions, two different bounds (REV7-04):** the `state='failed'` branch has **no age condition** — a `failed` row is re-claimable **immediately** by the next caller — and only the `state='claimed'` branch carries the 2-minute stale window; both stores implement exactly this predicate and nothing stricter (no rate limit on failed retries is added — Non-goals). What makes the immediate re-claim honest is the **finalisation rule**: a worker finalises `failed` **only** on a **definite** Gmail refusal (a non-2xx response that proves the message was not accepted — 4xx other than 429; `host_not_connected`); an **ambiguous** send outcome (timeout, 5xx, 429, network error, process death) is **not** finalised — the row stays `claimed`, because Gmail may have accepted the message, and it becomes re-claimable only after the stale window. A `failed` generation therefore delivered **nothing**, so repeated immediate retries of a failed send raise `attempts` without raising the number of copies, and `attempts` remains the exact upper bound on copies (AC-13(l)). **The returned `attempts` value is the claim generation** (REV2-06): the sender keeps it and every finalisation carries it — `UPDATE … SET state=$s WHERE booking_id=$1 AND revision=$2 AND action=$3 AND recipient=$4 AND state='claimed' AND attempts=$gen`. A late finaliser whose claim was taken over after the 2-minute stale window (the row now carries `attempts = gen+1`) updates zero rows, logs `stale_finalize_ignored { bookingId, revision, action, recipient, gen }`, and never flips the new owner's row to `sent` or `failed`. `claim()` returns `{ gen }` and `finalize()` requires it in both stores; there is no finalisation path without a generation.

**Revision validation at claim time** (REV2-07): `bookings` carries `latest_action ∈ {confirm, reschedule, cancel}` written by the same T2 update that advances `revision` (C6). The notify route accepts two request forms and validates `(revision, action)` **together, under the booking's `FOR UPDATE` read inside the claim transaction**, never from a separate earlier read:

- **Revision-specific retry** — body `{ action, expectedRevision }` (the page/client always sends both, taken from the last `booking` it received): claims only if `bookings.revision = expectedRevision AND bookings.latest_action = action`; otherwise 409 `stale_revision` with the current `{ revision, latestAction }` in the body and nothing sent. `action` without `expectedRevision`, or `expectedRevision` without `action`, is 400 `notify_request_invalid`.
- **Explicit retry-latest** — body `{}` (neither field): the route resolves the booking's current `(revision, latest_action)` under the same lock and claims for exactly that pair; the response echoes which `{ revision, action }` it retried. This is the only form that can never be stale **once the booking's creation is finalized**; see the next bullet for the one state in which it, too, has no pair to claim. It is what the "Resend" control uses when the client has no revision in hand (e.g. a reload of `/b/{token}`).
- **The notify route's one ordering — lifecycle recovery *before* notification claiming (REV15-01; revises rev 15's unconditional NULL-pair refusal).** `POST /api/bookings/{id}/notify` is **two** things behind one route — a *lifecycle recovery* entry point (C6.4 `resumeCreate`, C6.8 repair takeover, the C6.3a reap) and a *notification claim* — and rev 15 stated the second's NULL-pair refusal without saying it runs after the first, which made the same row simultaneously owed a `stale_revision` refusal (this bullet) and a takeover (C6.8, Approach §5, AC-11(g)). **One ordering is normative.** Every phase runs under the host lock and re-reads the booking `FOR UPDATE` after the previous phase commits; a **notification claim is never a recovery, and a recovery never claims on notify's behalf**:

  - **N-1 — lifecycle recovery (the only phase that may take over another worker's op; never writes a `notification_deliveries` row).** From the `FOR UPDATE` read: `pending_op.kind='create'` → resume per C6.4 — **non-stale → 409 `operation_in_progress`** with `retryAfterSeconds`, route returns, nothing claimed and nothing sent; **stale → take over** (C6.3 `gen+1`, conditioned on `opId`/`gen`; zero rows → 409 `operation_in_progress`) **and complete it** (`events.get(eventId)` → present → create T2; definitely absent → one `events.insert` under the **same** intended id → create T2; definite insert failure → create T2′; ambiguous → `pending_op` retained, 503 `booking_outcome_unknown`). Either create T2/T2′ writes a **non-null** `latest_action`, clears `unfinished_create` (CF-3), and **issues the `(revision, 'confirm')` claim itself** — that claim belongs to the create, not to notify. `kind='calendar_repair'` → C6.8 (non-stale → 409 `operation_in_progress`; **stale → take over and resume**, REV4-04). `kind='reschedule'` or `'cancel'`, stale or not → **409 `operation_in_progress`**, route returns, nothing sent (those are taken over only by lifecycle calls). This phase is where AC-11(g)'s “next call (`notify` or a create retry) resumes” lives, and it is what unblocks the create that C6.7's cancel T2′ restored (CF-2).
  - **N-2 — calendar repair / reap** on the re-read row, exactly as C6.8 already specifies and unchanged by this ordering: `status='confirmed' AND calendar_state='failed' AND pending_op IS NULL` → begin the owned `calendar_repair` op; `status='cancelled'` → the bounded, fair C6.3a **reap** of that row's retired ids (cleanup — never `events.insert`/`events.patch`).
  - **N-3 — notification claim, and only now.** The `(revision, action)` validation of the two request forms above runs against the **re-read** row, under the same locked `FOR UPDATE` read as the claim. **The NULL-pair refusal applies here and only here:** a claim that reaches N-3 still carrying `latest_action IS NULL` has no `(revision, action)` pair — both forms are refused **409 `stale_revision`** with `{ revision, latestAction: null }`, the `{ action, expectedRevision }` form because `bookings.latest_action = action` cannot hold against `NULL` and the `{}` form because the pair it would resolve does not exist, and **no `notification_deliveries` row is written, no claim is made, nothing is sent**. By **CF-2** (`pending_op IS NULL ⇒ latest_action IS NOT NULL`) together with N-1 (which either finalizes the create or returns before N-3 ever runs), this branch is **unreachable on any committed row this PLAN produces**: it is a **defensive invariant check**, logged `notify_no_pair { bookingId, revision }`, and AC-11 asserts across the whole suite that it never fires.

  **What an unfinished create actually gets from notify is N-1's answer**, never `stale_revision`: 409 `operation_in_progress` while another worker owns the create (non-stale) or owns a reschedule/cancel over it, and otherwise the finalized booking's emails, because notify itself completed the create in N-1. A row whose create is unfinished still **requires no notification of its own** — the create's T2/T2′ issues the single `(1, 'confirm')` send at the moment it finalizes, and nothing else ever owes an email for `revision = 1`. On the read envelopes of such a row `delivery.email` is `"pending"` (no ledger row exists for either required recipient — C3) beside the `delivery.calendar: "pending"` of REV13-02; the row never rests there, because CF-2 guarantees it always has a resumable owner and **N-1 is what resumes it**.

A send for `(revision, action)` older than the booking's current `(revision, latest_action)` is therefore refused before claiming (409 `stale_revision`), so a late confirmation can never mark a reschedule/cancel delivery `sent`. **What claim-time validation does not do (REV5-05):** it does not order Gmail calls against lifecycle changes. A worker that has committed its claim for `(1, 'confirm')` and then stalls before calling Gmail may resume after another worker committed revision 2 (`cancel`) and sent the cancellation emails; its confirmation is then still sent, with revision-1 content, **after** the cancellation. Rev 6 states this ordering rather than pretending to prevent it: the ledger stays correct (the late send finalises only `(1, 'confirm', recipient)` under its own generation and can never touch a `(2, 'cancel')` row), **no pre-send re-validation is required or relied on** (it would narrow the window, never close it, and is not tested), and README says it in two sentences (REV6-05 — arrival order establishes nothing; the rev-6 clause "the most recent email describes the booking's current state" is **withdrawn**, because in exactly this interleaving the most recently received email describes a confirmed booking that is cancelled): *"Because emails are sent after the booking is saved, an earlier email (for example a confirmation) can occasionally arrive after a later one (for example a cancellation). The order in which emails arrive does not tell you the booking's current state; the booking page linked in every email is the authoritative status."* Every email carries that link (C4). Serialising send issuance with lifecycle changes would need a durable send queue or a lock held across Gmail — both out of scope (Non-goals). **Notify never inserts a calendar event on its own** (REV3-02): on a `status='cancelled'` booking the route first runs the C6.3a **reap** of that row's retired ids (cleanup, not repair — `events.get`, and an `If-Match` `events.delete` of anything present; REV5-04), then only claims and re-sends `(revision, 'cancel')` rows; it issues zero `events.insert`/`events.patch` on a cancelled row whatever `calendar_state` says; on a `status='confirmed'` booking with `calendar_state='failed'` it runs the owned calendar-repair operation of C6.8 (which is serialized with reschedule/cancel through `pending_op`), or takes over and resumes a *stale* `calendar_repair` left by an earlier notify (REV4-04), **before** the email claim, and reports the repair outcome in `delivery.calendar`. The in-memory store implements identical semantics with a synchronous map. **Unavoidable ambiguity, and the actual bound (REV6-04):** if Gmail accepts the message and the `sent` write then fails, the row stays `claimed` and becomes re-claimable after 2 minutes. What claim generations bound is **claim acquisition**, not delivery, and the acquisition bound is stated precisely (REV7-04): for one `(revision, action, recipient)` a **`claimed`** row — its send in progress, or its outcome unresolved (Gmail accepted but the `sent` write failed; a timeout; a stalled worker) — can be taken over at most once per 2-minute stale window (every claim resets `claimed_at`), and only when a caller invokes notify or a lifecycle send (there is no background retrier — Non-goals); a **`failed`** row is re-claimable immediately, without any window, because a `failed` finalisation is written only for a definite refusal that delivered nothing (finalisation rule above). So `attempts` is an exact, audit-visible upper bound on the number of copies ever sent for that row, and the number of copies can exceed one only through `claimed`-row takeovers — at most one new *delivering* generation per stale window; a burst of immediate failed retries inside one window is possible and adds no copies. The rev-7 sentence "a new generation can be acquired at most once per 2-minute stale window" is **withdrawn** as stated — it holds for `claimed` rows only. What they do **not** bound is *when* each generation's copy is delivered: a worker that claimed generation *g* and stalled before Gmail sends its copy whenever it resumes, so generations claimed at minutes 0, 2, 4, and 6 that all resume at minute 7 deliver **four** copies inside one window. The rev-2 statement "at most one duplicate per recipient per 2-minute stale window" is therefore **withdrawn**. No issuance-rate mechanism is added: a local send deadline checked before the Gmail call would narrow the cluster but cannot close it (the stall can occur between the check and the call), so it would be a bound in name only and is not offered as one; serialising sends is a Non-goal. README states the real bound (REV7-04 wording): *"A retry can occasionally deliver more than one copy of the same email, and delayed copies may arrive close together; the number of copies never exceeds the number of retries recorded for that email. A retry of an email whose earlier send is still unresolved is accepted at most once per 2-minute stale window; an email that Gmail definitely refused can be retried immediately."* The rev-7 clause "a new retry is accepted at most once per 2-minute stale window" is **withdrawn** (it was false for failed sends). AC-13(k) proves the clustered case; AC-13(l) proves repeated immediate failed retries inside one window. **`delivery.email` is computed against the required recipient set, not against whatever rows exist** (REV4-05): for the booking's current `(revision, latest_action)` and the constant recipient set `{invitee, owner}` (C4), `sent` iff a row exists for **both** recipients and both are `sent`; `failed` if any existing row is `failed`; otherwise `pending` — which includes a recipient with **no row at all** (the lifecycle T2 committed and the process died before that recipient was claimed) and a recipient whose row is `claimed`. Both stores implement the same function (`summarizeDelivery(rows, requiredRecipients)`), and every envelope — lifecycle responses, `GET /api/bookings/{id}`, `/b/{token}` — uses it. Notify therefore **always issues the C5 claim for every required recipient** of the current pair; the `INSERT … ON CONFLICT` inserts a missing row as a fresh claim (`attempts=1`), re-claims `failed`/stale rows, and leaves `sent` and fresh `claimed` rows alone, so a recipient that was never claimed is sent exactly once and a recipient already `sent` is never re-sent. `latest_action IS NULL` (create T1 committed, its T2/T2′ not yet) is not a claimable pair — and notify does not answer it with a refusal: **N-1 resumes the create** (C6.4) and the create's own T2/T2′ issues the `(1, 'confirm')` claim; only a claim that still reaches **N-3** unfinalized is refused `stale_revision`, and CF-2 makes that branch defensive (REV15-01).

### C6. Lifecycle state machine (REV1-08, REV1-09, REV1-10, REV2-01..05, REV3-01..03, REV4-01..04, REV7-01..03, REV9-01, REV9-02; rev 11 Product bar: C6.0, C6.9, REV10-02, REV10-03)

`bookings` carries `status ∈ {confirmed, cancelled}`, `revision int`, `latest_action`, `google_event_id`, `google_event_etag` (C6.3c), `calendar_state` (C6.8), `reserved_start`/`reserved_end timestamptz null`, `unresolved_inserts jsonb` (C6.3a — one entry `{ attemptId, eventId, opId, gen, issuedAt, inspectSeq, inspectedAt }` per insert attempt whose outcome this app never observed, REV5-01; `inspectSeq` is the fair-traversal mark of REV6-02 as revised by REV13-01 — a value drawn from the row's monotonic `reap_cursor`, `null` until first inspected, and the **only** ordering key of the reap; `inspectedAt` is a diagnostic timestamp that orders nothing), `unfinished_create jsonb` (C6.4a — `null`, or the create operation that a non-create op inherited but did **not** complete: `{ opId, gen, eventId, startedAt, attempts }`, REV14-01), and `pending_op jsonb` (`null` or `{ kind: "create" | "reschedule" | "cancel" | "calendar_repair", opId: uuid, gen: int, startedAt, eventId?, newStart?, newEnd?, oldEventId?, fallbackEventId?, etag?: string, attempts: Attempt[] }` — `attempts` is the durable attempt ledger of C6.3b and survives takeover). Create, reschedule, and cancel all begin with the **same per-host serialization boundary**: `SELECT pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext($hostId))` in pg mode, a per-host async mutex in memory mode (the live path is single-host only — group and collective rows are memory-only fixtures outside this contract, C6.9); under it they `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` the booking (lifecycle ops) and re-read `status`, `revision`, and `pending_op`. Every state-changing `UPDATE` is conditional on `id AND revision = $expected AND status = 'confirmed'`; zero rows → 409 `booking_changed`. The lock is held only for the duration of a transaction (T1, T2, T2′, reconciliation); **it is never held across a Google call** — durable reservations (below) make that unnecessary. **Reads vs. mutations (REV6-03):** a Google *read* (`events.list`, `events.get`) may be issued outside any transaction, including *before* T1 — the external-availability read R0 of C6.4/C6.6; a Google *mutation* of the booking's own event (`events.insert`/`patch`/`delete`) is issued only after the transaction that durably records the operation and the attempt (T1, or the attempt write) has committed. The only mutation a pre-T1 read may trigger is C7's reap of *other* rows' retired ids, which is unowned cleanup and never names the id this operation intends.

**C6.0 Shared Google error classification (REV10-02; one contract for C6.2–C6.8 and both adapters).** Every Google Calendar response — insert, get, patch, delete, bump, list — is classified by **one** exported function, `classifyCalendarError` in `lib/google/errors.ts`, used by the live adapter, the mock adapter, the fake `fetch` model, and every C6 step; no lifecycle step carries its own status-code test. The classes: **`applied`** — a definite 2xx; **`definite`** rejection — a 4xx **other than 409, 412, and 429** (400, 401, 403, 404/410 on insert or patch, 422, …; plus `host_not_connected` from the token exchange): the request provably did nothing; **`already_exists`** — 409 `duplicate` (this request created nothing; the *op* is decided by `events.get`, C6.2); **`precondition_failed`** — 412 (this mutation did not happen; C6.3c reconciliation); **`ambiguous`** — timeout, 5xx, **429**, network error, malformed response, process death: the request may have executed or may still execute. **429 is ambiguous, never definite** (Product bar lock §2): a rate-limited request may still be applied by Google, and a definite classification would clear the attempt and its `pending_op` while the insert can still land — exactly the state C6.3a exists to prevent. Consequences, stated once and referenced by every step below: an `ambiguous` insert/patch/delete/bump leaves its attempt `unresolved` (C6.3b), leaves `pending_op` in place, releases no reservation, writes no T2′, and answers 503 `booking_outcome_unknown` with `retryAfterSeconds`; the next attempt — by the same worker, a takeover (`gen+1`), or a cancel taking the op over — reconciles by `events.get` and **carries the 429 attempt forward**: takeover keeps the ledger, the clearing update copies it into `unresolved_inserts`, cancel T2 retains it, and the reap attributes and retires it if its insert ever lands (AC-25). A `definite` rejection is the only class that resolves an attempt as `rejected` and the only one that may enter T2′. Gmail responses use the same rule for the C5 finalisation (4xx other than 429 → definite refusal → `failed`; 429/5xx/timeout/network → ambiguous → the row stays `claimed`), implemented by `classifyGmailError` next to it with identical semantics. Wherever this PLAN says "definite" or "ambiguous" about a Google outcome, it means these classes.

**C6.1 Durable reservations (REV2-01).** A slot is occupied from the moment a transaction that intends to use it commits, not from the moment the calendar or the final update succeeds. Occupancy for a host is the union of: (a) every `status='confirmed'` row **that assigns the host** (`host_id = host`) and its `[start, end)` — including rows whose `pending_op` is a `create` still awaiting its calendar step and rows whose `pending_op` is a `reschedule` (the **old interval is retained** until T2 or reconciliation releases it); and (b) every non-null `[reserved_start, reserved_end)` written by a reschedule T1 (the **destination is reserved durably** in T1). **Every** conflict query — create, reschedule, the owner-scoped availability route, and the booking availability route — is computed over this union, in both stores; occupancy is **single-host and exclusive**: any confirmed row or reservation of the host that overlaps the interval makes it busy, and no event type shares a slot among several attendees on the live path (group capacity is out of LIVE-INT — Product bar lock, C6.9). The one and only exclusion is *the booking's own* interval/reservation/managed event during its own reschedule (AC-8). A reservation is released only by the T2 that consumes it or by a T2′/takeover reconciliation that abandons it; a stale reservation (> 2 min) is reconciled by the next call that reads it, never silently ignored. Interleavings that must serialize correctly (proved in AC-4/AC-18): create vs. reschedule (a create into a reserved destination, or into a rescheduling booking's old slot, is 409 `slot_unavailable` until that reschedule completes or is abandoned); reschedule vs. reschedule across two different bookings (B may not reserve A's destination while A holds it, and may not reserve A's old slot until A's T2 commits); cancel vs. create (a booking under cancellation still occupies its slot until cancel T2).

**C6.2 Operation identity, intended event ids, idempotent inserts (REV2-03).** Every lifecycle operation writes `pending_op.opId` (fresh uuid), `pending_op.gen` (ownership generation, starts at 1), and — before any calendar mutation — the **exact Google event id it intends to create**, if it may create one: `pending_op.eventId` on create, `pending_op.fallbackEventId` on reschedule. Ids are generated app-side in Google's accepted alphabet (`[a-v0-9]{5,1024}`, derived as `marlo` + base32hex of a fresh uuid) and passed as the `id` field of `events.insert`, so a replay is **idempotent by observation**: Google *usually* answers a replayed id with 409 `duplicate` (mapped to `AlreadyExists`), but Google documents that id-collision detection at creation time is **not guaranteed**, so the app never treats `duplicate` (or its absence) as proof of anything — after any insert response other than a `definite` rejection (C6.0 — 429 is never definite), the caller confirms the outcome with `events.get(id)` (present with this booking's `marloBookingId` → applied; absent → not applied), and it is `events.get`, not the insert status, that decides (REV4-01). Every insert is an **attempt** in the C6.3b ledger; the insert or get response's `etag` is captured into `google_event_etag` by the completing update (C6.3c). Because the intended id is durable before the call, an accepted-but-response-lost insert, a timeout, or a process death between the insert and the completing update is reconciled by the next call that reads the `pending_op`: `events.get(intendedId)` → present → proceed to the completing update; absent (404/410) → re-insert with the same id. Operation state is cleared **only** by the completing update, and only after the Google outcome is known; an ambiguous Google outcome (C6.0: timeout, 5xx, 429, network error) leaves `pending_op` in place and is resolved by `events.get` on the next attempt, never by a blind delete. Both calendar adapters (mock and live) honour client-supplied ids and `AlreadyExists`.

**C6.3 Ownership generations and takeover (REV2-04).** `pending_op.gen` is the operation's ownership generation. A worker that resumes a stale op (`startedAt` > 2 min ago, same or different kind per the policy in C6.6) takes ownership with `UPDATE … SET pending_op = pending_op || {gen: gen+1, startedAt: now()} WHERE id=$1 AND pending_op->>'opId'=$opId AND (pending_op->>'gen')::int=$gen` (zero rows → someone else took over first → 409 `operation_in_progress`). **Every** completing update (T2), clearing update (T2′), reservation release, and compensation is conditioned on `pending_op->>'opId'=$opId AND (pending_op->>'gen')::int=$gen` in addition to `revision`. A worker whose conditional update matches zero rows is **superseded**: it must not compensate, must not insert, must not delete, and must not clear anything. It re-reads the booking (fresh transaction, under lock) and **reconciles the winning operation**: if the row shows exactly the outcome it was trying to reach (reschedule: `status='confirmed'`, `revision` advanced, `start = newStart`; cancel: `status='cancelled'`; create and create replay: the row carries this worker's **immutable create identity** and its business creation is **complete** — C6.3a(1), REV9-01 — never a `latest_action` test) it returns that outcome **with the calling route's success status** — **201** with the C3 envelope for a create (first-time or same-key replay, incl. the legacy and C10 link routes), **200** for reschedule, cancel, and notify/repair (REV8-02) — exactly as if it had done the work; otherwise it returns 409 `operation_superseded` with the current `booking`. A takeover begins by determining what the previous owner achieved from Google, not from memory: `events.get(oldEventId)` and, if set, `events.get(fallbackEventId)` / `events.get(eventId)`; the takeover then completes or abandons the op from that observed state (C6.4–C6.6). Before **every** Google mutation (insert, patch, delete, compensation, version bump) the worker writes the attempt under lock (C6.3b: `UPDATE … SET pending_op = jsonb_set(pending_op, '{attempts}', pending_op->'attempts' || $attempt) WHERE id AND pending_op->>'opId'=$opId AND (pending_op->>'gen')::int=$gen`, `COMMIT`); zero rows → superseded, and a superseded worker that *observes* it is superseded never reaches Google. A takeover's **first** Google mutation is always the version bump of C6.3c, so a predecessor that passed its attempt write and then paused cannot mutate the existing event either.

**C6.3a Intended-id retention and reap (REV4-01; withdraws the rev-4 event-id fence).** Database generations do not fence Google: a worker can pass its attempt write, pause, and issue its insert after a takeover; or Google can execute an insert long after the request timed out on our side. Rev 4 answered this with a `status='cancelled'` placeholder under the intended id; that is **withdrawn**, because Google's events contract guarantees neither that a cancelled event's id is retained for the calendar's lifetime nor that an id collision is detected at creation time — a placeholder is at best a hint, never exclusion. Rev 5 therefore **assumes nothing about exclusion and tracks the id instead**; rev 6 tracks the **attempt** (REV5-01) and keeps two questions separate: *does an event exist under this id now?* — answered by `events.get(id)`, and the only thing an op needs in order to complete against the id — and *has each individual insert request for this id finished executing?* — answered **only** by that request's own definite response (C6.3b), never by observing an event (a different request for the same id may have created it) and never by deleting one (another request for the same id may still execute). An **event is present** when `events.get(id)` returns a non-cancelled event carrying this booking's `marloBookingId`; the op that intended the id is then *completed* against it (a takeover never abandons a present id), or, when the row is cancelled, it is deleted with a definite outcome (C6.3c `If-Match` delete; 2xx/404/410). An **insert attempt is resolved** only when its issuing request returned a definite 2xx (`applied`) or a `definite` non-409, non-429 4xx (C6.0) / 409 `duplicate` (`rejected` — this request created nothing); an attempt whose request timed out, 5xx'd, was answered 429, lost its connection, or whose worker died is `unresolved` for as long as this app exists. Every `unresolved` insert attempt is **retained on the row**: the clearing update that nulls `pending_op` (T2, T2′, cancel T2, repair T2) copies each `unresolved` insert entry of `pending_op.attempts` into `unresolved_inserts` (jsonb array of `{ attemptId, eventId, opId, gen, issuedAt }`, keyed by `attemptId`, deduplicated) — **including attempts whose `eventId` is the resulting `google_event_id`**: a create whose first insert timed out and whose retry applied keeps attempt 1 listed under the live id, and a successful T2 discards nothing. **Live vs. retired (REV5-02):** an id is **live** iff `status='confirmed' AND id = google_event_id`; every other id an entry names is **retired**. The two states are mutually exclusive by definition and *retired is absorbing*: `google_event_id` is only ever assigned a fresh, never-before-used id (create T1, reschedule fallback), a cancelled row never returns to `confirmed`, and repair only ever targets the row's current `google_event_id` — **no id is ever promoted from retired back to live**. Only **retired** ids are reap-eligible; an unresolved attempt on the live id is not reaped (an event under the live id *is* the booking's event: C6.8 repair adopts it, C7 matches it) and becomes reap-eligible the moment the id is retired (cancel T2; a reschedule T2 that switched to its fallback id). The **reap** is one procedure, callable by any worker, needing no ownership: it reads the row under the host lock and computes the **eligible set** `{ e.eventId | e ∈ unresolved_inserts } \ { google_event_id if status='confirmed' }` **in that same read** (never from an earlier snapshot); **orders it fairly** (REV6-02, ordering key revised by REV13-01: by the smallest `inspectSeq` among the entries naming each id, `null` first, ties by `issuedAt`, then by `eventId` — a total order over the eligible set), selects the first `REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST = 5` ids as this request's **batch**, and — in that same locked transaction, before any Google call — **advances the row's reap cursor once per batched id**, in batch order, and stamps the resulting values as `inspectSeq` on every entry naming those ids (one statement inside the locked transaction: `UPDATE bookings SET reap_cursor = reap_cursor + k, unresolved_inserts = <the k batched ids' entries stamped with reap_cursor+1 … reap_cursor+k and inspectedAt = now()> WHERE id = $1`). **`inspectSeq` is the reap's only ordering key; `inspectedAt` is a diagnostic and orders nothing (REV13-01).** `bookings.reap_cursor bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (C8) is a per-row sequence advanced under the same host lock that selects the batch, so the traversal advances **strictly monotonically whatever the clock does** — a frozen fake clock, a timestamp granularity coarser than the gap between two calls, or a backwards NTP step can no longer give two inspections the same mark, and a paused or failed reaper still cannot keep its batch at the head of the order.
