An id that is definitely absent therefore moves to the back of the order and can never starve a later id: every eligible id is inspected within ⌈|eligible| / `REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST`⌉ consecutive observing calls and, in the form AC-11(p) proves, **whenever `|eligible| ≤ 2 × REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST` the union of any two consecutive observing calls inspects every eligible id** — with no assumption that the clock moved between them. Two concurrent reapers select **disjoint** batches only when at least `2 × REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST = 10` ids are eligible; below that the two batches necessarily overlap in `2 × REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST − |eligible|` ids (six eligible ids against a batch of five overlap in four — two five-id batches drawn from six ids cannot be disjoint), which is harmless and is not a guarantee this PLAN relies on: the two reapers are serialized by the host lock, the second one's batch *begins* with exactly the ids the first did not take (those carry the lower `inspectSeq`), and a second `events.get`/delete against an id the first already reaped finds it absent and retires nothing — `retireAttempt` is idempotent by `attemptId` and never touches another attempt's entry (REV6-01). Then, outside the lock, for each batched id: `events.get(id)` → present with our `marloBookingId` → read the event's `marloAttemptId` (**attribution**, REV6-01: every `events.insert` this app issues sets `extendedProperties.private.marloAttemptId = <that attempt's attemptId>` next to `marloBookingId`, so an event names the one request that created it) → `events.delete(id, If-Match: <observed etag>)` → **2xx/404/410** → `retireAttempt(bookingId, marloAttemptId)`: under lock, `UPDATE bookings SET unresolved_inserts = <the list minus the entry whose attemptId = $a> WHERE id=$1` — the observation proved that *this* request executed (the event it created existed), so exactly that attempt is retired, **never the oldest and never an unidentified one**; the retirement is **idempotent by `attemptId`** (retiring an already-retired attempt changes nothing) and is the one cleanup rule shared by reapers, cancel, notify, C7, and issuing workers, so two reapers that both observed the same event — one whose delete answered 2xx and one whose delete answered 404 — retire the same single entry once, and an entry whose request may still execute is never touched by a deletion it did not cause; 412 → one more `events.get`: present under a *different* `marloAttemptId` → retire the observed attempt anyway (its event existed and is gone) and leave the new event for the next batch (never deleted with a stale etag); same `marloAttemptId` with a new etag → one delete retry with the observed etag; absent → nothing changes (every entry may still land); ambiguous get/delete → nothing changes; a managed event under a retired id that carries no `marloAttemptId` (nothing this PLAN issues — defensive) → deleted with `If-Match`, retires nothing. Because a retired id is never live again, a reaper that paused after computing its eligible set — or after its `events.get` — and issues its `If-Match` delete arbitrarily later can only delete an event nobody will ever adopt (REV5-02, proved sequentially and paused in AC-23(j)(k)). The reap runs (a) at the start of every **reschedule, cancel, notify, `GET /api/bookings/{id}`, and `/b/{token}`** call that reads a row with a non-empty **eligible set** — **and never on a create-route L0 replay of a completed row** (REV11-02): the C6.4 L0 / C9 same-key replay of a row with `pending_op IS NULL` answers 201 from the row alone with **zero Google calls of any kind**, even when that row is `cancelled` and `unresolved_inserts` retains reap-eligible ids (AC-19(i)(7)); the entries stay listed and the next designated observing call reaps them. A stale-create takeover has no eligible set by construction (no clearing update has yet run on a row whose only op is its create), so the create route never reaps its own row; the only reap the create route can trigger is C7's, during R0, over *other* rows' retired ids — and (b) inside C7's `events.list` classification for any managed item whose `marloBookingId` row names the item's `id` as a **retired** id — bounded to `REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST = 5` deletes per availability request, chosen in the same fair `inspectSeq` order and stamped from the row's `reap_cursor` the same way (the listed items need no `events.get`; the item's `marloAttemptId` attributes the deletion); in every case the item is **external busy for that request** whether or not the reap succeeded (C7), so a late-landed insert can never free-ride into a double booking. The **product guarantee is stated honestly** (README, Assumptions; REV5-03): a superseded or delayed insert (i) is **never offered as free time** — it is external busy for every availability computation that observes it, whether or not the reap succeeds; (ii) **never creates, changes, or rejects a booking row**; and (iii) **never persists** — it is deleted by the next call that observes it. What is **not** promised: interval occupancy is *not* held while an insert may still execute (Google bounds nothing about delayed execution, so the hold would be unbounded — Non-goals). A delayed insert that lands **after** its booking was cancelled and the interval released, and after a **new** booking has taken that interval, therefore produces a **transient overlap on the host's Google Calendar** — the new booking's event and the late retained-id event — until the next observing call reaps the late one; the new booking is never rejected, undone, or re-notified because of it, and the late event is never a booking. AC-12(l) proves this interleaving; README states it in the same words. The **superseded worker's side — response reconciliation and cleanup eligibility are two separate determinations made in one locked read (REV7-01; revises the rev-7 procedure, under which "the row no longer shows my target" was enough to enter cleanup)**: on any insert response the worker runs the C6.2 `events.get(id)` and attempts its owned completion; zero rows → superseded → it re-reads the row **under lock** and, from that single read, decides both of the following. **(1) Response — reschedule/cancel:** the row shows exactly its target (C6.3: reschedule `status='confirmed'`, `revision` advanced, `start = newStart`; cancel `status='cancelled'`) → **200** with the reconciled `booking` as if it had done the work; otherwise → 409 `operation_superseded` with the current `booking`. **(1) Response — create and create replay (REV9-01; revises the rev-8 `latest_action='confirm'` test, which a later reschedule or cancel legitimately falsifies):** success is decided from two facts the intervening lifecycle can never change, and from nothing else — never `latest_action`, `status`, `start`, `revision`, or `calendar_state`. *(i) Identity:* the locked read `SELECT … WHERE id = $rowId` returns a row whose `owner_id`, `idempotency_key`, and `create_fingerprint` equal this worker's (all three are written once in create T1 and never updated — C9/REV3-05 — and `id` is app-generated, so the row is *this* booking and no other). *(ii) **Finalized** business creation (REV14-01; revises the rev-9 test `pending_op IS NULL OR pending_op->>'opId' <> $opId`, which a cancel that merely **replaces** an unfinished create also satisfies):* `latest_action IS NOT NULL` **and** (`pending_op IS NULL OR pending_op->>'opId' <> $opId`). The second conjunct is the rev-9 clearing test, kept verbatim. The first is the durable finalization witness of C6.4a, and it tests the column's **nullness, never its value** — so the rev-9 objection to `latest_action = 'confirm'` (a later reschedule or cancel legitimately changes the *value*) does not apply, and (r-ii)/(r-iii)'s intervening reschedule and intervening cancel still answer 201 exactly as REV9-01/REV11-01 require. A create op cleared by its own T2, its own T2′, or a takeover that **completed** it satisfies both conjuncts: the booking is a finalized business record, whatever has happened to it since. A create op cleared by the C6.7 cancel that took over a **stale create whose event was absent** satisfies only the second: that cancel replaced the create without completing it, the booking's creation is still unfinished (`latest_action IS NULL`, `calendar_state='pending'`, CF-1), and no 201 may be issued for it — the only envelope such a row could produce carries `delivery.calendar: 'pending'`, which C3 forbids on a create 201, and C5 has no `(revision, action)` pair to compute `delivery.email` from. Both hold → **201** with the complete C3 envelope of the row's **current** state — precisely what a C9 replay of the same key returns (C9): after an intervening reschedule `start = T`, `revision = 2`, `latest_action = 'reschedule'`; after an intervening cancel `status = 'cancelled'`, `latest_action = 'cancel'`, `delivery.calendar = 'deleted'`; `delivery` computed over the current `(revision, latest_action)` per C5. The shipped client recognises only 201 as create success (REV8-02), so this is the only success status a create worker ever answers. The row still names this `opId` under a **higher `gen`** (the takeover is still running; (ii) fails) → 409 `operation_in_progress` with `retryAfterSeconds` — the same **non-terminal** answer a C9 replay receives while the create is active elsewhere, so the client keeps its key and replays into the completed booking instead of treating a live booking as lost. **Identity holds, (ii) fails, and the creation is unfinished under someone else's op or under a restored one (REV14-01):** `latest_action IS NULL` **and** either `pending_op` names a **different** op (the C6.7 cancel that took this create over with its event absent, `kind='cancel'`) or `unfinished_create` is non-null → 409 `operation_in_progress` with `retryAfterSeconds`, **non-terminal**, with **zero Google calls and zero mutations of its own**: the client keeps its key (C9) and replays into whichever outcome the cancel reaches — cancel T2 → the cancelled booking's 201 (r-iii/REV11-01, unchanged), cancel T2′ → the restored, immediately-takeable create op (C6.7), which the replay takes over and resumes to its own T2 → 201 with `delivery.calendar: 'created'`. This is the one branch in which “my op is no longer named” does **not** mean “my booking was created”; it never answers 201, never answers `operation_superseded`, and never answers a terminal error. Cleanup eligibility is, as always, the separate determination (2), and in this branch the intended id is **live** (`status='confirmed' AND google_event_id = id`) → **not eligible** → zero deletes, zero further gets; the attempt stays listed and is handled by the cancel's attributed delete or by the reap (AC-11(r-v)). No row under this `id`, or a row with a different create identity → 409 `operation_superseded` with the current `booking` (unreachable by construction — ids are app-generated and the identity columns immutable — kept as the defensive branch and logged `create_identity_mismatch`; C9 treats it as non-terminal too). The identity rule is the single implementation behind `reconcileSuperseded` for create (Approach §4) and behind the C9 replay of a completed row; the two can never disagree about whether a booking exists. **(2) Cleanup eligibility of its own attempt** — independent of (1) and **unchanged by REV9-01** (an intervening reschedule by patch keeps the id live → not eligible, zero deletes; an intervening cancel retires the id and its attributed deletion already retired this worker's attempt → not retained → zero deletes; an intervening reschedule that fell back to a new id retires this one with the attempt still retained → eligible → the worker removes only its own attributed event; all three are proved in AC-11(r)), and never inferred from the row's interval: the id is **live** (`status='confirmed' AND google_event_id = id`) → **not eligible**, *even when the booking no longer matches this worker's target*: the winner adopted the event (a takeover completes a present id, C6.3/C6.7) and may since have moved it by patch (the id stays live across a move, C6.6), so the event under it *is* the booking's event; the worker issues **no delete, no further `events.get`, no mutation**, and its entry stays listed (in the op's ledger until the clearing update copies it, then in `unresolved_inserts` under the live id, where it is not reap-eligible until the id is retired and the reap attributes it — AC-11(l)). The id is still an **intended id of an open op** (`pending_op` non-null and naming it as `eventId`/`fallbackEventId`; the takeover has not cleared the op) → **not eligible**: the winner may yet adopt it. The id is **retired** (`unresolved_inserts` names it and it is not live — the row is `cancelled`, or `google_event_id ≠ id`) **and** the worker's own `attemptId` is **retained** there → **eligible**. Retired but **not retained** (a reaper already attributed and retired the entry — its event is already gone) → not eligible, nothing to do. **Cleanup** runs only for an eligible attempt and only on a **definite 2xx** insert response (the one thing that resolves *this* attempt, C6.3b: a 2xx means *this request* created the event, and the response body carries its own `marloAttemptId`): `events.delete(id, If-Match: <the insert's response etag>)` → 2xx/404/410 → `retireAttempt(bookingId, own attemptId)` through the same idempotent rule (REV6-01: a 404/410 here means a reaper already deleted the event and attributed it — the reaper retired the same `attemptId`, so this retirement is a no-op, and no *other* attempt's entry is touched by either party; concurrent-reaper and delayed-response interleavings are proved in AC-11(n)(o)); 412 → `events.get(id)` → present under a *different* `marloAttemptId` → its own event is already gone: retire its own entry, delete nothing; present under its **own** `marloAttemptId` with a new etag (a version bump) → **one** delete retry with the observed etag — permitted *only because* eligibility was determined under lock and **retired is absorbing** (REV5-02: no id is ever promoted from retired back to live, so an id found retired in that read is retired at the moment of the retry, and the retry can remove only the worker's own attributed event, which nothing will ever adopt); absent → nothing changes. The observed-etag retry is **never** reachable from a live or open-op id, because those never enter cleanup. A 409/4xx insert response means the worker created nothing → it retires its entry with no delete (same locked read; a live/open-op id keeps its entry until the clearing update handles it). An ambiguous response leaves the entry for the reap, which will find the event attributed to it if it ever lands. Beyond that it makes zero mutations of its own. The rule of this PLAN is unchanged and now actually holds for the superseded worker too: **no worker ever deletes an event that is not either the row's `google_event_id` under its own owned op or a retired id named by `unresolved_inserts`**; AC-12(m) proves the delayed-insert-response → takeover/adoption → subsequent-move interleaving that the rev-7 wording violated. The mock and live adapters and the fake `fetch` model **both** documented Google behaviours for a re-used id — `collisionMode: 'duplicate'` (409) and `collisionMode: 'land'` (the late insert succeeds and the event exists; a held insert released while a non-cancelled event still exists under that id answers 409 in both modes, and lands only after that event was deleted) — ; every insert body carries `extendedProperties.private.marloAttemptId`, `get`/`list` return it, and the fake rejects an insert without it (REV6-01); and every REV4-01, REV5-01..03, REV6-01/02, and REV7-01 scenario is run under both.

**C6.3b Attempt ledger (REV4-03).** `pending_op.attempts` is a durable, append-only list `{ attemptId: uuid, kind: 'insert' | 'patch' | 'delete' | 'bump', eventId, gen, issuedAt, ifMatch?: string, outcome: 'unresolved' | 'applied' | 'rejected', resolvedAt? }`. The entry is written **before** the request (the attempt write of C6.3, conditioned on opId/gen — for the first insert of a create/reschedule/repair it is part of T1 itself) and finalised **only by the issuing worker** from that request's own response: definite 2xx → `applied`; `definite` non-409 4xx (C6.0 — never 429) → `rejected`; 409 `duplicate` → `rejected` for *this* attempt (this request created nothing; whether an event exists is answered by the following `events.get`, which decides the **op**, not the attempt — REV5-01); 412 → `rejected` for *this* attempt (the mutation did not happen) and the worker enters C6.3c reconciliation; timeout / 5xx / **429** / network / process death (C6.0 `ambiguous`) → stays `unresolved` forever. **A later attempt never resolves an earlier one**: a retry (same worker or a takeover under a new `gen`) appends a new entry; its definite 4xx marks only its own entry `rejected`. Takeover keeps the list (`pending_op || {gen, startedAt}` never touches `attempts`); `events.get` observations apply to **ids**, never to attempts (REV5-01): "present" lets the op complete against the id and "absent" lets a takeover abandon or cancel around it, but neither changes any attempt's `outcome` — an attempt is `applied` or `rejected` only by its own request's response, and a deletion resolves nothing in the ledger either (the reap retires the *row entry* of the one attempt the deleted event names through its `marloAttemptId`, C6.3a/REV6-01). **Clearing rule (REV5-01):** a completing/clearing/cancelling update (T2, T2′, cancel T2, repair T2) may set `pending_op = NULL` only when **every** `unresolved` insert attempt in `pending_op.attempts` — **including those whose `eventId` is the resulting `google_event_id`** — has been appended to `unresolved_inserts` as `{ attemptId, eventId, opId, gen, issuedAt }` in that same `UPDATE` (C6.3a); the ledger is never discarded while it still carries uncertainty, and a *successful* T2 discards nothing either. `create` T2 after a timed-out first insert and an applied retry therefore writes `calendar_state='created', pending_op=NULL, unresolved_inserts = unresolved_inserts || [attempt 1]` (its id is live, hence not reap-eligible, until cancel or a fallback reschedule retires it — AC-11(l)); `create` T2′ after a **definite** insert failure writes `calendar_state='failed', pending_op=NULL, unresolved_inserts = unresolved_inserts || [every unresolved attempt]` (the row's `google_event_id` still names that id for repair, C6.8, and stays live). **Cancel observes outstanding inserts on every row**, including `calendar_state='failed'`: before its delete step it runs the bounded, fair reap batch over the row's retired ids (cleanup, never a T2 precondition — REV6-02) and `events.get` over **every** intended id of the taken-over op (C6.7), and its T2 retires the live id, so a late landing under it — including by the first insert of a create whose retry succeeded — is reaped per C6.3a. Outstanding attempts are logged `attempt_unresolved { bookingId, opId, gen, attemptId, eventId }` on every clearing update that retains an id.

**C6.3c ETag-conditioned mutations and version invalidation on takeover (REV4-02).** Google's resource versioning (`etag` on every event; `If-Match` on `patch`/`update`/`delete`; 412 Precondition Failed on mismatch) is the only provider-side fence the contract offers, and rev 5 uses it for **every** mutation of an existing event. The row stores `google_event_etag`, written by every T2 that creates, patches, observes, or bumps the event (from the insert/patch/get response). T1 of reschedule and cancel snapshots it into `pending_op.etag`; if it is `NULL` (a row created before this column, or `calendar_state='failed'`), the op first runs `events.get(google_event_id)` after T1 and records the observed etag in the op. Every `events.patch` (move, compensating move, bump) and `events.delete` (cancel, compensating delete, reap) sends `If-Match: <the etag this worker last observed or produced>`; the adapter maps 412 to `PreconditionFailed`. **412 is reconciliation, never a blind retry**: the worker marks the attempt `rejected`, re-reads the row under lock; not owner → superseded path (C6.3, zero mutations); still owner → `events.get(id)` → (i) the event already shows this op's **target** (interval = `newStart/newEnd` and our `marloBookingId`; or, for a delete, 404/410) → treat as applied, capture the etag, proceed to T2; (ii) the event shows this op's **expected pre-state** (interval = the row's current `[start,end)`, our `marloBookingId`) with a different etag → **one** retry with the observed etag after a fresh attempt write; a second 412 → (iii); (iii) anything else (host-edited interval, foreign change) → definite failure: T2′ (`calendar_patch_failed` / `calendar_delete_failed`), reservation released, `pending_op` cleared per C6.3b, the event left as-is (C7 treats a displaced managed event as busy; no synchronization, Non-goals). **Takeover invalidates the predecessor's version before anything else**: after taking ownership (`gen+1`) and before it releases a reservation, completes another op, abandons, or deletes, the new owner issues a **version bump** — `events.patch(id, { extendedProperties.private.marloOpGen: '<opId>:<gen>' }, If-Match: <etag observed by its own events.get>)` — on the taken-over op's `oldEventId` (and on `fallbackEventId`/`eventId` when `events.get` shows it applied). A bump changes the etag, so every `If-Match` the predecessor holds (its snapshot, its own patch's response etag, its insert's response etag) is stale: a delayed move, a delayed compensating patch, a delayed compensating delete, and a delayed cancel delete all get 412 and enter the superseded path. Bump outcomes: 2xx → the new etag is stored in the row and the op, proceed; 412 → `events.get` → the etag already differs from anything the predecessor could hold → re-observe and proceed (once), else treat as (iii); 404/410 → nothing exists for the predecessor to patch, proceed (its insert attempts are governed by C6.3a); **ambiguous** (C6.0, incl. 429) → the takeover stops, keeps `pending_op` under its new `gen`, returns 503 `booking_outcome_unknown`; the retry re-observes with `events.get` (an etag that differs from the pre-bump value proves the bump applied). Compensation (C6.6 T2 definite failure) and the superseded worker follow the same rule: a compensating patch/delete carries the etag the worker's own mutation returned and is preceded by an attempt write; 412 → zero further mutations, reconcile. Both calendar adapters and the fake `fetch` model etags (a new value on every mutation, `If-Match` enforcement with 412, `etag` in every response) and the mock adapter exposes them identically in memory mode.

**C6.4 Create (two-phase, REV2-03, REV2-09, REV3-03, REV3-04, REV6-03, REV7-02, REV8-01).** **L0 — locked idempotency lookup, before any Google traffic (REV7-02):** under the host lock, `SELECT … WHERE owner_id=$1 AND idempotency_key=$2` (the C9 lookup) **and the rejection-fence lookup** `SELECT reason FROM create_rejections WHERE owner_id=$1 AND idempotency_key=$2 AND create_fingerprint=$3` (REV8-01; the request's fingerprint is computed per C9 before the lock). **Fence present** → 409 with the fenced `reason` (`slot_unavailable` / `session_full`), zero Google calls, nothing written (the rejection this key+payload already received is repeated, never re-evaluated). **Row present** → compare the request's fingerprint with `create_fingerprint` (C9): different → 422 `idempotency_key_reused`; equal → **return or resume from the row with zero `events.list` calls**, decided by **the same finalization predicate the superseded-worker rule uses** (C6.4a / C6.3a(1)(ii), REV14-01 — the replay rule and the identity rule can never disagree about whether a booking exists): **`latest_action IS NOT NULL`** (creation finalized) → **201** with the row's current state, zero Google calls of any kind, **whatever `pending_op` holds** — `NULL`, or a live reschedule/cancel/repair — and **including when the row is `cancelled` and `unresolved_inserts` retains reap-eligible ids**: this read does not trigger the C6.3a reap (REV11-02); those ids are cleaned up by the designated observing calls listed in C6.3a. **`latest_action IS NULL`** (creation unfinished; equivalently `calendar_state='pending'`, CF-1) → **never 201**, and the answer is decided by `pending_op`: `kind='create'` not stale → 409 `operation_in_progress` with `retryAfterSeconds` (zero Google calls); `kind='create'` stale → take over (C6.3) and *resume* below (its `events.get(eventId)` and insert-if-absent are the only Google calls, both against the op's own intended id — never a read of the host's calendar) — this is also how the create op **restored by a cancel T2′** (C6.7) is picked up, its `startedAt` being the original create's and therefore already past the stale window; `kind='cancel'` (a C6.7 cancel took this create over with its event absent and has not finished; `unfinished_create` is set) → **409 `operation_in_progress`** with `retryAfterSeconds`, zero Google calls, nothing written, **non-terminal** (REV14-01) — a create replay never takes over a cancel at any age (the same one-policy rule C6.6 states: a stale cancel is resumed only by a cancel call), and the row is driven to resolution by the guest's cancel retry, which C12's `pending → stalled` state exposes (REV8-03). `pending_op IS NULL` together with `latest_action IS NULL` is excluded by CF-2 and is never produced by any transaction in this PLAN; if it is ever read it is logged `unfinished_create_orphan` and answered 409 `operation_in_progress`, never 201. A replay therefore never depends on the Calendar read path: a completed booking whose 201 was lost, and a create still owned elsewhere, replay during a Calendar outage (AC-19(i)); the shipped `booking.ts → listAvailableTimes → provider` chain is entered only for a missing key. **Neither present** → the L0 transaction ends (lock released) and the request proceeds to R0. **R0 — external-availability read**, outside any transaction and lock, **missing keys only**: `events.list` on the host's primary calendar over `[start, end)` classified per C7 (this is the create route's existing `listAvailableTimes` step in `lib/booking/booking.ts`, now backed by C7's `events.list` classification for the primary calendar) → the external busy set **X** for the interval; fail-closed → 503 `availability_unknown`, nothing written, **no fence** (non-terminal: the client retains its key, C9). R0 is a *read*; the only mutation it may trigger is C7's reap of *other* rows' retired ids (unowned cleanup that never names this booking's intended id). T1 (under lock): the **mandatory second** C9 idempotency lookup **and the mandatory second fence lookup** (the key may have been consumed — *or rejected* — by a concurrent same-key request between L0 and this lock: row present → the L0 rules above apply from this read, X discarded: a replay of an existing row never consults X; fence present → 409 with its `reason`, nothing written, X discarded — REV8-01); **external-busy check**: `[start, end)` intersects X → **terminal rejection** (below) with `reason = 'slot_unavailable'`; then the **managed** conflict check over C6.1 occupancy (the locked re-check — X is a snapshot, managed occupancy is authoritative under the lock) → overlap → terminal rejection `slot_unavailable` (single-host, exclusive; `session_full` is produced only by the memory-mode group fixture's existing capacity check, C6.9, and is fenced the same way where it occurs). **Terminal rejection = durable fence (REV8-01; revises the rev-6 "no key consumed" rejection):** no `bookings` row is inserted and no op is written, but before `COMMIT`, in the **same T1 transaction under the host lock**, the worker inserts the fence row `create_rejections (owner_id, idempotency_key, create_fingerprint, reason, rejected_at)` (C8; memory store: the equivalent `rejectedKeys` map under the host mutex), commits, and only then answers 409 `reason`. The fence is what makes the rejection **terminal** for the client (C9): the locked lookup proved that no row exists for this key *now*, and the fence guarantees that **no request carrying this key and fingerprint can ever insert one later** — in particular an earlier request that passed L0 and R0 and is **paused before its T1**: its T1 runs under the same lock, its mandatory second lookup finds the fence, and it returns the same 409 with zero `INSERT INTO bookings`, zero `pending_op`, zero `events.insert`, logged `create_fenced { key, reason }`. The locked lookup alone proved absence only at that instant; the fence turns it into absence forever. Fence and row are **mutually exclusive by the lock**: a fence is written only when no row exists for the key, a row only when no fence exists for `(key, fingerprint)`, both decided in the transaction that writes. The fence is per `(key, fingerprint)`, not per key: the browser client never reuses a key after a terminal response (C9), so for it the two are equivalent, while the C10 link path, whose key is the link token, may still book a different slot after a rejected one (AC-4(e), AC-22(j)). A fence `COMMIT` that fails definitely → 500 `booking_failed` (non-terminal: the client retains its key and replays; no fence, no row); unknown → C6.5 reconciliation reads both `bookings` by `(owner_id, idempotency_key)` and `create_rejections` by `(owner_id, idempotency_key, create_fingerprint)` after the lock is reacquired: row → continue at the calendar step, fence → the fenced 409, neither → 500 `booking_failed`. `INSERT` (app-generated `id`, `token`, `idempotency_key`, `create_fingerprint` (C9), `status='confirmed'`, `revision=1`, `latest_action=NULL`, `calendar_state='pending'`, `google_event_id = intendedId`, `pending_op = {kind:'create', opId, gen:1, eventId: intendedId, startedAt}`) → `COMMIT`. The slot is now durably held. Calendar: `events.insert(id=intendedId, attendees per C4)` (attempt recorded in T1, C6.3b); the outcome is decided by `events.get` per C6.2 (`AlreadyExists` → this attempt is `rejected` (it created nothing) and `events.get` decides the op: a live event with our `marloBookingId` → the create completes against it; absent → one further insert attempt, while any earlier timed-out attempt for the id stays `unresolved` — REV5-01); a worker whose T2 then matches zero rows is superseded and reconciles by create identity, not by `latest_action` (C6.3a(1), REV9-01). T2 (under lock): `UPDATE latest_action='confirm', calendar_state='created', google_event_etag=$etag, pending_op=NULL, unresolved_inserts = unresolved_inserts || <every unresolved insert attempt of the op, incl. those for eventId> WHERE id AND revision=1 AND pending_op->>'opId'=$opId AND (pending_op->>'gen')::int=$gen` (C6.3b clearing rule, REV5-01) → `COMMIT` → email (C5, `revision=1`, `action=confirm`) → 201 with `booking` (incl. `token`) and `delivery`. **Resume** (same key retried, or any later lifecycle call that reads `pending_op.kind='create'`): if the op is **not stale** (`startedAt` within 2 min) another worker still owns it → 409 `operation_in_progress` with `retryAfterSeconds`, nothing written, no Google call (the client keeps its key and retries, C9); if stale → take ownership (C6.3), `events.get(eventId)` → present → T2; absent → insert with the same id → T2; then return 201 with the original booking and its current `delivery`. A create is **never abandoned**: its intended id is either completed or, when the row is cancelled by a takeover (C6.7), retained in `unresolved_inserts` until it is observed and reaped (C6.3a). Failures: T1 `COMMIT` definite failure → nothing to compensate (no Google mutation has happened; R0 was a read), 500 `booking_failed`, no token. T1 `COMMIT` unknown → C6.5 reconciliation by `(owner_id, idempotency_key)` (and, for a rejecting T1, the fence by `(owner_id, idempotency_key, create_fingerprint)` — REV8-01): row present → continue at the calendar step; fence present → the fenced 409; both absent → 500 `booking_failed`; unresolvable → 503 `booking_outcome_unknown` with `retryAfterSeconds` and no token (the client retries with the same key). Calendar **definite** failure (C6.0: 4xx other than 409/412/429, `host_not_connected`) → T2′ `UPDATE latest_action='confirm', calendar_state='failed', pending_op=NULL, unresolved_inserts = unresolved_inserts || <every earlier unresolved insert attempt> WHERE … opId/gen` → `COMMIT` (REV4-03: a definite 4xx on a *retried* insert resolves only the retry; an earlier timed-out insert of the same id stays outstanding and is retained — the timeout → takeover-retry-4xx → cancel → delayed-insert interleaving is proved in AC-11(j), and the timeout → takeover-retry-**2xx** → cancel → delayed-insert one in AC-11(l), REV5-01) → email (C5, `(1, 'confirm')` — the business creation is **finalized** exactly as on the success path; only the calendar outcome differs, REV3-03) → 201 with `delivery.calendar: failed`, `delivery.email` per the send (AC-10(d)). From that persisted state (`revision=1, latest_action='confirm', calendar_state='failed', pending_op=NULL`) both notify forms are valid (`{ action:'confirm', expectedRevision:1 }` and `{}` → `(1,'confirm')`), and the calendar is repaired only by the owned C6.8 operation (same intended id), never by an unowned insert. Calendar **ambiguous** failure (C6.0: timeout, 5xx, 429, network) → `pending_op` retained, 503 `booking_outcome_unknown` with `retryAfterSeconds`; the retry reconciles via `events.get`. T2 failure after a confirmed insert → `pending_op` retained; the retry resumes at T2 (no second insert: `events.get` first). Email failure after T2 → 201 with `delivery.email: failed`.

**C6.4a Create finalization, the unfinished-create carry, and the pending-row invariants (REV14-01).** A booking row exists from create T1, but **business creation is not finalized until the create's own outcome is written**. The durable, monotonic witness of that is `latest_action`: create T1 writes it `NULL` exactly once, and the only transactions that ever write it again — create T2 (`'confirm'`), create T2′ (`'confirm'`), cancel T2 (`'cancel'`), reschedule T2 (`'reschedule'`), repair T2 (value unchanged) — all write a **non-null** value. No transaction in this PLAN ever writes `latest_action = NULL` after T1, so the column's *nullness* is monotonic even though its value is not. That is exactly the property REV9-01 needed and the equality test `latest_action = 'confirm'` lacked: **`latest_action IS NOT NULL` means “this booking's creation was finalized”**, and no later reschedule or cancel can falsify it. Three invariants follow, and AC-11 and AC-23(a) assert them:

- **(CF-1)** `calendar_state = 'pending' ⇔ latest_action IS NULL`. The two columns are written by exactly the same set of transactions; create T1 sets (`pending`, `NULL`) together and every later writer resolves both together. A finalized row therefore never reads `delivery.calendar: 'pending'`, and C3's “`pending` never appears on a create 201” is a **consequence** of the create-success predicate, not a separate rule anyone must remember.
- **(CF-2)** `pending_op IS NULL ⇒ latest_action IS NOT NULL`. **No committed row ever rests with no owner and an unfinished create.** The one transaction that could otherwise produce that state — a cancel T2′ after the cancel took over a stale create whose event was absent — is defined in C6.7 to **restore a resumable create op** instead of clearing `pending_op`.
- **(CF-3)** `unfinished_create IS NOT NULL ⇒ latest_action IS NULL`; the column is cleared to `NULL` by whichever transaction finalizes the create.

**The carry.** `bookings.unfinished_create jsonb null` (C8) holds the create operation that a **non-create op inherited but did not complete**: `{ opId, gen, eventId, startedAt, attempts }`, copied verbatim from the `pending_op` the taking-over transaction replaced. It is written in exactly **one** place — the C6.7 cancel T1 that takes over a **stale create whose event `events.get` found definitely absent** — because that is the only takeover in this PLAN that replaces a create op without completing it. C6.6's reschedule takeover never writes it: a create or repair there is *always completed, never abandoned*, and “completed” means exactly what C6.4's Resume means — event present → its T2; event **absent** → one `events.insert` under the **same** intended id → its T2 — so the create is finalized (`latest_action='confirm'`) before the reschedule's own op begins. The column is cleared by the transaction that finalizes the create: the inherited create's own T2/T2′ once a later attempt finds or lands the event, or **cancel T2**, whose commit makes the row a terminally settled business record (`status='cancelled'`, `latest_action='cancel'`, `calendar_state='deleted'`) — which is why the REV9-01/REV11-01 replay of a cancelled booking is untouched and still answers 201. It is **not** cleared by cancel T2′, which restores the create from it (C6.7).

**What the marker is for.** Two shared predicates read it and nothing else distinguishes the two cases they must tell apart — a create that was *finalized* by someone else, and a create that was merely *replaced* by an in-progress cancel: the superseded create worker's success predicate C6.3a(1)(ii), and the C6.4 L0 / C9 same-key replay. Both answer **201** only for the first and the **non-terminal** 409 `operation_in_progress` for the second, so the two can never disagree, and neither can ever be forced to emit a 201 whose `delivery.calendar` would have to read `'pending'` (C3) or whose `delivery.email` would have to be computed over a `(revision, NULL)` pair C5 cannot claim.

**C6.5 Unknown-outcome reconciliation (REV2-02).** An unknown commit outcome (SQLSTATE class `08`, socket `ECONNRESET`/`ETIMEDOUT`, or no server response) is **never** interpreted from a plain read: absence of a row or an unchanged `revision` proves nothing while the original transaction may still be in flight. The reconciler opens a **fresh connection**, sets `SET LOCAL lock_timeout = '5s'`, and reacquires the same per-host advisory lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext($hostId))`). Because the original transaction holds that lock until it commits or rolls back, a successful reacquisition **establishes that the original transaction has finished**; only then does the reconciler read (`SELECT … WHERE owner_id AND idempotency_key` for create T1, together with the `create_rejections` fence for `(owner_id, idempotency_key, create_fingerprint)` — an unknown outcome of a *rejecting* T1 resolves to the fenced 409 when the fence is present, REV8-01; `SELECT revision, status, pending_op` for T2s). If the lock cannot be reacquired (lock timeout, connection error) or the read fails, the outcome stays **unknown**: no compensation, no clearing, `pending_op` (if any) retained, log `outcome_unresolved { bookingId, opId, gen }`, respond 503 `booking_outcome_unknown` with `retryAfterSeconds`. The memory store has the same boundary (await the host mutex, then read). The fake `Queryable` models **delayed commit completion**: a `COMMIT` whose response is lost while the server-side transaction stays open for a configurable time, holding the lock, and then commits or rolls back; a reconciler that reads before that resolution must block on the lock, and a test asserts that no compensation happens during the window.

**C6.6 Reschedule.** T1 (under lock): re-read FOR UPDATE; reject unless `status='confirmed'` and `revision = expectedRevision`; `pending_op` policy: `NULL` → proceed; `kind='reschedule'`, `'create'`, or `'calendar_repair'` and stale → take over (C6.3), **bump the event version** (C6.3c), and reconcile that op **first** (complete it from observed Google state, or — reschedule only — abandon it, retaining its `fallbackEventId` in `unresolved_inserts` when outstanding per C6.3a; a create or repair is always completed, never abandoned), then decide by **what the reconciliation achieved, against what this request asks for (REV7-03)**: **(i) same-target recovery** — the reconciled op was a reschedule that **completed** (its T2 committed: `revision` advanced, `start = its newStart`, its `(revision, 'reschedule')` emails claimed by that T2) **and** its `newStart` equals this request's `start` → the completed move *is* this request: return **200** with that outcome — the current `booking` (new `revision`, `start`) and the `delivery` of that pair — exactly the response the dead worker would have returned; **no new op, no reservation, no availability check, no calendar mutation**, and C12's current-start exclusion is not consulted (the request is not "a move to the current time", it is the move that happened); **(ii) different target, reconciliation completed** — the reconciled reschedule completed to a start **other than** this request's `start` → 409 `booking_changed` carrying the current `booking`: the reconciliation stands (it is committed and observed at Google), the caller's `expectedRevision` predates a move the guest has not seen, and the page reloads and re-picks per C12 — no new op is started under a revision the caller did not see; **(iii) proceed as a new op** — the reconciled op was **abandoned** (a reschedule with nothing achieved; `revision` unchanged) or was a create/repair (whose completion changes neither `start` nor `revision`) → continue with this reschedule as a new op under the caller's `expectedRevision`. A same-target request whose op was abandoned therefore succeeds through a fresh op (iii) and one whose op completed succeeds through recovery (i); a move that happened is never answered `slot_unavailable` (AC-18(vii), AC-21(k)); not stale → 409 `operation_in_progress` (`retryAfterSeconds` = remaining stale window); `kind='cancel'` → 409 `operation_in_progress` regardless of age (a stale cancel is resumed only by a cancel call). Availability check for the new slot: first against the external busy set X of **R0** (REV6-03 — read *before* this T1 opened, outside any transaction: `events.list` over `[newStart, newEnd)` per C7 minus this booking's own managed event; fail-closed → 503 `availability_unknown` before T1, nothing written; `[newStart, newEnd)` intersecting X → 409 `slot_unavailable`, nothing written, no op, no reservation), then over C6.1 occupancy excluding this booking's own occupancy, with `start` validated as a candidate of the **reschedule slot increment** generator (C12, REV3-08 — the same generator the booking availability route uses, so every offered slot is accepted and nothing else is); `UPDATE pending_op = {kind:'reschedule', opId, gen:1, newStart, newEnd, oldEventId: google_event_id, fallbackEventId: intendedId, etag: google_event_etag, attempts: [], startedAt}, reserved_start=$newStart, reserved_end=$newEnd WHERE id AND revision=$r AND status='confirmed'` → `COMMIT`. Calendar: attempt write → `events.patch(oldEventId, newTime, If-Match: pending_op.etag)` (412 → C6.3c reconciliation, never a blind retry); on 404/410 → attempt write (doubles as the ownership re-check, C6.3) → `events.insert(id=fallbackEventId)` (outcome decided by `events.get`, C6.2; a zero-row T2 → superseded, C6.3a). T2 (under lock): `UPDATE start, "end", rescheduled_from, google_event_id=$resultingId, google_event_etag=$etag, calendar_state='created', revision=revision+1, latest_action='reschedule', pending_op=NULL, reserved_start=NULL, reserved_end=NULL, unresolved_inserts = unresolved_inserts || <every unresolved insert attempt of the op> WHERE id AND revision=$r AND pending_op->>'opId'=$opId AND (pending_op->>'gen')::int=$gen` → `COMMIT` → email (`action=reschedule`, new revision). When `$resultingId` is the fresh `fallbackEventId`, the former `google_event_id` is **retired** by this commit and any entry naming it becomes reap-eligible (C6.3a, REV5-02). Failures: patch/insert **definite** failure (C6.0) → T2′ (conditioned on opId/gen) clears `pending_op` and the reservation (retaining per C6.3b the id of any earlier `unresolved` insert attempt), booking unchanged, 502 `calendar_patch_failed` (retryable, no email) — a *definite* 4xx marks only that attempt `rejected`; an **ambiguous** outcome leaves its attempt `unresolved`, and any later abandonment retains its id (C6.3a); **ambiguous** calendar failure (C6.0, incl. 429) → `pending_op` and reservation retained, 503 `booking_outcome_unknown`; the retry reconciles with `events.get`. T2 definite failure → compensate **only after** an attempt write under lock re-verifies ownership (`patch` back to the old time with `If-Match: <the etag this worker's own patch returned>`, or `delete(fallbackEventId, If-Match: <the insert's etag>)`); 412 → zero further mutations, superseded path (C6.3c); `pending_op` stays for resume, 500 `reschedule_failed`. T2 unknown → C6.5 (reacquire lock, then read `revision`): advanced → success path; not advanced and still owner → compensate as above; superseded → C6.3. Email failure after T2 → 200 with `delivery.email: failed`; retry via notify route.

**C6.7 Cancel — one policy (REV2-05).** Cancel **never runs concurrently with a reschedule of the same booking and never undoes one.** T1 (under lock): re-read FOR UPDATE; reject unless `status='confirmed'` and `revision = expectedRevision` (409 `booking_changed` if a reschedule advanced `revision` — the page reloads and the guest cancels the new time). `pending_op` policy: `NULL` → proceed; `kind='cancel'` → resume (take over if stale, else 409 `operation_in_progress`); `kind='reschedule'`, `'create'`, or `'calendar_repair'` **not stale** → **refuse** with 409 `operation_in_progress` and `retryAfterSeconds` — the token page waits and retries, it does not race; **stale** → **take over the reschedule/create/repair first** (C6.3), **bump the version of every event that exists** (C6.3c: `oldEventId`, and `fallbackEventId`/`eventId` when present), then from `events.get(oldEventId)` / `events.get(fallbackEventId)` / `events.get(eventId)` determine what was achieved — a moved or fallback-inserted event means the reschedule is completed via its own T2 (revision+1, `google_event_id` = resulting id); a create/repair whose event is present is completed via its T2; nothing achieved means the reschedule is abandoned via T2′ (reservation released) with its outstanding `fallbackEventId` **retained** in `unresolved_inserts` (C6.3a), and a create/repair whose event is absent is **not** abandoned: the cancel retains `eventId` (a paused inserter that lands later is reaped, never freed) and proceeds with the cancel against the absent id — and, because replacing a **create** that way leaves that create's business outcome **unfinished** (`latest_action` stays `NULL`, `calendar_state` stays `pending`, CF-1), the same T1 **carries it durably on the row**: `unfinished_create = { opId, gen, eventId, startedAt, attempts }`, copied verbatim from the create `pending_op` this cancel is replacing (C6.4a, REV14-01), so the create survives the replacement of `pending_op` and is not lost if this cancel never commits a T2. A **repair** whose event is absent writes nothing there: a repair is begun only on a row whose create already finalized (`calendar_state='failed'`, C6.8), so `unfinished_create` is and stays `NULL` on that path — and only then begin the cancel against the **resulting** event id under the new revision. `UPDATE pending_op = {kind:'cancel', opId, gen:1, oldEventId: google_event_id, etag: google_event_etag, attempts: [], startedAt} WHERE id AND revision=$r AND status='confirmed'` → `COMMIT`. Status stays `confirmed` and occupancy stays held until the event is gone. Calendar: **first the reap and the outstanding-insert check** (C6.3a/C6.3b: the bounded, fair reap batch over the row's **retired** ids, and `events.get` on **every** intended id of the op it took over — deleting with `If-Match` anything present and retiring the attributed attempt; when an `unresolved_inserts` entry names the live id, the delete step below is preceded by `events.get(oldEventId)` so that deletion is attributed too (REV6-01); the live id, **also when `calendar_state='failed'`** and an earlier insert of it may still execute, is handled by the delete step itself and retired by T2), then attempt write → `events.delete(oldEventId, If-Match: pending_op.etag)` (404/410 tolerated; 412 → C6.3c reconciliation). T2 (under lock): `UPDATE status='cancelled', calendar_state='deleted', revision=revision+1, latest_action='cancel', pending_op=NULL, unfinished_create=NULL, reserved_start=NULL, reserved_end=NULL, unresolved_inserts = unresolved_inserts || <every unresolved insert attempt of the op and of any op it took over> WHERE id AND revision=$r AND pending_op->>'opId'=$opId AND (pending_op->>'gen')::int=$gen` → `COMMIT` → email (`action=cancel`). **Cancel T2 finalizes any create it inherited unfinished (REV14-01):** it writes a non-null `latest_action` and clears `unfinished_create`, so the row becomes a terminally settled business record and every later same-key replay or delayed create worker answers **201** with the cancelled envelope by C6.3a(1)(ii) — the REV9-01/REV11-01 cancelled-booking replay behaviour is unchanged and is proved unchanged in AC-11(n)(r-iii)(r-v). Cancel T2 **retires** `google_event_id` (the row is no longer `confirmed`), so every entry naming it — e.g. the timed-out first insert of a create whose retry applied, AC-11(l) — is reap-eligible from this commit (REV5-01). A cancel T2 is permitted **only** when, in this attempt, every intended id of the op it took over (at most three: `oldEventId`, and `fallbackEventId`/`eventId`) received a **definite** `events.get` answer, every present one of those received a definite delete outcome (2xx/404/410), the live id's delete was definite, and every version bump it owed was definite. **The reap of retired ids is not a T2 precondition (REV6-02):** it runs before the delete step as the bounded, fair C6.3a batch (≤ `REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST` ids, `inspectSeq` stamped from the row's monotonic `reap_cursor`, REV13-01), its ambiguous outcomes block nothing, and every entry it did not retire is carried into T2's `unresolved_inserts` unchanged — a retired id can never be adopted (retired is absorbing, REV5-02) and any landing under it is reaped by a later observing call, so inspecting all of them before T2 buys no safety and would make a row with more than five retained ids uncancellable in one attempt. A cancel of a row with six retained ids therefore completes in one attempt (≤ 5 reap gets + ≤ 3 inherited gets + 1 delete) and the sixth id is inspected by the next observing call (AC-11(p)); an **ambiguous** get/delete/bump leaves `pending_op` (kind `cancel`, carrying the inherited ids and the attempt ledger) and returns 503 `booking_outcome_unknown`. Ids that were definitely **absent** do not block T2 — T2 retains them in `unresolved_inserts` and the reap handles a later landing (C6.3a). Failures: delete definite failure (C6.0) → T2′ (opId/gen), booking still confirmed, 502 `calendar_delete_failed` (retryable). **Cancel T2′ recovery (REV14-01):** when `unfinished_create IS NULL` — nothing was inherited unfinished, or what was inherited the takeover completed — T2′ clears `pending_op` to `NULL` exactly as before, and the row is a finalized booking that is still `confirmed`. When `unfinished_create` is **set**, T2′ may **not** leave the row ownerless with an unfinished create (CF-2); in the **same transaction** it **restores the inherited create as a resumable op**: `pending_op = {kind:'create', opId: uc.opId, gen: <the highest gen this row has carried for that opId> + 1, eventId: uc.eventId, startedAt: uc.startedAt, attempts: uc.attempts ‖ <every unresolved insert attempt of the cancel op>}`, leaving `unfinished_create` set, `latest_action` `NULL`, `calendar_state` `pending`, `status` `confirmed`, `revision`, and the reservation unchanged. `startedAt` is the **original create's**, already older than the 2-minute stale window, so the restored op is **immediately takeable** — nobody waits on a worker that no longer exists — while the bumped `gen` invalidates the original create worker's ownership (C6.3c), so that worker can only reconcile (C6.3a(1)), never complete under its old generation. The next create replay resumes it through the ordinary C6.4 L0 stale-create path (`events.get(eventId)` → present → T2; absent → insert under the **same** id → T2) and finalizes the booking; a second **cancel** call instead re-takes it over by the same C6.7 rule and cancels normally. What T2′ must never produce is the state the rev-14 wording allowed — `confirmed`, `calendar_state='pending'`, `latest_action=NULL`, `pending_op=NULL` — for which C6.4 L0 would have had to answer 201 with `delivery.calendar: 'pending'` (forbidden by C3), C5 could claim no notification, and C6.8 repair was unreachable (repair runs only from `failed`), leaving the row permanently unresolvable; ambiguous (C6.0, incl. 429) → retained, 503 `booking_outcome_unknown`. T2 definite/unknown failure after a successful delete → `pending_op` stays; the next cancel call resumes at T2 (delete of an already-deleted event is a tolerated 404), so the retry response is 200 `cancelled`. Consequently a reschedule that reads `pending_op.kind='cancel'` is refused with 409 `operation_in_progress`, a reschedule whose T2 finds its op taken over by a cancel returns 409 `operation_superseded` carrying the current booking (even when the takeover completed its move before cancelling — the row is now `cancelled`, which is not the outcome the reschedule was reaching for), and **no replacement event is ever inserted for a booking under cancellation or by a superseded worker** (REV1-08, REV2-04, REV2-05) — and a superseded worker's insert that *does* land at Google lands under a retained id: it is external busy from the moment it is visible and is deleted by the next call that observes it, never offered as free time (C6.3a, REV4-01); its delayed patch, compensating patch, or delete meets a bumped version and gets 412 (C6.3c, REV4-02).

**C6.8 `calendar_state` transitions and the calendar-repair operation (REV3-02).** `calendar_state ∈ {pending, created, failed, deleted}` (schema `CHECK`), written only by the lifecycle transactions below — every lifecycle outcome names its transition:

| Transaction | `calendar_state` after |
|---|---|
| create T1 | `pending` |
| create T2 (event present) | `created` |
| create T2′ (definite insert failure) | `failed` |
| reschedule T1 | unchanged |
| reschedule T2 (patched or fallback-inserted) | `created` |
| reschedule T2′ (abandoned / definite failure) | unchanged (still `created` or `failed`) |
| cancel T2 | `deleted` (also when the delete was a tolerated 404; unresolved attempts are retained in `unresolved_inserts` and the live id is retired, C6.3a) |
| cancel T2′ (definite delete failure) | unchanged (`created`, `failed`, or — when the cancel had taken over a create whose event was absent — `pending`, with that create restored as a resumable `pending_op`: C6.7, CF-2, REV14-01) |
| repair T2 | `created` |

`delivery.calendar` in every envelope mirrors this column (`pending`/`created`/`failed`/`deleted`; `skipped` only when the calendar adapter is absent in memory mode). The `pending` row of the table above is observable on the read surfaces only and renders per C3/C12 as a status line with no control (REV13-02). **Repair** is an explicit owned operation and runs in **N-2** of the C5 notify ordering, after N-1's lifecycle recovery and before N-3's claim (REV15-01): `POST /api/bookings/{id}/notify` (either form), when the FOR UPDATE read under the host lock shows `status='confirmed' AND calendar_state='failed' AND pending_op IS NULL`, writes `pending_op = {kind:'calendar_repair', opId, gen:1, eventId: google_event_id, attempts: [], startedAt}` (conditioned on `revision` and `status='confirmed'`) → `COMMIT`; then `events.get(eventId)` → present → repair T2; absent → attempt write → `events.insert(id=eventId)` (outcome by `events.get`, C6.2; a zero-row T2 → superseded); repair T2 (under lock): `UPDATE calendar_state='created', google_event_etag=$etag, pending_op=NULL, unresolved_inserts = unresolved_inserts || <every unresolved insert attempt of the op> WHERE id AND revision=$r AND status='confirmed' AND pending_op->>'opId'=$opId AND (pending_op->>'gen')::int=$gen` → `COMMIT` — repair **removes nothing** from `unresolved_inserts`: an entry naming the live id is simply not reap-eligible (C6.3a), the id was never retired, and because a retired id is never promoted back to live, no reaper that read the row before, during, or after the repair can delete the repaired event (REV5-02; AC-23(j)(k)); then the email claim proceeds for the current `(revision, latest_action)`. Definite insert failure (C6.0) → T2′ clears `pending_op` (retaining any earlier `unresolved` attempt's id, C6.3b), `calendar_state` stays `failed`, `delivery.calendar: failed`, emails still retried; ambiguous insert (C6.0, incl. 429) → `pending_op` retained, 503 `booking_outcome_unknown`. **Repair T2 recovery (REV4-04):** T2 definite failure → `pending_op` retained, **no compensation** (the inserted event is the booking's intended event), 500 `repair_failed`, nothing sent; T2 unknown → C6.5; in both cases the next notify — from any isolate — resumes at `events.get(eventId)` → present → T2 (zero inserts). Repair **never** runs on a cancelled row (the read shows `status='cancelled'` → the C6.3a **reap** of that row's retired ids, which is cleanup, not repair, then the email retry; zero `events.insert`/`events.patch` — REV5-04). When notify's read shows a non-null `pending_op`: `kind='create'` → resume it per C6.4 (non-stale → 409 `operation_in_progress`, stale → take over and complete) — **this is N-1 of the C5 notify ordering, which runs before any notification claim and therefore before N-3's NULL-pair check, so a create restored by C6.7's cancel T2′ (CF-2) is taken over and finalized here rather than refused `stale_revision` (REV15-01)**; **`kind='calendar_repair'`** → non-stale → 409 `operation_in_progress` with `retryAfterSeconds`; **stale → notify takes it over** under the C6.3 rules (`gen+1`, conditioned on opId/gen; zero rows → 409 `operation_in_progress`) and resumes it at the observed step — `events.get(eventId)` → present → repair T2; absent → attempt write → `events.insert(id=eventId)` → repair T2 — then proceeds to the email claim (REV4-04: a repair whose insert timed out or whose worker died is unblocked by the next notify, without a reschedule or cancel); `kind='reschedule'` or `'cancel'`, stale or not → 409 `operation_in_progress` with `retryAfterSeconds`, nothing sent (those are taken over only by lifecycle calls). Repair is itself subject to takeover by reschedule/cancel/notify (C6.6/C6.7/above: a stale repair is completed from `events.get`, never abandoned; a cancel taking over a repair retains `eventId` if absent, C6.3a). A reschedule of a booking with `calendar_state='failed'` needs no repair first: its patch 404s and the fallback insert creates the event (T2 → `created`).

**C6.9 Group and collective event types — memory-only fixtures; refused on the live path (Product bar lock rev 11; supersedes the rev-10 memory-mode collective boundary of REV9-02; closes REV10-01 and REV10-03 by scope-out).** The existing `group` (capacity-N — `lib/availability/event-type.ts`, `tests/group.test.ts`, `tests/group-route.test.ts`) and `collective` (`hostIds` — `tests/collective.test.ts`, `tests/collective-route.test.ts`) event types are **not part of LIVE-INT**. **Memory mode (`DATABASE_URL` unset):** both kinds keep their **existing** code paths and semantics exactly as the existing tests define them — the group same-type exclusion and exact-start capacity count in `lib/booking/booking.ts`, the collective `extraBusyForHosts`/`calendarIdsForHosts` read and the existing lock helper — as **fixtures outside the C6 contract**: no C6.1 occupancy rule, C12 reschedule grid, takeover, reap, or multi-host lock order is specified, promised, or proved for a group or collective row, and no AC-4/AC-8/AC-18 interleaving involves one. The builder may route them through the shared memory create only if the existing tests stay green **unchanged**; it must not extend the C6 guarantees to them, and it must not put them under live Calendar or email (memory mode has no live adapters — AC-6). **Pg/live mode (`DATABASE_URL` set):** the catalog carries no `group` or `collective` rows — the migration seed and sign-in materialization write `kind='one_on_one'` only (C1, C8) — so the production resolution path for such a slug is the ordinary **404 `event_type_not_found`** after the read-only owner/event-type catalog `SELECT`s (C2). **Catalog resolution is distinguished from booking side effects (REV10-03):** the owner-scoped and legacy availability and booking routes, and every `/b/{token}` lifecycle route, may run the read-only owner, event-type, and schedule lookups (`resolveEventType`; `SELECT` on `owners`/`event_types`/`availability_schedules`; for `/b/{token}` also the token lookup on `bookings`) — those are catalog reads, not side effects, and the rev-10 "zero `SELECT`s" wording is withdrawn as unsatisfiable on the production path. Immediately after that resolution and **before** any advisory lock, any `bookings`/`create_rejections`/`notification_deliveries` write, any `FOR UPDATE` read, L0/R0, `freeBusy`, `events.list`, calendar mutation, or email, a resolved `kind` of `group` → **501 `group_not_supported`**, `collective` → **501 `collective_not_supported`**: zero locks, zero writes, zero Google or Gmail calls, no fence (the key is not consumed — non-terminal for C9; the client shows the error and retains its record). Because no such row can exist in pg, the 501 branch is reached only through a **fixture-resolution path** that tests exercise explicitly — the pg fake's catalog pre-populated with a `group`/`collective` row directly, bypassing seed and materialization — and AC-24 proves both the 404 production path and the 501 fixture path with the exact SQL each performs. `/b/{token}` never meets a group/collective row in pg mode (none can be created), so its 501 branch is defensive only. README lists group and collective as memory-mode fixtures outside the live product.

### C7. Calendar busy classification (REV1-03, REV1-04, REV3-10)

- External busy for the host's **primary** calendar is computed from `events.list` (`calendarId=primary`, `timeMin/timeMax` = the availability window, `singleEvents=true` so recurring series are expanded, `showDeleted=false`, `maxResults=250`, and a `pageToken` loop until `nextPageToken` is absent). An item is **managed** iff `extendedProperties.private.marloBookingId` is present (set on every Marlo insert/patch). A managed item is excluded from external busy **only when the store accounts for it** (REV3-10): its `marloBookingId` resolves to a row of this host and the item's `[start, end)` equals that row's current `[start, end)` or its `[reserved_start, reserved_end)` (the row's committed or reserved occupancy, which C6.1 already counts). A managed item that is **unmatched** (no such row, a cancelled row) or **displaced** (a live event the host moved by hand in Google Calendar, so its interval matches neither the row's interval nor its reservation) is treated conservatively as **external busy** at its actual interval — the store's own occupancy for that row is still counted too, so a host-moved event blocks both its old and its new interval until the host fixes it; no calendar-to-store synchronization is attempted (Non-goals). **Reap on observation (REV4-01, REV5-01/02):** when the item's `id` is a **retired** id of its row (named by an `unresolved_inserts` entry and not the row's live `google_event_id` — a late-landed insert of a superseded or timed-out attempt), the availability computation runs the C6.3a reap for it — at most `REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST = 5` deletes per request, chosen in C6.3a's fair order with `inspectSeq` stamped under the lock from the row's monotonic `reap_cursor` for the chosen ids (REV6-02, REV13-01), each `If-Match: item.etag`, each retiring **the entry whose `attemptId` equals the item's `marloAttemptId`** on 2xx/404/410 (idempotent; REV6-01) — and the item is **external busy for this request regardless** of the reap's outcome; the next request no longer sees it. An item under a confirmed row's live id is never reaped here, even when an `unresolved_inserts` entry names that id: it is *matched* (excluded) when its interval equals the row's, *displaced* (busy) otherwise. **Transient overlap (REV5-03):** a late-landed item may overlap a *different* confirmed row's matched event (it landed after its own row was cancelled and the interval re-booked); classification then yields external busy for the late item (the interval is not offered) and matched for the live one, the reap removes the late item, and the live booking is untouched. Cancelled items (`status='cancelled'`) are never busy. Every other item with `status != 'cancelled'` and `transparency != 'transparent'` is external busy; `dateTime` bounds are used as-is, all-day `date` bounds are converted in the host's timezone.
- `freeBusy.query` remains the read path for the existing multi-host (collective) availability in `lib/calendar/google-freebusy.ts` (memory-mode collective fixture only — in pg/live mode a `group`/`collective` kind is refused after catalog resolution and before any read, C6.9), and its fail-closed handling (AC-7) stays. Therefore the consent scope set is **exactly** `openid email profile https://www.googleapis.com/auth/calendar.freebusy https://www.googleapis.com/auth/calendar.events https://www.googleapis.com/auth/gmail.send` with `access_type=offline`, `prompt=consent` (REV1-04).
- Both `events.list` and `freeBusy` are fail-closed: non-2xx, a page that cannot be parsed, an item missing `start`/`end`, or a pagination loop that errors mid-way → `AvailabilityUnknown` → 503 `availability_unknown`.

### C8. Schema and migrations (REV1-11)

`sql/001_init.sql` is the complete initial schema for an **empty** database: `schema_migrations(version pk, applied_at)`, `owners(id pk, slug unique, first_name, email unique, calendar_id, created_at)`, `host_tokens(owner_id pk fk owners, refresh_token_enc bytea, updated_at)`, `event_types(id pk, owner_id fk, slug, kind, duration_min, capacity, notification_mode, schedule_id, unique(owner_id, slug))`, `availability_schedules(id pk, owner_id fk, timezone, rules jsonb)`, `bookings(id pk, token unique not null, idempotency_key not null, create_fingerprint text not null, owner_id fk, event_type_id fk, host_id, start timestamptz, "end" timestamptz, status, revision int default 1, latest_action, google_event_id, calendar_state not null default 'pending' check (calendar_state in ('pending','created','failed','deleted')), rescheduled_from timestamptz, reserved_start timestamptz, reserved_end timestamptz, pending_op jsonb, unfinished_create jsonb (C6.4a — the create op a non-create op inherited but did not complete: `{opId, gen, eventId, startedAt, attempts}`; `null` on every finalized row, REV14-01), unresolved_inserts jsonb not null default '[]' (array of `{attemptId, eventId, opId, gen, issuedAt, inspectSeq, inspectedAt}`, C6.3a; both `null` until first inspected, REV6-02), reap_cursor bigint not null default 0 (the row's monotonic reap-traversal cursor; every `inspectSeq` is drawn from it under the host lock and is the reap's only ordering key, so the traversal never depends on the clock — REV13-01), google_event_etag text, invitee_name, invitee_email, notes, metadata jsonb, created_at, unique(owner_id, idempotency_key), check (id <> token))` with indexes `bookings(host_id, start)`, `bookings(host_id, reserved_start) where reserved_start is not null`, `bookings(event_type_id, start) where status='confirmed'`, `bookings(token)`; `create_rejections(owner_id fk owners, idempotency_key text not null, create_fingerprint text not null, reason text not null check (reason in ('slot_unavailable','session_full')), rejected_at timestamptz not null default now(), primary key (owner_id, idempotency_key, create_fingerprint))` — the C6.4 terminal-rejection fence (REV8-01; rows are never expired, exactly like idempotency keys); `notification_deliveries` per C5 (`attempts` doubles as the claim generation); and a seed `INSERT` for owner `demo` + its event type/schedule under the C1 ids. `scripts/migrate.cjs` (`npm run db:migrate`) applies `sql/*.sql` in filename order inside one transaction each, skipping versions already in `schema_migrations`. The pg store checks `schema_migrations` on first use and refuses to serve with `store_not_migrated` when the latest `sql/` version is missing; `/api/health` reports `schema` from its **own** bounded readiness query on every request (C13, REV9-03) — it never reads the store's cached observation and the store never reads health's. `lib/db/schema-version.ts` exports `LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION`, shared by `scripts/migrate.cjs`, the store guard, and health, and a unit test asserts it equals the highest `sql/*.sql` version, so nothing reads `sql/` at runtime. README orders it: set `DATABASE_URL` → `npm run db:migrate` → set `LIVE_*`. `event_types.kind` and `capacity` are catalog data only: the seed and materialization write `kind='one_on_one'`, no pg code path implements capacity sharing or host assignment, and `create_rejections.reason` keeps `session_full` in its `CHECK` for wire compatibility although pg mode never writes it (C6.9, Product bar lock).

### C9. Create idempotency key (REV2-09, REV3-04, REV3-05, REV3-06, REV8-01, REV9-01, REV14-01, REV15-02, REV15-03)

- The **client** owns the key **and the payload it was minted for**. `booking-form-store` generates one uuid (`crypto.randomUUID()`) when the guest starts a submission and persists an **unresolved-submission record** `{ key, payload: { ownerSlug, eventSlug, start, invitee: { name, email }, notes? }, startedAt }` in `sessionStorage` under `marlo:submission:{ownerSlug}/{eventSlug}` *before* the request is sent. Every replay sends the **stored** payload with the stored key, never the current form state.
- **Terminal vs. non-terminal responses (REV3-04).** The record is cleared **only** on: 201 (recovered or first-time success — the client navigates to `/b/{token}`); 400 (request rejected before any store write); 409 `slot_unavailable` / 409 `session_full` (the C9 lookup runs first under the lock, so either code proves no row exists for this key — **and, because the server fences the rejection durably under `(owner_id, key, fingerprint)` in the same locked transaction (C6.4, REV8-01), none can ever be inserted for this key and payload by an attempt still executing on the server**: clearing the record and minting a new key for the next submission cannot leave an earlier attempt free to commit later); 422 `idempotency_key_reused` (a row exists but is not this submission's — cannot happen for a stored-payload replay; treated as terminal, new key). The record is **retained** and the same key + payload are replayed on: 409 `operation_in_progress` (the original create is still active on another worker — **non-terminal**; the client waits `retryAfterSeconds` and replays, automatically once, then via "Try again"), 409 `operation_superseded` on a create (the defensive C6.3a(1) branch — non-terminal; the replay resolves through the C9 lookup, REV9-01), 501 `collective_not_supported` / `group_not_supported` (no key was consumed and nothing was written, C6.9 — shown as an error, record retained; REV9-02, rev 11), 503 `booking_outcome_unknown`, any 5xx, a network error, and a page reload. A new key is minted only after a terminal response, or when the guest changes slot/invitee **and no unresolved record exists** (an unresolved record must be resolved or explicitly discarded first — see recovery).
- **Late responses for a superseded key (REV8-01).** Every request the store issues is tagged with the key it carried. A response or error that arrives for a key **other than the current unresolved record's key** — the record was cleared by a terminal response, replaced by a later submission's key, or explicitly discarded — is **dropped**: it never navigates, never clears or retains the current record, never refreshes availability, and is logged `stale_response_ignored { key, status }`. This is the client half of the fence: a first attempt that timed out on the client, was replayed under the same key, and whose replay's terminal 409 cleared the record, may still be executing on the server; there it is fenced (409, nothing written), and here whatever it eventually returns is ignored. The no-key-consumption contract is therefore stated precisely: a terminal 409 consumes nothing the client can reuse (no row, no op, no token) but **does** consume the `(key, payload)` pair on the server, which is exactly why the client must mint a new key and never replay the old pair.
- **Reload recovery (REV3-06).** On construction, `BookingFormStore` checks for an unresolved record for its `{ownerSlug, eventSlug}` **before** it loads availability: if one exists it enters `recovering` (form hidden behind "Finishing your booking…") and calls `client.recoverBooking({ key, payload })` — the same `POST` with the same header, but through a client entry point that **bypasses** the new-submission gates: no elapsed-start check (`lib/api/client.ts` `createBooking` keeps its check for new submissions), no requirement that `start` still appears in availability, no dependence on `selectedStart/name/email` (the constructor's reset of those fields is unchanged). Outcomes follow the terminal/non-terminal rule above: 201 → navigate to `/b/{token}` of the original row; 409 `operation_in_progress` → wait and replay; 503/5xx/network → `recovering` shows "Try again" (replay) and "Start over" (**explicit discard** of the record, logged `submission_discarded`, then normal flow); a terminal 4xx → record cleared, one notice ("Your earlier booking didn't go through"), normal flow with availability loaded. Only after the record is resolved or discarded does the store load availability and accept a new selection. The server keeps its own gates for **new** submissions only: the elapsed-start and availability checks in the create route apply when no row exists for the key; a replay whose row exists returns that row regardless of whether its start has elapsed.
- `lib/api/client.ts` `createBooking({ ownerSlug, eventSlug, body, idempotencyKey })` and `recoverBooking({ key, payload })` both send `Idempotency-Key`; the route requires it (400 `idempotency_key_required` when missing or not a uuid) and passes it to the store.
- **The requirement is scoped to the live product's event kind (REV15-03; boundary also stated in C2 and AC-24).** C9 governs the **`one_on_one`** create path and nothing else. The header check is positioned **after** the read-only catalog resolution and **after** the C6.9 kind gate on every create entry point (owner-scoped, legacy `demo`, and the C10 link route), so a resolved `group`/`collective` kind never reaches it: in **pg/live** mode C6.9's 501 `group_not_supported` / `collective_not_supported` is returned first, and in **memory** mode the row is a C6.9 fixture explicitly outside the C6/C9 contract — it books through its **existing** fixture path with **no key, no `create_fingerprint`, no `(owner_id, idempotency_key)` record, and no `create_rejections` fence**, and no C9 guarantee (replay, recovery, fencing) is claimed or proved for it. This is an **exemption, not a helper change**: `tests/group-route.test.ts` and `tests/collective-route.test.ts` send only `content-type` and are required to pass **byte-for-byte unmodified** (AC-24(a)(f)), which is exactly the preserved fixture semantics C6.9 promises. A `one_on_one` create without the header is still 400 `idempotency_key_required`, unchanged.
- **Server side.** The store keys idempotency on `(owner_id, idempotency_key)` (unique index, C8) and persists, in the create T1 `INSERT`, an **immutable `create_fingerprint`** (REV3-05): `sha256` of the canonical JSON `{ eventTypeId, start: <original start, ISO-8601 UTC ms>, inviteeEmail: <lower-cased, trimmed> }` — never recomputed, never updated by reschedule. `createBooking` looks the key up **first**, under the host lock — the L0 lookup of C6.4, which precedes the R0 external read, so a replay of an existing key issues zero `events.list` calls and succeeds during a Calendar outage (REV7-02) — together with the `(owner_id, idempotency_key, create_fingerprint)` rejection fence (REV8-01: fenced → the recorded 409, zero Google calls, nothing written; a terminal rejection writes that fence in the rejecting T1 itself, so no later T1 for the same key and payload can insert): row present → compute the fingerprint of the *request* and compare with the stored one: **equal** → reconcile and return the **current** state of the original booking, decided by the **same shared finalization predicate** the superseded create worker uses (`createFinalized`, C6.3a(1)(ii), Approach §4 — REV15-02), in this order: (1) `pending_op.kind='create'` → resume it per C6.4 (non-stale → 409 `operation_in_progress`, stale → take over and complete, then answer from the finalized row); (2) else `latest_action IS NOT NULL` — the create is **finalized** (C6.4a), whatever `pending_op` now holds, including an open reschedule or cancel op — → 201 with the same `id`, `token`, the row's *current* `start`/`revision`/`status` — a booking rescheduled from S to T replays as 201 with `start = T`, `revision = 2`; a cancelled one replays as 201 with `status='cancelled'` — still with zero Google calls when that cancelled row retains reap-eligible ids in `unresolved_inserts`: the L0 replay is exempt from the C6.3a reap, REV11-02); (3) else — `latest_action IS NULL` with `pending_op` naming a **non-create** op (the C6.7 cancel that replaced the create) or with `unfinished_create` non-null — → **409 `operation_in_progress`**, **non-terminal**, zero Google calls, nothing written (REV14-01, AC-11(r-v)); the fourth combination, `pending_op IS NULL AND latest_action IS NULL`, is unreachable by CF-2. **Nullness, not value (REV15-02):** step (2) reads whether `latest_action` is null, never which of `confirm`/`reschedule`/`cancel` it holds — the equality test REV9-01 rejected stays rejected, and (r-ii)/(r-iii)'s intervening reschedule and cancel still replay as 201. **different** → 422 `idempotency_key_reused`, nothing written. **The same identity rule answers a superseded create worker** (C6.3a(1), REV9-01): a delayed create insert response reaches the client as the 201 the completed row would replay as, after any intervening reschedule or cancel, because both paths run the one `createFinalized` predicate — `(owner_id, idempotency_key, create_fingerprint, id)`, `latest_action IS NOT NULL`, and a cleared create op — and neither ever compares `latest_action` against a **particular value** (REV15-02: the rev-9 objection was to the equality test `= 'confirm'`, which a later lifecycle change falsifies, not to the column's monotonic nullness — C6.4a). The comparison never reads the mutable `bookings.start`. Never a second row, never a second calendar event.
- A committed-but-unreconciled outcome (T1 committed, the 201 never reached the client, the process died before the calendar step or T2) is therefore recoverable from **any** isolate and **any** later time: the retry finds the row by key, completes it, and returns the original token. The same path serves the legacy one-off link route in memory mode with the link token as the key (C10).

### C10. Legacy one-off link entry point (REV2-11)
