- `POST /api/links/{token}/bookings` and `GET /api/links/{token}/available-times` (single-use links over `event_type` or `one-off meeting` targets, `lib/availability/single-use-link.ts`, `lib/availability/one-off.ts`) are **not** part of the live product surface: links and one-off meetings remain in-memory fixtures with no durable records, and are not materialized into Postgres (Non-goals).
- **Memory mode (`DATABASE_URL` unset):** behaviour is retained for fixtures/tests, but `bookSingleUseLink` no longer owns its own `tokenLocks` boundary or calendar ordering. It resolves the target, then calls the **shared** `BookingStore.createBooking` (C6.4 two-phase create under the per-host mutex, C6.1 reservation-aware conflict check, C9 key = `link:{token}`), and consumes the link inside T2 of that create (same mutex, same versioned update; consumption is **idempotent for the same booking** — the link records `consumedByBookingId`, and re-consuming for that id is a no-op). It therefore cannot bypass a host conflict created through the owner-scoped route, cannot double-book when two link bookings for the same host interleave, and cannot leave a calendar event behind when link consumption fails (consumption failure is a T2 failure → resume, not a rollback after a Google insert). **Consumed-link replay (REV3-09):** `resolveSingleUseTarget` no longer rejects a consumed link up front for `POST`. The `POST` handler resolves the link, then always enters the shared create with key `link:{token}`; the C9 lookup finds the original row → fingerprint equal → 201 with the original booking (a lost 201 is recoverable); fingerprint different → the handler maps 422 `idempotency_key_reused` to the existing **410** `SingleUseLinkConsumedError` response so the retained fixture test (`tests/single-use-route.test.ts`: reuse with a *different* start/invitee → 410) still holds. `GET /api/links/{token}/available-times` on a consumed link stays 410 (unchanged). `listSingleUseAvailableTimes` computes over C6.1 occupancy.
- **Pg/live mode (`DATABASE_URL` set):** both routes return 501 `links_not_supported` after resolving nothing — the check is the first statement in each handler, before the link lookup, before any store query, calendar call, or email. `/api/health` is unaffected. README lists the routes as fixture-only.

### C11. Booking id / token split and migration (REV2-08)

- **Today** `Booking.id` *is* the confirmation token (`lib/api/client.ts` `mapBooking` sets `token: booking.id`; `/b/{token}` reads `getBooking(token)`; `GET /api/bookings/{id}` takes the token as `{id}`). Rev 3 splits them: `Booking { id: 'bk_' + uuid, token: 32 random bytes base64url (43 chars) }` on both stores, generated by the store at create (`id <> token` is a schema check and a store invariant).
- **Store:** `BookingStore.getById(id)` and `getByToken(token)` (`SELECT … WHERE token=$1` in pg; a `byToken` index map in memory); lifecycle methods take `id` and are called only after the route has authenticated the token against the row.
- **Server adapter (`lib/api/server.ts`):** `getBookingByToken(token)` → `store.getByToken` (never `getById`); the `/b/{token}` page passes its path segment straight through, so a booking **id** in the `/b/` path renders the not-found shell.
- **Routes:** `/api/bookings/{id}` and every `/api/bookings/{id}/*` route resolve `getById(id)`, then require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` equal (constant-time) to `row.token`; missing header → 401 `token_required`; unknown id or mismatch → 404 `booking_not_found` (uniform, no enumeration). The token is never accepted in the query string or the JSON body.
- **Client (`lib/api/client.ts`):** `PublicBooking { id, token, … }`; `mapBooking` requires both `booking.id` and `booking.token` as strings and maps each explicitly; `getBooking({ id, token })`, `getBookingAvailability({ id, token, timeMin, timeMax })`, `rescheduleBooking({ id, token, start, expectedRevision })`, `cancelBooking({ id, token, expectedRevision })`, `retryNotification({ id, token, action?, expectedRevision? })` all set the bearer header. The confirmation navigation carries only the token (`/b/{token}`); the page obtains `id` from the row.
- **Fixtures/tests:** every fixture and test that built a booking with `id === token`, called `/api/bookings/{token}`, or navigated to `/b/{id}` is migrated; a grep-style test asserts no remaining `token: booking.id` / `getBooking(token)` in `lib/api/**` and `app/**`. `lib/api/DIVERGENCES.md` records the split.

### C12. `/b/{token}` controls and states (REV2-10, REV3-07, REV3-08, REV8-03)

- `app/(public)/b/[token]/page.tsx` renders, for a `confirmed` booking, a client component `BookingControls` with three explicit controls: **Reschedule** (opens the slot picker), **Cancel** (two-step: button → inline confirm), and **Resend confirmation** (existing AC-10 surface; sends the C5 retry-latest form when the page has no revision in hand, the revision-specific form otherwise). **Cancelled branch (REV3-07):** keeps its book-again anchor, renders **no Reschedule or Cancel control**, and renders the cancellation **delivery status** (`delivery.email` of the current revision: "Cancellation emails sent" / "…pending" / "…failed") plus a **Retry notification** control **iff** `delivery.email` is `pending` or `failed` (per C5/REV4-05 a recipient with no ledger row is `pending`, so a cancellation whose process died before any claim shows the control on a fresh load). That control is the same resend surface (`retryNotification`): `{ action: 'cancel', expectedRevision }` right after an in-page cancel, `{}` after a reload; the server retries only the cancel emails and never inserts anything (C5, C6.8). This holds both for the immediate post-cancel render (the `done` state below) and for a fresh load of `/b/{token}` — there is no background delivery worker (Non-goals), so this control is the only retry path and must exist.
- **Calendar status line (REV13-02).** Both the confirmed and the cancelled branch render `delivery.calendar` as a one-line status beside the email line: `created` → nothing (the silent default), `pending` → "Calendar invite is still being created." with **no** control, `failed` → the existing repair path (the Resend / retry-notification control, which begins the C6.8 calendar repair), `deleted`/`skipped` → nothing. `pending` never disables a control, never changes `controlsState`, and never triggers a retry or a poll — it is a read-only mirror of `calendar_state` (C3, C6.8).
- **Reschedule picker:** reuses the BOOK-FE slot-picker components, fed by `getBookingAvailability` (C2 booking availability route, which excludes the booking's own occupancy). **Reschedule slot increment (REV3-08):** the public picker's generator (`lib/availability/slots.ts`) steps by `durationMinutes`, so excluding own occupancy alone can never produce an overlapping move (a 30-min booking at 09:00 yields only 09:00 and 09:30). `generateSlots` therefore takes an explicit `incrementMinutes`; the owner-scoped/public/legacy availability routes and the create route keep `incrementMinutes = durationMinutes` (BOOK-FE behaviour and its tests unchanged), while the **authenticated reschedule candidate policy** uses `RESCHEDULE_SLOT_INCREMENT_MIN = 15` (constant in `lib/availability/slots.ts`; `min(15, durationMinutes)` for shorter meetings) — applied identically by `GET /api/bookings/{id}/available-times` and by the reschedule mutation's validation (C6.6), which accepts `start` iff it is a candidate of that generator over the C6.1 occupancy minus own occupancy. A 30-min booking at 09:00 under the normal fixture schedule is thus offered 09:15 (overlapping) and 09:30 (adjacent) among others; the booking's **current** start is excluded from candidates by both the route and the mutation (an unchanged-time reschedule is 409 `slot_unavailable`, never a calendar call). README documents the 15-minute reschedule grid. Selecting a slot calls `rescheduleBooking` with `expectedRevision = booking.revision`.
- **States** (a discriminated `controlsState`): `idle` · `pending` (an operation is in flight or the server answered 409 `operation_in_progress`: controls disabled, "Working on it…", automatic single retry after `retryAfterSeconds`; **when that single automatic retry is also answered 409 `operation_in_progress` — another caller may have taken the stale op over immediately before the retry and renewed its window — the page leaves `pending` for `stalled`; it never stays in `pending` with nothing in flight and no retry left, REV8-03**) · `stalled` (`{ request, retryAfterSeconds }` — **no request in flight, no further automatic retry**: "Working on it…" is replaced by "Another change to this booking is still finishing." and two explicit actions: **Try again** re-sends the *same* request — same route, same body, same `start`, same `expectedRevision` — as a manual retry and re-enters `pending` with one fresh automatic retry (a booking changed meanwhile is reconciled by the existing `conflict` handling: 409 `booking_changed` → reload → "changed elsewhere" → re-pick/re-confirm; the page never silently substitutes a newer revision into a request the guest did not re-confirm), and **Reload** calls `getBooking`, re-renders from the returned `booking`, and returns to `idle` with the fresh `revision`; Reschedule, Cancel, and Resend stay disabled in `stalled` until one of the two actions resolves it, so the guest always has exactly one way forward and the page can never remain disabled with nothing in flight) · `conflict` (409 `slot_unavailable`/`session_full` → picker refreshes availability and shows the recovery copy; 409 `booking_changed`/`operation_superseded`/`stale_revision` → reloads the booking via `getBooking` and shows "This booking changed elsewhere — showing the latest") · `error` (5xx, 502 `calendar_*_failed`, network → "Try again" keeps the same `expectedRevision`; 503 `booking_outcome_unknown` → "We're not sure that went through" with a reload action that re-reads the booking rather than re-submitting) · `done` (200 → the page re-renders from the returned `booking`, incl. the new time or the cancelled panel, and shows the `delivery.email` outcome with the resend / retry-notification control if not `sent` — on the cancelled panel too, REV3-07).
- All controls are proven **through the real handlers** via `handler-transport` (no mocked client): the tests drive the React components against the actual `app/api/bookings/[id]/*` routes with fake adapters and the lock-queue fake DB.

### C13. `/api/health` contract (REV9-03; revises AC-15's "without touching the network")

- **Envelope:** `{ ok, sha, store: "memory" | "pg", schema: "current" | "behind" | "missing" | "unknown" | "n/a", db: "ok" | "unreachable" | "n/a", calendar: "mock" | "live", email: "mock" | "live" }`. `ok` is true **iff** `schema ∈ {current, n/a}`; HTTP **200** iff `ok`, otherwise HTTP **503** with the same envelope — an unmigrated, behind, or unreachable database fails the post-deploy health check loudly (the automatic revert in CLAUDE.md is the intended consumer) instead of reporting a green deploy that `store_not_migrated` would then refuse to serve.
- **Memory mode** (`DATABASE_URL` unset, or `MARLO_PROOF=1`): `store: "memory"`, `schema: "n/a"`, `db: "n/a"`; **zero I/O** of any kind.
- **Pg mode — the bounded readiness query.** The route issues **exactly one** query per request, on the same `Queryable` the store uses (`lib/db`), and derives `schema` from its result alone: `SELECT version FROM schema_migrations ORDER BY version DESC LIMIT 1`, bounded by `HEALTH_DB_TIMEOUT_MS = 2000` (a client-side deadline that abandons the query and answers; the query is also issued with `statement_timeout = 2000` as a per-query option so the server abandons it too). Top row `= LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` (C8) → `current`; a row `< LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` → `behind`; zero rows, or SQLSTATE `42P01` (`schema_migrations` does not exist — an empty database) → `missing`; any other error, connection failure, or the deadline → `schema: "unknown"`, `db: "unreachable"`. `db: "ok"` whenever the query answered.
- **Fresh isolate (the finding's case):** health is answerable as the **first request an isolate ever serves** — it does not call `assertMigrated()`, does not instantiate the store's serving guard, reads no cached observation (there is none), and caches nothing for later requests; the first and the thousandth request compute `schema` the same way. `DATABASE_URL` alone never decides `schema` — an empty database and a migrated one differ only by the query.
- **Forbidden:** no Google or Gmail call, no OAuth refresh, no `fetch` of any kind, no second query. `calendar`/`email` report **configuration** (`LIVE_CALENDAR`/`LIVE_EMAIL` via `lib/env`), never connectivity. The grep exception for `app/api/health/route.ts` covers only the SHA env names (`VERCEL_GIT_COMMIT_SHA`, `VERCEL_DEPLOYMENT_ID`); `DATABASE_URL` reaches the route only through `lib/env`/`lib/db`.
- **Offline proof (AC-6 unchanged):** the health tests run against the pg fake `Queryable`, which answers, refuses, errors, or stalls the readiness query as each case directs; the test-setup `fetch` guard stays armed throughout.

## Acceptance criteria

Every criterion is observed **only** via `PROOF_CMD` (`npm test`). No AC requires a database, Google, Gmail, or the network: live implementations are proven against injected fakes (a stateful recording `Queryable` for SQL with an explicit lock queue and configurable `COMMIT` outcomes, a fake `fetch` for Google APIs). The real-inbox / real-calendar check is the **Founder live smoke** in `## Verification`, which is manual and not graded.

| ID | Criterion | Proof (inside `npm test`) | Closes |
|---|---|---|---|
| AC-1 | `GET /{ownerSlug}/{eventSlug}` renders the booking page for a fixture owner+event; unknown owner, or unknown event under a known owner → 404. Page title, hero heading, slot-picker intro, confirmation heading, and email subject/body all interpolate the owner's `firstName` (no hard-coded "Shahar"/"the host" in host-facing copy). `/demo/intro-30` still renders and is tagged `fixture: true` in its page data; no non-demo owner slug resolves to fixtures when `DATABASE_URL` is set. **Two owners sharing an event slug are distinct:** owners `ada` and `bob` both have `intro-30`; each page, availability call, and booking resolves to its own owner's event type, calendar id, host id, and owner email. | Render + route tests with fixture owners `ada`/`bob` (different `firstName`, calendar id, email) both offering `intro-30`: book each; assert the store rows carry the correct `ownerId`/`eventTypeId`, the calendar fake received inserts on each owner's calendar id, and the owner-recipient of each confirmation email is the respective owner. Grep-style test that `app/**` and `lib/email/**` contain no hard-coded host first name outside `fixtures/**`. | P1, REV1-01 |

| AC-11 | Create follows C6.4: **L0** (locked C9 key lookup **before any Google traffic**; an existing key validates its fingerprint and returns or resumes with zero `events.list` calls — REV7-02) → R0 (external read, **missing keys only**) → T1 (lock → the mandatory second key lookup **and rejection-fence lookup** (REV8-01) → external-busy check → reservation-aware conflict check → `INSERT` with `pending_op.create {opId, gen, eventId}` and `google_event_id = intendedId` → commit; a rejection instead writes the `create_rejections` fence in the same transaction → 409) → `events.insert(id=intendedId)` → T2 (clear `pending_op`, conditioned on opId/gen) → email. **No Google *mutation* happens before T1 commits (REV6-03) — the only Google traffic before T1 is the R0 `events.list` read (and C7's reap of *other* rows' retired ids, which never names this booking's intended id), `events.insert` for the intended id is issued only after T1's `COMMIT` — and no calendar event is ever deleted on the strength of an unknown outcome.** **Every deletion of a retained-id event retires exactly the attempt the event names (REV6-01)**, through one idempotent `retireAttempt`, so a delayed insert response or a second concurrent reaper can never retire an attempt that may still execute; **retired ids are traversed fairly** (REV6-02), so no retained id is starved by absent ones ahead of it. Both behave identically against mock and live adapters. **Cancel-vs-reschedule — one policy (C6.7):** cancel never runs while a reschedule owns `pending_op` and never undoes one. A cancel that reads a non-stale `pending_op.kind='reschedule'` is refused with 409 `operation_in_progress` + `retryAfterSeconds` and makes zero calendar calls; once the reschedule completes, the cancel deletes the **resulting** event id (patched or fallback). **variant** (B's target equals T): B `{ start: T, expectedRevision: 1 }` → completes A's op → same-target recovery → 200 at T, revision 2 (REV7-03, AC-18(vii)); A's late 2xx → **one locked read**: `status='confirmed'`, `google_event_id = F` → F **live** → **not eligible** → zero `events.delete` calls by A, no 412, no observed-etag retry, zero mutations of any kind; that same read shows exactly the outcome A sought (`status='confirmed'`, `revision` 2 advanced, `start = T` = A's `newStart`) → A reconciles as **200** with the current booking (`start = T`, `revision = 2`, `google_event_id = F`) and the `delivery` envelope of the completing `(2,'reschedule')` claim, **never** `operation_superseded` (C6.3, C6.3a(1), AC-18(iii); | AC-13 | On book, **both** invitee and owner receive a confirmation (subject and body use the host's `firstName` and the invitee's name; owner copy says who booked; both carry the `.ics`). Cancel emails use cancel-specific intro copy and **never** the confirmation intro; reschedule emails carry old and new times. **Delivery is tracked per `(revision, action, recipient)` with an atomic claim that returns a generation (C5):** `POST /api/bookings/{id}/notify` re-sends only rows not `sent` for the booking's current revision/action; two simultaneous retries produce exactly one send per recipient; every finalisation carries the claim generation and a late finaliser after a stale-claim takeover changes nothing; a revision-specific request `{ action, expectedRevision }` is validated against `(revision, latest_action)` under the claim lock and refused 409 `stale_revision` (with the current pair) when either differs; the explicit retry-latest form `{}` claims for the current pair and echoes it; a confirmation attempt that finishes after a cancel cannot mark the cancel delivery sent. A second notify after full success is a no-op 200. **Delivery status is computed against the required recipient set** (REV4-05): `sent` requires an explicit `sent` row for both invitee and owner; a recipient with no row is `pending`; notify claims and sends every required recipient that has no row, a `failed` row, or a stale claim, and never re-sends a `sent` one. **Notify on a cancelled booking never inserts or patches** (REV3-02, REV5-04): whatever `calendar_state` holds, both forms retry only the `(revision, 'cancel')` rows, and the only calendar calls are the C6.3a reap of that row's retired ids (`events.get`, and an `If-Match` `events.delete` of anything present). **In-flight sends are not ordered against lifecycle changes (REV5-05):** a send whose claim committed before a later revision was written may reach Gmail after that revision's emails; the ledger stays generation-correct, no pre-send re-validation is required, and README states the ordering **and that arrival order does not establish the booking's state — the booking page, linked from every email (C4), is the authoritative status (REV6-05)**. **The duplicate bound is stated honestly (REV6-04) and precisely (REV7-04):** `attempts` is the exact upper bound on copies per `(revision, action, recipient)`; a **`claimed`** row is taken over at most once per stale window and only on a caller's retry; a **`failed`** row is re-claimable **immediately** (the claim SQL's `failed` branch carries no age condition in either store), and `failed` is finalised only on a **definite** Gmail refusal — an ambiguous send outcome leaves the row `claimed` — so a failed generation delivered nothing and immediate failed retries add no copies; the delivery timing of stalled generations is unbounded, so copies may cluster, and no send-deadline, issuance-rate, or failed-retry rate guard is claimed. The `/b/{token}` resend control sends `expectedRevision` from the last `booking` it holds (C12). | String assertions on subject and first body line for each of confirm/reschedule/cancel × invitee/owner; negative assertion on cancel bodies. Ledger tests on both stores: (a) concurrent double retry → one send per recipient; (b) send succeeds, `sent` write fails → row stays `claimed`, immediate retry sends nothing, retry after the 2-minute stale window sends once; (c) **claim-then-stall** (REV5-05): worker A claims `(1, 'confirm')` for both recipients (claim committed) and pauses **between the claim commit and the Gmail call**; a cancel commits revision 2 and sends both cancel emails; A resumes → its two Gmail calls **are issued** with revision-1 confirmation content (the mock email adapter's send log reads `[cancel×2, confirm×2]`), its `finalize(gen 1, sent)` updates only the `(1, 'confirm')` rows, the `(2, 'cancel')` rows are untouched and `delivery.email` for the current pair stays `sent`; a subsequent `notify { action: 'confirm', expectedRevision: 1 }` → 409 `stale_revision` with `{ revision: 2, latestAction: 'cancel' }`; the README test asserts the "can occasionally arrive after a later one" sentence **and** the "does not tell you the booking's current state" sentence, and that README nowhere claims the most recent email describes the current state (REV6-05); a template test asserts each of the six bodies contains the absolute `/b/{token}` URL of its booking and the handler-transport tests assert it is built from the request origin; (d) **late finaliser**: worker A claims (gen 1) and stalls past the stale window, worker B re-claims (gen 2) and finalises `sent`; A's `finalize(gen 1, failed)` updates zero rows, logs `stale_finalize_ignored`, row stays `sent`; reverse (B still `claimed`, A late `sent` with gen 1) → row stays `claimed` under gen 2; (e) `{ action: 'confirm' }` without `expectedRevision` → 400 `notify_request_invalid`; `{}` after a reschedule → claims `(2, 'reschedule')` and the response echoes it; (f) the token page's resend after a reload (no revision in hand) sends `{}` and after an in-page reschedule sends `{ action: 'reschedule', expectedRevision: 2 }` (handler-transport); (g) **cancelled + calendar failed** (REV3-02): create with a definite calendar failure (`calendar_state='failed'`), cancel (delete 404 tolerated → `cancelled`, `calendar_state='deleted'`) with the email adapter failing; at t = 0 + 2 s → `attempts` 3 → `failed`; the send log shows three refused attempts per recipient and **zero** deliveries; the adapter is repaired → `notify {}` at t = 0 + 3 s → `attempts` 4 → `sent`; the test asserts `attempts = 4`, delivered copies = 1 ≤ 4, and that the pg fake's claim SQL matched the `state='failed'` branch without any `claimed_at` comparison; **contrast** — the adapter answers an **ambiguous** outcome (timeout) at t = 0 → the worker does **not** finalise, the row stays `claimed`, `delivery.email: pending`; `notify {}` at t = 0 + 1 s → **zero** claims, zero sends; at t = 2 min + 1 s → one claim (`attempts` 2), one send, `sent`; both branches on both stores. | AC-18 | Every booking mutation is a **versioned, owned conditional update** (`WHERE id AND revision = $expected AND status = 'confirmed'`, plus `pending_op->>'opId' = $opId AND (pending_op->>'gen')::int = $gen` on every completing/clearing/compensating update); a lost revision check yields 409 `booking_changed`; a lost ownership check makes the worker **superseded** (C6.3): it reconciles the winner and returns 200 or 409 `operation_superseded`, and never compensates, inserts, clears, or deletes a live or open-op id — the only deletion a superseded worker may ever issue is the C6.3a cleanup of its **own attributed** event under an id that one locked read shows **retired with its attempt retained**, and that eligibility is decided separately from the response and never from the row's interval (REV7-01). **A stale reschedule taken over by a reschedule is reconciled against the request (REV7-03):** a completed same-target move is returned as the request's own success (200, no second operation); a completed different-target move is 409 `booking_changed` with the current booking; an abandoned op is followed by the requested move as a new op — a move that happened is never answered `slot_unavailable`. `pending_op` (with `opId`, `gen`, and the intended event id where one may be created) is written before any calendar mutation and cleared only by the completing update; a stale `pending_op` (> 2 min) is taken over with `gen+1` per the C6.6/C6.7 policy and reconciled from observed Google state; **no abandonment or cancellation completes without a definite observation of every intended id of the taken-over op** — a present id is completed against (its unresolved attempts are still retained — observation completes ops, never attempts, REV5-01), an absent one is retained in `unresolved_inserts` for the reap, an ambiguous one keeps `pending_op` and returns 503 (C6.3a, REV4-01) — the bounded reap of already-retired ids is never such a precondition (REV6-02); every patch/delete/compensation of an existing event carries `If-Match` and a takeover bumps the event version before it proceeds, so a predecessor's delayed mutation gets 412 and reconciles instead of landing (C6.3c, REV4-02); every Google mutation is preceded by a durable attempt entry that only its own response resolves (C6.3b, REV4-03); a non-stale op of a different kind is refused with 409 `operation_in_progress` + `retryAfterSeconds`. T2 unknown outcomes are resolved only after reacquiring the host lock on a fresh connection (C6.5); compensation happens only when the worker is still the owner **and** the original transaction is known to have rolled back. Lifecycle failure responses are exactly those in C6 (`calendar_patch_failed`, `calendar_delete_failed`, `reschedule_failed`, `repair_failed`, `booking_outcome_unknown`, `operation_in_progress`, `operation_superseded`), and a cancel retry after a successful delete but failed T2 returns 200 `cancelled` without a second delete being required. | Fake-DB scenarios for reschedule: patch definite failure → 502, row unchanged, `pending_op` and reservation null; fallback insert then T2 failure → `delete(fallbackEventId)`; T2 unknown with row advanced → lock reacquired on a fresh connection first, then 200; T2 unknown with **delayed commit** (fake holds the transaction 500 ms then commits) → no compensation, 200; T2 unknown and lock reacquire times out → 503, no compensation, `pending_op` retained. **Takeover / paused worker:** worker A passes T1 and pauses; fake clock advances > 2 min; worker B (same kind) takes over (`gen` 1→2), `events.get` observed, completes T2; A resumes: (i) its patch/insert path re-checks ownership → zero `events.insert`; (ii) its T2 matches zero rows → zero compensation; (iii) its response is 200 with the completed outcome (same `newStart`) or 409 `operation_superseded` if B moved elsewhere; `events.patch` back to the old time is never called; (iv) **reschedule-over-reschedule abandonment retains** (REV4-01): A's patch 404s, A pauses after its attempt write; B (a new reschedule of the same booking, > 2 min later) takes over, bumps the version, observes nothing achieved, abandons A's op retaining A's `fallbackEventId`, then runs its own op with a **fresh** `fallbackEventId` and completes; A's late insert lands (`'land'`) → A's `events.get` shows it → A re-reads, not owner; its fallback id is **retired** (`google_event_id` is B's fresh fallback) and its attempt **retained** → eligible (REV7-01) → A deletes its own landed event (`If-Match`) and retires its entry → `operation_superseded`; under `'duplicate'` → 409 → get 404 → `operation_superseded`; exactly one live event (B's) exists for the booking in both modes; (vii) **retry after death between patch and T2** (REV7-03): A `{ start: T, expectedRevision: 1 }` patches `S→T` (etag `e2`) and dies before T2 (hook after the patch response); clock > 2 min; the guest's retry `{ start: T, expectedRevision: 1 }` → R0 over `[T, T+d)` excludes the booking's own managed event (X empty) → T1: stale reschedule → takeover (gen 2) → bump (`e2→e3`) → `events.get` shows T → A's op **completed** via T2 (`revision` 2, `start = T`, `google_event_etag = e3`, `(2,'reschedule')` claimed and sent) → **same-target recovery** → **200** with `booking.start = T`, `revision = 2`, `delivery.email: sent`; the pg fake shows **no** second `pending_op` written, **no** reservation, zero further patches/inserts, and no 409 of any kind; a second `{ start: T, expectedRevision: 2 }` → 409 `slot_unavailable` (an unchanged-time move, C12) with zero calendar calls; the client reloads (AC-21(d)) and `{ start: U, expectedRevision: 2 }` → 200 at U, `revision` 3, exactly one patch; **abandoned + same target**: A died *before* sending its patch (nothing achieved) → the retry for T takes over, bumps, observes S, abandons (T2′, reservation released) → proceeds as a new op to T → 200 at T, `revision` 2, exactly one patch; **taken over by a different kind**: the same dead-A state and a `notify {}` → 409 `operation_in_progress` (unchanged, C6.8) — same-target recovery is a reschedule-over-reschedule rule only; (v) **412 reconciliation** (REV4-02): a patch answered 412 while the worker is still owner and `events.get` shows the event at the expected pre-state with a new etag → exactly one retry with that etag (after a fresh attempt write) → 200; the event at a foreign interval → 502 `calendar_patch_failed`, reservation released, zero further patches; a 412 while not owner → `operation_superseded`, zero retries; (vi) **attempt ledger** (REV4-03): after any takeover the predecessor's `unresolved` entries are still present in `pending_op.attempts`, and the clearing update that follows copies **every** one of them into `unresolved_inserts` — an `events.get` that showed the id present completed the op and resolved no attempt (REV5-01); | AC-19 | **Create idempotency (C9).** The create route requires `Idempotency-Key`; the form persists an unresolved-submission record `{ key, payload }` before sending and **retains it on every non-terminal response** — 409 `operation_in_progress`, 503, 5xx, network error, reload — clearing it only on 201, 400, 409 `slot_unavailable`/`session_full`, or 422 (REV3-04); every replay sends the stored payload. The store keys on `(owner_id, idempotency_key)` and compares replays against the immutable `create_fingerprint` (event type, **original** start, invitee email), never against the mutable `bookings.start` (REV3-05); an equal fingerprint returns 201 with the **original** `id`/`token` and the row's **current** state (start/revision/status); a different one → 422 `idempotency_key_reused`. A same-key replay while the original create is still active (non-stale `pending_op.create` owned elsewhere) → 409 `operation_in_progress`, no write, no Google call. **A replay never enters the Calendar read path (REV7-02):** the locked key lookup (C6.4 L0) precedes the R0 `events.list` read, so a replay of a completed create or of a non-stale pending create issues **zero** Google calls and succeeds — or returns `operation_in_progress` — even while `events.list` throws; **the completed-create replay is exempt from the C6.3a reap (REV11-02):** a `cancelled` row whose `unresolved_inserts` retains reap-eligible ids still replays as 201 with zero `events.get`/`events.delete`, its entries untouched — cleanup of those ids is the job of reschedule, cancel, notify, `GET /api/bookings/{id}`, `/b/{token}`, and C7's list classification, never of the create route; a stale pending create's takeover issues only `events.get`/`events.insert` for its own intended id; only a **missing** key performs R0, followed by T1's mandatory second lookup. No second row, no second calendar event, no duplicate confirmation email beyond the C5 window. **A terminal rejection is durably fenced (REV8-01):** 409 `slot_unavailable`/`session_full` writes `create_rejections (owner_id, key, fingerprint, reason)` in the rejecting T1 under the lock, L0 and T1's second lookup both check it, and a same-key, same-payload attempt still executing on the server — one paused between R0 and T1 — is answered the same 409 from that fence and inserts nothing, so the client that cleared the key and booked another slot can never end up with two bookings; the client drops any response for a key that is no longer its unresolved record (`stale_response_ignored`). **Reload recovery (REV3-06):** a fresh `BookingFormStore` with an unresolved record replays it *before* loading availability, bypassing the elapsed-start and availability gates for that replay only, and navigates to the original `/b/{token}`; genuinely new submissions keep both gates. (d) owner sign-in in pg mode materializes only `kind='one_on_one'` rows (the materialized set contains no `group`/`collective` row) and `sql/001_init.sql`'s seed inserts none (asserted by reading the file); (e) with `DATABASE_URL` set, `/b/{token}` reschedule/cancel/notify on a booking whose fixture-injected event type is `group`/`collective` → 501 before any lock, `FOR UPDATE`, or write (defensive branch; statement log = the token lookup `SELECT` plus catalog `SELECT`s only); (f) **the header-exemption boundary is proved on both sides (REV15-03):** in **memory** mode a legacy `POST /api/event-types/{slug}/bookings` sent with **no** `Idempotency-Key` header returns **400 `idempotency_key_required`** for a `one_on_one` slug (C2/C9 unchanged) and the **existing fixture outcomes** for the `group-4` and `collective-ab` slugs (the 201s and the capacity/conflict 409s the retained tests already assert), with no `(owner_id, idempotency_key)` record, no `create_fingerprint`, and no `create_rejections` row written for either; in **pg** mode the same header-less group/collective POST is **501 `group_not_supported` / `collective_not_supported`** — never 400 — proving the kind gate precedes the header check; and the exemption is proved to be an exemption rather than a test edit by asserting `tests/group-route.test.ts` and `tests/collective-route.test.ts` are byte-for-byte identical to their pre-change contents (`git diff --exit-code -- tests/group-route.test.ts tests/collective-route.test.ts` is clean in the proof run). | REV9-02 (superseded by scope-out), REV10-01, REV10-03, REV15-03 |
| AC-25 | **Calendar HTTP 429 is ambiguous everywhere, and its attempt is retained through takeover and cancel (C6.0, REV10-02; Product bar lock §2).** One exported classifier decides every Google outcome for both adapters and every C6 step; 429 is never a definite rejection, never clears `pending_op`, never enters T2′, never releases a reservation, and never resolves an attempt. | (a) **classifier table test:** `classifyCalendarError` over `{200, 201, 204}` → `applied`; `{400, 401, 403, 404, 410, 422}` → `definite`; `409 duplicate` → `already_exists`; `412` → `precondition_failed`; `{429, 500, 502, 503, 504, timeout, ECONNRESET, malformed JSON}` → `ambiguous`; the live adapter, the mock adapter, and the fake `fetch` all import it (import-graph test: no module under `lib/booking/**` or `lib/google/**` compares a numeric status against `429`, `4xx` ranges, or `>= 400` outside `lib/google/errors.ts`); `classifyGmailError` gives 429 the same class (the C5 finalisation keeps the row `claimed` — already AC-13). (b) **create insert 429:** T1 committed → `events.insert` → 429 → attempt 1 stays `unresolved`, `pending_op` retained (`kind='create'`, `gen=1`), no T2′, `calendar_state='pending'`, 503 `booking_outcome_unknown`, zero emails; same-key replay → `events.get(eventId)` 404 → re-insert with the **same** id (attempt 2) → 2xx → T2 → 201; T2 wrote `unresolved_inserts = [attempt 1]` under the live id (not reap-eligible). (c) **429 → stale → cancel takeover → late landing:** attempt 1 answers 429 and is *held* by the fake (`collisionMode: 'land'`); the op goes stale; a cancel takes it over (`gen=2`), `events.get(eventId)` 404 → no bump owed, `eventId` retained per C6.7, cancel T2 commits with `unresolved_inserts` naming attempt 1 (`attemptId`, `eventId`, `opId`, `gen=1`); the held insert is then released and lands → the next `events.list` / `/b/{token}` read classifies it busy, reaps it with `If-Match`, and retires exactly attempt 1 by `marloAttemptId`; the **negative control** — the same run with a deliberately mis-configured classifier that maps 429 to `definite` — leaves no entry, the landed event persists and is never reaped, and the test asserts that the retained entry is what makes the reap possible. (d) **patch 429 on reschedule:** T1 committed with the reservation → `events.patch` → 429 → attempt `unresolved`, `pending_op` and `[reserved_start, reserved_end)` retained, no compensation, 503; retry → `events.get` shows the old interval → new attempt → patch → 2xx → T2 → 200. (e) **delete 429 on cancel:** `events.delete` → 429 → `pending_op` retained, `status='confirmed'`, occupancy still held (a create into the slot → 409), 503; retry → `events.get` 404 (the delete had executed) → T2 → 200 `cancelled` with exactly one further `events.get` and zero further deletes. (f) **bump 429 on takeover** (C6.3c): the takeover stops under its new `gen`, 503; the retry re-observes and proceeds. (g) **mock and live parity:** (b)–(e) run against the mock adapter and against the live adapter over the fake `fetch`, asserting identical route status, identical `pending_op`/`unresolved_inserts`, and identical attempt outcomes. | REV10-02 |

## Approach

Work proceeds in the order below; each step lands with its tests so `npm test` is green at every step.

### 1. Env + proof hardening first (AC-6, AC-15; LIVE-03, LIVE-09, REV1-12)
- `lib/env.ts` is the single reader: `resolveEnv(overrides?)` with `setEnvOverride()` for tests; `MARLO_PROOF=1` forces `store=memory, calendar=mock, email=mock`.
- `scripts/proof.cjs`: extract `provisionDependencies()` (the existing `npm ci` fallback) and `runProof()`; provisioning runs first, logs the network step, and exits 2 under `MARLO_OFFLINE=1`; `runProof` spawns typecheck/build/unit with the explicit disabled child env. Export the functions for the unit test (guarded by `require.main === module`). `.github/proof.yml` is not touched.
- Test-setup `fetch` guard throws on any non-localhost URL; grep test exception list contains only `app/api/health/route.ts`.

### 2. Owner-scoped identity + per-owner routing + host chrome (AC-1, AC-2, AC-14; P1, REV1-01, REV1-02)
- `lib/owners.ts`: `Owner { id, slug, firstName, email, calendarId }`, `stableIds(ownerSlug, eventSlug, scheduleKey)` (C1), `OwnerStore` (memory + pg), `resolveOwner(slug)`, `RESERVED_ROOT_SLUGS` + `isReservedRootSlug(slug)` + `ReservedOwnerSlugError` (REV5-06): the OAuth callback fails sign-in with `owner_slug_reserved`, both stores refuse `upsert`, `resolveOwner` short-circuits to not-found, `publicBookingPath`/`bookAgainHref` throw; `tests/reserved-slugs.test.ts` derives the expected set from the `app/` tree and covers `/b/…`, `/host/…` (incl. `middleware.ts` matcher + `isAuthorizedForPath`), and the owner-scoped API routes. `ensureDemoFixtures()` and the OAuth callback both go through `materializeOwner(owner)` which upserts the owner and its fixture-defined event types/schedule under stable ids.
- `lib/availability/event-type.ts`: `createEventType({ ownerId, slug, … })`, uniqueness on `(ownerId, slug)`, `resolveEventType(ownerId, slug)`; `getEventType(id)` resolves from the store in pg mode.
- Routes: `app/(public)/[slug]/[event]` treats `[slug]` as the owner slug (no alias/canonicalisation from event to host); `app/demo/intro-30` pins `owner=demo, fixture=true`. New `app/api/owners/[ownerSlug]/event-types/[eventSlug]/{available-times,bookings}`; legacy `app/api/event-types/[slug]/*` delegate with `ownerSlug='demo'` and 404 `owner_required` otherwise.
- `booking-form-store` carries `{ ownerSlug, eventSlug }`; `lib/api/client.ts` builds owner-scoped paths and maps the C3 envelope; `lib/api/DIVERGENCES.md` updated.
- `hostCopy(owner)` returns all host-facing strings; templates take `owner.firstName` explicitly.

### 3. Schema, durable stores, host lock, single-host occupancy (AC-3, AC-4, AC-8, AC-17; LIVE-01, LIVE-05, REV1-02, REV1-11)
- `sql/001_init.sql` per C8; `scripts/migrate.cjs` + `npm run db:migrate`; `lib/db/schema-version.ts` `LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` (shared by migrate, the store guard, and health; filesystem-asserted); `PgBookingStore.assertMigrated()` on first use; `/api/health` per C13 (its own bounded query — Approach §8).
- `PgBookingStore.createBooking` is two-phase (C6.4): L0 = `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext($hostId))` → `(owner_id, idempotency_key)` lookup + `create_rejections` fence lookup by `(owner_id, idempotency_key, create_fingerprint)` → fence present → the fenced 409 (REV8-01) → row present → fingerprint check → return/resume with zero `events.list` calls (`lookupKey()` in `lib/booking/create.ts`, REV7-02) → neither → `COMMIT` (lock released) → R0 = the C7 `events.list` external read over the interval, outside any transaction, missing keys only (REV6-03) → T1 = `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext($hostId))` → the mandatory second `(owner_id, idempotency_key)` lookup + second fence lookup → external-busy check against R0's snapshot → C6.1 occupancy query (`status='confirmed'` intervals ∪ non-null `[reserved_start, reserved_end)`) → either `INSERT` with `pending_op.create`, intended `google_event_id`, `idempotency_key` → `COMMIT`, or, on rejection, `INSERT INTO create_rejections` (`fenceRejection()`) → `COMMIT` → 409 (REV8-01); calendar step outside any transaction; T2 = lock → owned conditional `UPDATE` → `COMMIT`. Same lock key for reschedule/cancel (C6). Memory store: per-host async mutex with identical observable ordering, `byToken`, `byIdempotencyKey`, and `rejectedKeys` (`(ownerId, key, fingerprint) → reason`) maps, and the same `pending_op`/reservation fields; group and collective rows exist only as memory-mode fixtures on their existing code paths (C6.9). Pg store: `resolveEventType` (a read-only catalog lookup) runs first in every route and a resolved `kind` of `group` or `collective` short-circuits to 501 `group_not_supported` / `collective_not_supported` before the advisory lock, any `PgBookingStore` booking method, R0, the calendar, or Gmail is touched (C6.9, REV10-03); the seed and materialization write `kind='one_on_one'` only.
- `lib/booking/occupancy.ts`: one `hostOccupancy(store, hostId, window, { excludeBookingId? })` used by create, reschedule, and both availability routes — no second conflict query exists (C6.1); it selects the host's rows (`host_id = hostId`) together with their reservations, so `extraBusyForHosts`/`listConfirmedBookingsForHost` in `lib/booking/booking.ts` become thin wrappers over it rather than a second occupancy definition (REV9-02); `externalBusy(provider, window, { excludeBookingId? })` is the single R0 read (C7 classification incl. reap-on-observation), which create and reschedule call before their T1 and never inside it (REV6-03).
- `lib/booking/ids.ts`: `newBookingId()`, `newBookingToken()`, `newCalendarEventId()` (Google alphabet), `constantTimeEqual`; `lib/booking/fingerprint.ts`: `createFingerprint({ eventTypeId, start, inviteeEmail })` (canonical JSON → sha256), written once in create T1 and read by the C9 replay comparison (REV3-05).
- `lib/availability/slots.ts`: `generateSlots` gains `incrementMinutes` (default `durationMinutes`) and `RESCHEDULE_SLOT_INCREMENT_MIN = 15`; `rescheduleCandidates(booking, window, occupancy)` is the single generator used by the booking availability route and by reschedule validation, and excludes the booking's current start (REV3-08).
- `lib/booking/occupancy.ts` `externalBusy` takes the store's managed occupancy map so `listBusy` can classify each managed item as **matched** (excluded) or **unmatched/displaced** (busy) per C7 (REV3-10).
- Availability = `externalBusy(events.list, managed-excluded)` ∪ `managedBusy(hostOccupancy)`.

### 4. Google adapters, busy classification, fail-closed reads, compensation + reconciliation (AC-7, AC-11, AC-14; LIVE-04, LIVE-08, REV1-03, REV1-04, REV1-10)
- `lib/google/calendar.ts`: `insert({ id, … })` / `get(id)` / `patch(id, body, { ifMatch })` / `delete(id, { ifMatch })` (all set `extendedProperties.private.marloBookingId`; `insert` also sets `extendedProperties.private.marloAttemptId` to the attempt's id and `get`/`listBusy` return both — REV6-01; attendees + `sendUpdates` per C4; `insert` always passes the client-supplied `id` and maps Google 409 `duplicate` → `AlreadyExists`; every response carries `etag`; `patch`/`delete` send `If-Match` and map 412 → `PreconditionFailed`; `get` maps 404/410 → `null`) and `listBusy(window)` implementing C7 pagination/classification; `lib/calendar/google-freebusy.ts` keeps fail-closed `freeBusy` for the collective path. Both throw `AvailabilityUnknown`. Calendar errors are classified by the single C6.0 function `classifyCalendarError` (`lib/google/errors.ts`) — `applied` / `definite` (4xx other than 409/412/429) / `already_exists` (409) / `precondition_failed` (412) / `ambiguous` (timeout, 5xx, **429**, network) — imported by both adapters, the fake `fetch`, and every C6 step; no other module tests a status code (REV10-02) — the mock adapter exposes the same classes, honours client ids, models etags/`If-Match`/412, and supports `collisionMode: 'duplicate' | 'land'` for a re-used id (C6.3a).
- `lib/google/oauth.ts`: refresh-token exchange with injected `fetch`; `invalid_grant` → `HostNotConnected`. `lib/auth/config.ts` scope list = C7 set; callback materializes the owner.
- `lib/booking/create.ts`: C6.4 two-phase create; `classifyCommitError(err)` → `definite | unknown`; `lib/booking/reconcile.ts` `withFinishedTransaction(hostId, fn)` implements C6.5 (fresh connection, `lock_timeout`, advisory lock reacquire, then `fn`), shared by create T1, reschedule T2, and cancel T2 unknown outcomes; `resumeCreate()` (`events.get` → insert-if-absent → T2) used by the key replay, notify, and any lifecycle call that reads `pending_op.kind='create'`; `lookupKey()` is the L0 locked lookup that runs before `externalBusy()` and short-circuits every existing key (REV7-02) and every fenced `(key, fingerprint)` (REV8-01); `fenceRejection(tx, { ownerId, key, fingerprint, reason })` writes the fence inside the rejecting T1 and is the only path to a terminal 409 on create. `lib/booking/superseded.ts` `reconcileSuperseded(op, insertResponse)`: one locked read returns `{ response: 'success' | 'operation_in_progress' | 'operation_superseded', eligible: boolean }` — for a create op `'success'` is decided by the **single shared finalization predicate** `createFinalized(row, op)` — `lib/booking/create-identity.ts`, the one implementation behind C6.3a(1)(ii), the C6.4 **L0** lookup, and the C9 replay, so the three can never disagree (REV15-02): `createIdentityMatches(row, op)` (`owner_id`, `idempotency_key`, `create_fingerprint`, `id` — all written once and never updated, C9/REV3-05) **and** `row.latest_action IS NOT NULL` (the C6.4a finalization witness) **and** `pending_op` no longer naming `op.opId` (`pending_op IS NULL OR pending_op->>'opId' <> $opId`, the rev-9 clearing test kept verbatim). **The rev-9 prohibition is narrowed to what it always meant (REV15-02):** what no predicate may do is compare `latest_action` against a **particular value** — `= 'confirm'`, which a later reschedule or cancel legitimately falsifies (REV9-01) — while testing the column's **nullness** is *required*, because nullness is monotonic (C6.4a) and is the only durable fact that separates a create someone **finalized** from one a C6.7 cancel merely **replaced**; the rev-9 phrasing “never `latest_action`” is withdrawn as over-broad. Two **non-terminal** branches, never 201 and never `operation_superseded`: a higher `gen` under the same `opId` → `'operation_in_progress'` with `retryAfterSeconds` (REV9-01); and identity matching with `latest_action IS NULL` while either `pending_op` names a different op or `unfinished_create` is non-null → `'operation_in_progress'` as well, with zero Google calls and zero mutations of its own (REV14-01, AC-11(r-v)), for reschedule/cancel by the C6.3 target test — the **route** maps `'success'` to its own success status (`POST …/bookings`, the legacy create route, and the C10 link route → **201** with the C3 envelope; reschedule/cancel/notify → 200; REV8-02) so a superseded create worker never answers 200 to the shipped client — `eligible` iff the row's `unresolved_inserts` retains the worker's own `attemptId` under an id that is not live and not an intended id of the open `pending_op`; only an eligible attempt with a definite 2xx enters the own-event cleanup (`If-Match` delete → `retireAttempt`; 412 → re-observe; same-attribution retry with the observed etag), and the row's interval is never an input (REV7-01). Logs `outcome_unresolved`, `operation_superseded`, `stale_finalize_ignored`, `attempt_unresolved`, `event_reaped`, `version_bumped`, `submission_discarded`, `create_fenced`, `stale_response_ignored`. The only calendar deletes the create route can cause are C7's R0 reap of *other* rows' retired ids and a superseded worker's own-event cleanup (C6.3a(2)); `lookupKey()`'s completed-row replay never invokes the reap and issues zero Google calls whatever `unresolved_inserts` holds (REV11-02); `lib/booking/reap.ts`'s `selectBatch`/`reap` are called only from reschedule, cancel, notify, `GET /api/bookings/{id}`, `/b/{token}`, and the C7 classifier. `lib/booking/attempts.ts` implements the C6.3b ledger (`beginAttempt(op, kind, eventId, ifMatch?)` — the owned pre-mutation write — and `finishAttempt(attemptId, outcome)`); `lib/booking/retain.ts` `resolveOrRetainIntendedIds(op)` runs `events.get` over every intended id before any abandonment/cancel T2 (applied → complete; absent → retain; ambiguous → 503) and `reapUnresolved(row, { max })` implements the C6.3a reap; `lib/booking/version.ts` `bumpVersion(eventId, op)` implements the C6.3c takeover bump and `reconcilePrecondition(op, id)` the 412 rule (REV4-01..03).
- Fake `Queryable` gains: `commitOutcome: 'ok' | 'definite' | 'unknown-committed' | 'unknown-rolled-back' | { unknown: true, resolveAfterMs, as: 'commit' | 'rollback' }` (delayed completion holds the fake advisory lock until resolved), per-connection identity (to assert the reconciler used a fresh one), `lock_timeout` simulation, and a fake clock for the 2-minute stale window.

### 5. Email: validation, MIME/ICS, dual recipients, delivery ledger (AC-5, AC-10, AC-13; LIVE-02, LIVE-07, LIVE-10, REV1-05, REV1-06, REV1-07)
- `lib/email/address.ts` strict single-mailbox parser (400 at the API boundary); `lib/email/mime.ts` RFC 2047 / quoted-printable / base64 `.ics`; `lib/email/ics.ts` RFC 5545 escaping.
- Templates `confirm` / `reschedule` / `cancel` × `invitee` / `owner`; cancel intro is its own constant with a disjointness test. Every body carries the absolute `/b/{token}` URL built from the request origin (`bookingPageUrl(origin, token)`) and the "always shows the current status" sentence (C4, REV6-05).
- `lib/notify/ledger.ts`: `claim({ bookingId, revision, action, recipient }) → { gen } | null` / `finalize({ …, gen, state })` per C5 on both stores (finalisation without `gen` is a type error); `lib/notify/dispatch.ts` sends to both recipients for every action, threads `gen` from claim to finalize, and consults `notificationMode` only for attendees/`sendUpdates`. `POST /api/bookings/{id}/notify` is implemented as the **three ordered phases of C5 (REV15-01)**, in `lib/notify/route-order.ts`, each under the host lock and each re-reading the booking `FOR UPDATE` after the previous phase commits, with the claim never running before recovery: **N-1 lifecycle recovery** — `resumeCreate()` when `pending_op.kind='create'` (non-stale → 409 `operation_in_progress`; stale → `takeOver()` then resume to create T2/T2′, which writes the non-null `latest_action`, clears `unfinished_create`, and issues the `(revision, 'confirm')` claim itself), `resumeRepair()` for a **stale** `calendar_repair` (REV4-04), 409 `operation_in_progress` for a non-stale repair and for any reschedule/cancel op — this phase writes **zero** `notification_deliveries` rows; **N-2 repair/reap** — on a confirmed row with `calendar_state='failed'` and no `pending_op`, begin the owned `calendar_repair` op of C6.8 (`lib/booking/repair.ts`: T1 writes the op, `events.get` → insert-if-absent with the persisted id, owned T2 sets `calendar_state='created'`), on a cancelled row run only the C6.3a reap; **N-3 notification claim** — parse the two C5 request forms, validate `(expectedRevision, action)` against the **re-read** `(revision, latest_action)` together inside the claim transaction, claim per recipient, refuse stale pairs with the current pair in the body, and refuse a claim that is *still* unfinalized (`latest_action IS NULL`) with 409 `stale_revision` + `{ latestAction: null }` — a defensive branch CF-2 makes unreachable, logged `notify_no_pair`, asserted never to fire; on a cancelled row it runs only the C6.3a reap of retired ids and never inserts or patches (REV3-02, REV5-04); the send path performs no pre-send revision re-check and no send-deadline or issuance-rate guard — an already-claimed send is issued as claimed, and `attempts` is the documented bound on copies (REV5-05, REV6-04). `GmailSendError` carries `definite: boolean` (4xx other than 429 and `host_not_connected` → definite; timeout/5xx/429/network → ambiguous); `dispatch` calls `finalize(gen, 'failed')` **only** for a definite refusal and leaves the row `claimed` otherwise, and the claim predicate in both stores is exactly the C5 SQL — `failed` re-claimable immediately, `claimed` only after the stale window (REV7-04). Create T2′ writes `latest_action='confirm'` alongside `calendar_state='failed'` (REV3-03). `summarizeDelivery(rows, ['invitee','owner'])` in `lib/notify/ledger.ts` is the single `delivery.email` computation for every envelope (missing rows → `pending`), and notify claims every required recipient (REV4-05).
- Distinct `GmailSendError` / `CalendarError`; route maps them to `delivery.errors.*` and log codes.

### 6. `/b/{token}` reschedule + cancel with durable, owned operation state (AC-9, AC-12, AC-18; P2, P3, LIVE-06, REV1-05, REV1-08, REV1-09, REV2-01..05)
- `lib/booking/demo-meta.ts` shared `demoMetadata(booking)`.
- `lib/booking/ops.ts`: `PendingOp` type, `beginOp(kind)` (fresh `opId`, `gen: 1`, intended ids), `takeOver(op)` (`gen+1`, conditional), `ownedUpdate(sql, { opId, gen, revision })` helper that every T2/T2′/compensation goes through, `observeCalendarState(op)` (`events.get` on old/fallback/intended ids, capturing etags) for takeover and resume; takeover keeps `attempts` and runs `bumpVersion` before anything else (C6.3c).
- `lib/booking/reschedule.ts` and `lib/booking/cancel.ts` implement C6.6/C6.7: T1 reserves (`reserved_start/end`) and writes the op; ownership re-check before fallback insert; T2 releases the reservation; compensation only when still owner and the transaction is known rolled back (C6.5); superseded path reconciles the winner (`operation_superseded`) through `reconcileSuperseded` (§4), which never deletes a live or open-op id (REV7-01). `reschedule.ts` `reconcileStale(op, request)` implements C6.6 (i)–(iii): after taking over and completing a stale reschedule it compares the completed `newStart` with the request's `start` — equal → the completed outcome is returned as this request's 200 (no new op); different → 409 `booking_changed` with the current row; abandoned or non-reschedule kinds → the requested move proceeds as a new op (REV7-03). Cancel refuses a live reschedule (`operation_in_progress` + `retryAfterSeconds`) and takes over only a stale one, completing or abandoning it from observed Google state before deleting the resulting event; every abandonment and every cancel T2 is preceded by `resolveOrRetainIntendedIds` (REV4-01) and by the reap of the row's **retired** ids (`lib/booking/reap.ts`: `selectBatch(row)` = eligible ids (entries' ids minus the live id, computed in the same locked read) ordered by `inspectSeq` nulls-first, then `issuedAt`, then `eventId`, the first `REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST` taken and stamped in that same transaction with successive values of the row's monotonic `reap_cursor` — never with a clock reading, so the traversal advances under a frozen, coarse, or backwards clock — REV6-02, REV13-01; `reap(batch)` deletes each present event with `If-Match` and calls `retireAttempt(bookingId, event.marloAttemptId)`, the single idempotent retirement shared by reapers, cancel, notify, C7, and superseded workers — REV5-01/02, REV6-01; the batch is cleanup and never a T2 precondition); every clearing update (T2, T2′, cancel T2, repair T2) copies every `unresolved` insert attempt into `unresolved_inserts`, including attempts naming the resulting live id (REV5-01); an ambiguous observation keeps `pending_op` and returns 503; every patch/delete/compensation goes through `beginAttempt` and carries `If-Match` (REV4-02). Cancel T2 writes `calendar_state='deleted'`; reschedule T2 writes `'created'` (C6.8).
- Parity harness runs each scenario against `{mock adapters, live adapters + fake fetch}` × `{calendar_invitation, email_confirmation}` and asserts identical observable calls/recipients; interleaving and takeover tests use the lock-queue fake with the fake clock and a "pause here" hook between T1 and the calendar step and between the calendar step and T2.

### 7. Id/token split, create idempotency, token-page controls, one-off link (AC-19, AC-20, AC-21, AC-22; REV2-08, REV2-09, REV2-10, REV2-11)
- C11 split: `Booking.token` on both stores, `getByToken`/`getById`, bearer auth middleware `requireBookingToken(request, id)` shared by every `app/api/bookings/[id]/*` route (incl. the new `available-times`), `lib/api/server.ts` `getBookingByToken`, client methods with the header, `mapBooking` explicit mapping, fixture migration, `DIVERGENCES.md`.
- C9 key: `booking-form-store` unresolved-submission record `{ key, payload, startedAt }` (persisted in `sessionStorage` before send; cleared only on 201/400/409 `slot_unavailable`|`session_full`/422; retained on 409 `operation_in_progress`, 503, 5xx, network, reload), `recovering` state that replays via `client.recoverBooking` **before** availability loads and bypasses the elapsed/availability gates for that replay only, with "Try again" / "Start over" (REV3-04, REV3-06); `createBooking`/`recoverBooking` headers, route validation, store lookup comparing `create_fingerprint` + `resumeCreate()` replay (non-stale → 409 `operation_in_progress`), 422 on fingerprint mismatch (REV3-05); every request tagged with its key and responses for a key that is no longer the unresolved record dropped with `stale_response_ignored` (REV8-01).
- C12 UI: `app/(public)/b/[token]/BookingControls.tsx` (client) with the `controlsState` machine (incl. `stalled` after an exhausted automatic retry: Try again with the original `expectedRevision` / Reload, REV8-03), reschedule picker reusing the BOOK-FE slot components over `getBookingAvailability` (15-minute reschedule grid, REV3-08), two-step cancel, resend; the cancelled panel renders delivery status + Retry notification when delivery is not `sent` (REV3-07); copy keys added to `copy/en.json`; `handler-transport` tests through the real handlers. No visual redesign; BOOK-FE mobile/CSS tests unchanged.
- C10 link: `bookSingleUseLink` delegates to the shared store create with key `link:{token}` and consumes the link in T2 (idempotent per `consumedByBookingId`); `resolveSingleUseTarget` no longer rejects consumed links for `POST` — the C9 lookup replays identical payloads (201) and the handler maps a fingerprint mismatch to the existing 410 (REV3-09); `listSingleUseAvailableTimes` uses `hostOccupancy`; both `app/api/links/[token]/*` handlers start with the pg-mode 501 guard.

### 8. README + health (AC-15, AC-16)
- `app/api/health/route.ts` per C13: `healthSchema(db, { timeoutMs: HEALTH_DB_TIMEOUT_MS })` in `lib/db/health.ts` runs the single bounded `schema_migrations` readiness query against the injected `Queryable` and maps rows / `42P01` / errors / deadline to `current | behind | missing | unknown`; the route composes `store`/`calendar`/`email` from `lib/env`, sets HTTP 200/503 from `ok`, and neither imports nor instantiates the booking store, the Google adapters, or the Gmail adapter (REV9-03). README's health section states the envelope, the 503 rule, and that `calendar`/`email` are configuration, not connectivity; and lists group and collective event types as memory-mode fixtures outside the live product (C6.9, Product bar lock).
- README sections: env vars (incl. `MARLO_OFFLINE`), migration procedure and ordering, URL shape + owner-scoped routes, `Idempotency-Key` on create, booking `id` vs `token` and the bearer header, `/b/{token}` controls, reschedule/cancel + retry semantics (incl. `operation_in_progress` waits and `booking_outcome_unknown` reloads), 201/`delivery`/notify contract (both notify forms), offline contract, the honest, precise duplicate bound (copies ≤ retries recorded; one retry of an *unresolved* send per stale window; a definitely refused send can be retried immediately; delayed copies may cluster — REV6-04, REV7-04), the email-ordering and booking-page-is-authoritative sentences (REV6-05), the external-calendar check before save and its snapshot window (REV6-03), `/api/links/*` fixture-only status, Founder live smoke.

## Assumptions and risks

- **Assumption:** the host's Google account grants `calendar.freebusy`, `calendar.events`, and `gmail.send` in one consent; sending "as the host" via Gmail API is acceptable to the Founder for this slice (no custom domain / SES).
- **Assumption:** an owner's slug is the validated local-part of their Google email; a collision on sign-in (two accounts with the same local-part) fails the sign-in with `owner_slug_taken` rather than silently suffixing, and a local-part that is an application-owned root segment (`RESERVED_ROOT_SLUGS`, C1) fails it with `owner_slug_reserved` (REV5-06). Both documented in README.
- **Assumption:** `events.list` on the host's primary calendar returns every occupying item within the window when `singleEvents=true` is used; items on secondary calendars are out of scope (Non-goals).
- **Risk — Next env reload (LIVE-03):** `next build` re-reads `.env.local`; mitigated by the explicit disabled child env plus `MARLO_PROOF=1` hard override; the fetch guard fails the proof loudly rather than silently going live.
- **Assumption — Google client-supplied event ids:** `events.insert` accepts an `id` in `[a-v0-9]{5,1024}` and answers 409 `duplicate` for a reused id; this is what makes inserts idempotent (C6.2). The live adapter is proven against a fake `fetch` that enforces the alphabet and the duplicate response; the Founder live smoke confirms it against Google.
- **Assumption withdrawn — Google does not guarantee id exclusion (REV4-01):** Google documents that cancelled events are not retained indefinitely and that, because of the system's distributed nature, an id collision is *not guaranteed* to be detected at creation time. Rev 5 therefore relies on no exclusion at Google: a re-used id may be answered 409 `duplicate` **or may land**, and both are modelled by the fakes and handled identically (retain → observe → reap). The only Google-side guarantees relied on are those the contract states: `events.get` by id, `etag` on every event, and `If-Match`/412 on `patch`/`delete`.
- **Risk — Neon advisory locks under pooling:** `pg_advisory_xact_lock` is transaction-scoped and safe under PgBouncer transaction pooling; the builder must not use session-level locks. The lock is **never** held across a Google call (rev 3 two-phase create); the C6.5 reconciler's reacquisition is bounded by `lock_timeout = 5s`, after which the outcome stays unknown (503) rather than guessed.
- **Risk — at-least-once email:** a Gmail-accepted send whose `sent` write fails is re-sent after the 2-minute stale window (C5); accepted and documented. Late finalisers after a takeover are ignored by generation (REV2-06). **Bound (REV6-04, REV7-04):** `attempts` bounds the copies ever sent for a `(revision, action, recipient)`; a `claimed` row is re-acquired at most once per stale window and only on a caller's retry, a `failed` row (definite refusal, delivered nothing) is re-acquired immediately — so a guest can burn several `attempts` inside one window without a single copy, and no rate limit on that is added (Non-goals); delivery *timing* is unbounded — stalled generations can deliver clustered copies (AC-13(k)) — and no issuance-rate guard is claimed because none could close the window; README says so. **Ordering (REV5-05):** an already-claimed send may reach Gmail after a later revision's emails were sent (claim commit → stall → resume after a cancel); the ledger is unaffected, the guest may see a confirmation arrive after a cancellation, and README says so. Not prevented by design (Non-goals: no send queue, no lock across Gmail).
- **Risk — `pending_op` stuck when a process dies mid-operation:** the slot stays held (C6.1) and the op is taken over by the next call that reads it after the stale window (`gen+1`, reconciled from observed Google state); a different-kind call before that is refused with `operation_in_progress` + `retryAfterSeconds` and the token page waits. A create whose guest never retries leaves a confirmed row holding its slot with a possibly missing calendar event until any lifecycle/notify call reconciles it — accepted for this slice (the owner sees nothing until then; documented). No background reconciler by design.
- **Risk — Google patch vs host deletion race on reschedule:** a 404/410 on patch inserts a replacement with the persisted `fallbackEventId` after an ownership re-check; a duplicate is possible only if the host manually re-adds the event — accepted.
- **Risk — host edits Marlo events by hand (REV3-10):** a managed event the host moved in Google Calendar blocks both its old interval (store) and its new one (conservative external busy) until the host moves it back or the guest reschedules through `/b/{token}`; no sync is attempted. Accepted and documented in README.
- **Risk — late-landed events are visible until observed (REV4-01):** an insert that Google executes after our side gave up lands on the host's calendar under a retained id; it is external busy from its first appearance in `events.list` and is deleted by the next call that observes it (availability, lifecycle, notify, or a token-page read), but between landing and that call the host sees a stale Marlo event — and, when the interval was released and re-booked in the meantime, a **transient overlap** with the new booking's event (REV5-03: occupancy is not held for an insert that may still execute; the new booking is never affected). No background sweeper by design (Non-goals); documented in README in the narrowed form.
- **Risk — `unresolved_inserts` never empties for an insert whose execution is never observed:** an attempt whose request was lost and never executed — or executed while its id was still live, so nothing was ever reaped under it — stays listed for the life of the row (REV5-01: neither an observed event nor a deletion resolves an attempt); each read of that row costs at most `REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST` `events.get` calls, spread fairly over the **retired** listed ids by `inspectSeq` (REV6-02, REV13-01), and any event that ever lands under one of them is retired exactly once by attribution (REV6-01); entries naming the live id cost nothing. Accepted; the list is per row and audit-visible.
- **Risk — external-availability snapshot (REV6-03):** the R0 `events.list` read precedes T1; an external event the host adds between R0 and T1's commit is not seen by that create or reschedule — the same window the shipped `freeBusy` check has today. The next availability read treats it as busy (C7), and the overlap is between a Marlo booking and a host-entered event, never between two Marlo bookings (managed occupancy is checked under the lock). Accepted and documented in README.
- **Risk — the owner's email carries the guest's `/b/{token}` link (REV6-05):** the host can reschedule or cancel their own booking through the guest surface; there is no host dashboard (Non-goals) and the token is already the capability both parties act on. Accepted.
- **Risk — an adopted delayed insert leaves a permanent entry under the live id (REV7-01):** when a takeover adopts an event whose insert response is still in flight to the superseded worker, that worker's attempt stays listed in `unresolved_inserts` under the live id (it may not delete a live id, whatever the row's interval says); the entry costs nothing while the id is live and is retired by attribution the first time the id is retired and reaped (cancel, or a fallback reschedule). Accepted — the alternative (letting the superseded worker act on "the row no longer shows my target") deleted the winner's live event.
- **Risk — L0/T1 double lookup (REV7-02):** every create with a fresh key pays one extra locked `SELECT` before R0; a replay pays only that `SELECT`. The second lookup inside T1 is mandatory because a concurrent same-key request can commit between L0 and T1 (AC-19(i)(6)). Accepted.
- **Risk — same-target recovery returns the dead worker's outcome, not a fresh one (REV7-03):** the recovered response reports the `delivery` of the completing T2's `(revision, 'reschedule')` claim, which may be `pending`/`failed` if that T2's sends failed; the page's resend control covers it (C12). A different-target request that lands on a completed stale move is answered `booking_changed` rather than chained into a second move, because the caller's `expectedRevision` predates a change the guest has not seen. Accepted.
- **Risk — 412 from host edits (REV4-02):** a host who edits a Marlo event by hand between reschedule T1 and the patch changes its etag; the patch 412s, the worker observes a foreign interval and fails the reschedule with 502 `calendar_patch_failed` (the event stays busy at its actual interval per C7). Accepted: no calendar-to-store synchronization.
- **Risk — idempotency-key replay window:** keys are retained forever on the row (no expiry); a guest replaying an old key gets the old booking, which is the intended behaviour; a different payload under an old key is 422. Accepted.
- **Risk — rejection fences accumulate (REV8-01):** every terminal create rejection leaves one `create_rejections` row per `(key, fingerprint)`, never expired (the same policy as keys). Rows are bounded by client submissions — one per rejected attempt, and a browser client never reuses a key — and the table is read only by the two point lookups under the lock. Accepted; a retention job is out of scope (Non-goals: no cron).
- **Risk — `stalled` is not a wait bound (REV8-03):** the page leaves `pending` after one exhausted automatic retry, but the other operation may still be in flight when the guest clicks Try again; that click re-enters `pending` with one fresh automatic retry and can stall again. The guest is never stuck (Try again / Reload are always available) but may have to click more than once during a long-running operation; no wall-clock bound on the other caller is promised.
- **Risk — health readiness query cost (REV9-03):** every `/api/health` hit in pg mode costs one bounded round trip to Neon; under an uptime monitor at 1/min that is negligible, and the 2 s deadline caps the worst case; the route is never on a booking path. A transient `unknown` on a cold Neon compute is reported honestly (503) rather than masked — the post-deploy check retries per its own policy.
- **Risk — memory-mode group or collective rows (Product bar lock, rev 11):** memory-mode rows do not survive a deploy or an isolate recycle, so none can leak into pg mode; the pg 501 branch is defensive and is reached only through a fixture-injected catalog row (AC-24(c)). In memory mode those rows carry only their existing fixture semantics — a group reschedule through `/b/{token}`, for instance, is not covered by C6.1/C12 and behaves as the existing code does; accepted, because memory mode is the demo/test mode and neither kind is a product surface in this slice.
- **Risk — 429 as ambiguous costs a retry round trip (REV10-02):** a rate-limited insert/patch/delete answers 503 `booking_outcome_unknown` and is reconciled by `events.get` on the retry instead of failing fast; under sustained quota exhaustion the guest sees the existing Try again path rather than a definite error. Accepted — a definite classification can discard an attempt whose insert still lands.
- **Risk — a create worker superseded during a still-running takeover now answers `operation_in_progress` instead of `operation_superseded` (REV9-01):** the client replays once automatically; if the takeover is itself slow the guest sees the existing Try again / reload path (C9), never a false failure for a booking that exists.
- **Risk — encryption key rotation:** `OAUTH_TOKEN_KEY` rotation is not handled; documented as a manual re-connect.
- **Scope risk:** AC-1/2/10 touch the front-end shipped in BOOK-FE (#38). The builder must keep the BOOK-FE mobile/CSS tests green; no visual redesign.

## Verification

PROOF_CMD: npm test

`npm test` runs `scripts/proof.cjs` → (dependency provisioning only if `node_modules` is absent and `MARLO_OFFLINE` is unset) → typecheck → `next build` (with the explicit disabled env from AC-6) → unit/route tests. With dependencies installed it passes with no `DATABASE_URL`, no Google credentials, and no network (the test fetch guard enforces this). All AC-1..25 proofs are inside this run.

**Founder live smoke (manual, not graded):** set `DATABASE_URL` on Vercel; run `npm run db:migrate`; set `LIVE_CALENDAR=1`, `LIVE_EMAIL=1`, `OAUTH_TOKEN_KEY`, Google client id/secret; sign in at `/signin` (this creates the owner row); book `/{shahar}/{intro-30}`; confirm the event appears on the host calendar (with a `marlo…` event id) and both inboxes get the confirmation, each carrying a booking-page link that opens `/b/{token}`; open `/b/{token}`, use the page's Reschedule control (event moves, two reschedule emails), then Cancel (event removed, two cancel emails); submit the booking form twice quickly and confirm one booking; reload the booking page mid-submit (throttle the network) and confirm the reload finishes the original booking; in the Google Calendar UI move a Marlo event and confirm its new interval is no longer offered; **retention/reap check:** with the calendar adapter's debug script insert an event carrying a cancelled booking's `marloBookingId` **and the `marloAttemptId` of one of that row's entries** under a retired id named by its `unresolved_inserts`; request the owner's availability for that window — the interval is not offered, and on the next request the event is gone from the host calendar and exactly that entry is retired; **ETag check:** with the debug script patch a live Marlo event with a stale `If-Match` — expect 412; hit `/api/health` as the **first** request after a fresh deploy (before any booking page) and confirm HTTP 200 with `store: "pg", schema: "current", db: "ok", calendar: "live", email: "live"`; with `DATABASE_URL` pointed at an empty database confirm HTTP 503 with `schema: "missing"` (C13).

## Round budget

- Build rounds: 3 (builder → inspector → judge per round).
- Round 1: steps 1–4 (env/proof, owner-scoped routing, schema + two-phase store + reservations, Google adapters incl. idempotent insert and C6.5 reconciliation). Round 2: steps 5–6 (email + ledger with generations, reschedule/cancel with owned operation state and the C6.7 policy). Round 3: steps 7–8 (id/token split, idempotency key + fingerprint + reload recovery, token-page controls incl. the cancelled-panel retry and the reschedule grid, one-off link replay, README) + inspector findings. Retention/reap, the attempt ledger, and ETag-conditioned mutations (REV4-01..03), calendar repair with notify takeover (REV3-02, REV4-04), and the required-recipient delivery status (REV4-05) land in round 2 with C6/C5; attempt-level retention and live/retired reap eligibility (REV5-01/02), the narrowed overlap guarantee and its interleaving (REV5-03), cleanup-on-cancelled notify (REV5-04), and the send-ordering test (REV5-05) land with them; reserved root slugs (REV5-06) land in round 1 with §2. Attributed retirement and fair reap traversal (REV6-01/02) land in round 2 with C6; the R0 external read (REV6-03) in round 1 with §3/§4; the honest duplicate bound with its multi-generation test and the email booking-page link (REV6-04/05) in round 2 with §5, and their README sentences in round 3 with §8. The superseded-worker eligibility rule and its adoption-then-move interleaving (REV7-01) and same-target recovery (REV7-03) land in round 2 with §6; the L0 locked lookup before R0 (REV7-02) in round 1 with §3/§4; the precise claim bound with definite-only `failed` finalisation and the failed-retry test (REV7-04) in round 2 with §5, its README sentence in round 3 with §8. The create-identity reconciliation and the AC-11(r-ii..iv) interleavings (REV9-01) land in round 1 with §4; the group/collective scope-out — `kind='one_on_one'`-only seed and materialization, the post-resolution 501 guard, and the AC-24 catalog-vs-side-effect proofs (REV10-01, REV10-03) — in round 1 with §3 and AC-24; the shared C6.0 classifier and AC-25 (REV10-02) in round 1 with §4; the C13 health contract and `LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION` (REV9-03) in round 1 with §1/§3 and its README section in round 3 with §8. The monotonic `reap_cursor` / `inspectSeq` traversal and AC-11(p)(p-ii) (REV13-01) land in round 1 with §3 (the `bookings` column) and round 2 with §6 (`lib/booking/reap.ts`); the `delivery.calendar: 'pending'` envelope value and its C12 status line with AC-23(a) (REV13-02) land in round 2 with §5/§6. The C6.4a finalization witness, the `unfinished_create` carry, and cancel T2′ recovery with AC-11(r-v)(r-vi) (REV14-01) land in round 1 with §3/§4 (the column and the shared predicate) and round 2 with §6 (C6.7). The rev-16 alignments add no step: the notify N-1/N-2/N-3 ordering with AC-11(r-vi-b) (REV15-01) lands in round 2 with §5, the shared `createFinalized` predicate behind C6.3a(1)(ii)/L0/C9 (REV15-02) in round 1 with §4, and the `Idempotency-Key` kind-gate ordering with AC-24(f) (REV15-03) in round 1 with §3 and AC-24 — no new round, no new step.
- Any inspector finding that would require expanding Non-goals is dispositioned `out-of-scope` by the judge and logged for the next PLAN, not built.
- If `npm test` is red at the end of round 3, the PR is labelled `hold` and the PLAN is revised rather than extending rounds.
