## Codex REV2 dispositions

| Finding | Severity | Disposition | Closed by |
|---|---|---|---|
| REV2-01 reservation not durable; pending destinations invisible to conflict/capacity queries | high | accepted — reschedule T1 reserves `[reserved_start, reserved_end)` durably, old occupancy retained until T2/reconciliation, one `hostOccupancy` used by every conflict/capacity/availability query; create-vs-reschedule and reschedule-vs-reschedule interleavings proved | C6.1, C8, AC-4, AC-8; Approach §3 |
| REV2-02 unknown commit interpreted from a plain read while the transaction may still be in flight | high | accepted — Founder policy: no compensation until the original transaction is known finished; reconciler reacquires the per-host advisory lock on a fresh connection under `lock_timeout`, else stays unknown (503); fake models delayed commit completion | C6.5, AC-11, AC-18; Approach §4 |
| REV2-03 no pre-mutation operation identity / intended event id; inserts not recoverable | high | accepted — `pending_op {opId, gen, eventId/fallbackEventId}` persisted before any Google call, client-supplied event ids make `events.insert` idempotent (409 `duplicate` = success), `events.get` reconciles ambiguous outcomes; create becomes two-phase so the lock is never held across Google; accepted-but-lost and process-death scenarios proved for create and fallback insert | C6.2, C6.4, AC-11, AC-18; Approach §4 |
| REV2-04 no ownership generation; superseded worker can undo the winner | high | accepted — `pending_op.gen`; every completing/clearing/compensating update conditioned on `opId`/`gen`; superseded workers reconcile the winner (`operation_superseded`) and never compensate/insert; paused-worker-returns-after-takeover proved | C6.3, AC-12, AC-18; Approach §6 |
| REV2-05 contradictory cancel-vs-reschedule concurrency policy | high | accepted — Founder policy: cancel refuses (`operation_in_progress` + `retryAfterSeconds`) while a live reschedule owns `pending_op`, takes over only a stale one, then cancels the resulting event; ownership re-checked before any fallback insert; interleaving proof rewritten | C6.7, AC-12, C12 pending state; Approach §6 |
| REV2-06 delivery finalisation not tied to the claim that sent | medium | accepted — `attempts` returned by the claim is the generation; every `finalize` requires it; late finalisers after stale-claim takeover proved | C5, AC-13; Approach §5 |
| REV2-07 no `expectedRevision` on notify; revision/action validated separately | medium | accepted (rev 6, REV5-05: the "never sends stale content after a cancel" corollary is withdrawn — claim-time validation orders ledger rows, not Gmail calls) — two request forms: `{ action, expectedRevision }` validated together under the claim lock, and explicit retry-latest `{}` documented; page/client propagates the revision | C2, C5, C12, AC-13, AC-21; Approach §5 |
| REV2-08 id/token migration unspecified | medium | accepted — `Booking.token` distinct from `id` (schema check), `getByToken` for `/b/{token}`, bearer-header transport for `/api/bookings/{id}/*`, explicit `mapBooking`, fixture migration, end-to-end tests with unequal values and id-only rejection | C11, C2, C3, C8, AC-20; Approach §7 |
| REV2-09 create retries can duplicate rows after a lost response | high | accepted — Founder policy: durable client-retained `Idempotency-Key` on `(owner_id, idempotency_key)`; replay reconciles the unfinished create and returns the original booking + token; committed-but-unreconciled recovery from a fresh isolate proved | C9, C6.4, AC-10, AC-11, AC-19; Approach §7 |
| REV2-10 token page has no explicit controls, states, or client methods; reschedule availability undefined | medium | accepted — `BookingControls` with Reschedule/Cancel/Resend, `controlsState` machine, client lifecycle methods with bearer auth, authenticated `GET /api/bookings/{id}/available-times` excluding the booking's own occupancy; interaction tests through real handlers | C2, C12, AC-8, AC-21; Approach §7 |
| REV2-11 legacy one-off link entry point bypasses the durable boundary | medium | accepted — memory mode routes `bookSingleUseLink` through the shared two-phase create and occupancy; pg mode returns 501 `links_not_supported` before any side effect; coverage that it cannot bypass host conflicts or fail after a calendar insert | C10, C2, C3, AC-22; Approach §7 |

Founder locks P1 (per-owner URL + host first name), P2 (guest change path is `/b/{token}`), and P3 (real calendar event + real emails to both parties) are unchanged by every REV2 disposition; REV2-10 only makes P2's page explicit.

## Codex REV3 dispositions

| Finding | Severity | Disposition | Closed by |
|---|---|---|---|
| REV3-01 ownership check released before the fallback insert; DB generations do not fence Google; delayed execution leaves a live event behind a cancellation | high | accepted — **event-id fence**: a takeover never abandons an op and a cancel never completes while an intended id is unresolved; each id is observed present (→ complete, not abandon) or fenced at Google by inserting a `status='cancelled'` placeholder under that id, so any paused or late insert is answered 409 `duplicate`; ambiguous fence → `pending_op` retained, 503; a superseded worker treats a cancelled event behind `AlreadyExists` as supersession. Interleavings pausing after the ownership check and releasing a held Google request after takeover are proved. **Mechanism revised in rev 5 (REV4-01/02):** the placeholder fence is withdrawn; the same interleavings are closed by intended-id retention + reap and ETag version invalidation (C6.3a–c) | C6.3a–c, C6.4, C6.6, C6.7, AC-12(f)–(k), AC-18(iv)–(vi); Approach §4, §6 |
| REV3-02 unowned calendar re-insert on `calendar_state='failed'`; notify can recreate a cancelled event | high | accepted — `calendar_state ∈ {pending, created, failed, deleted}` with a transition per lifecycle outcome; repair is an owned `pending_op.kind='calendar_repair'` begun by notify on confirmed rows only, serialized with reschedule/cancel via `pending_op`; notify on a cancelled row retries cancel emails only and makes zero calendar calls (rev 6, REV5-04: zero `events.insert`/`events.patch`; the C6.3a reap of retired ids is permitted there) | C5, C6.8, C8, AC-13(g), AC-23; Approach §5 |
| REV3-03 create T2′ never sets `latest_action='confirm'`; AC-10(d) state has no valid notify action | medium | accepted — T2′ finalizes the business creation (`latest_action='confirm'`) and records the calendar failure separately; initial delivery and both notify forms proved against that exact persisted state | C6.4, AC-10(d); Approach §5 |
| REV3-04 client clears the create key on every 4xx incl. 409 `operation_in_progress` | high | accepted — key + payload retained on every non-terminal response (`operation_in_progress`, 503, 5xx, network, reload) and cleared only on 201 or a rejection that proves no row exists (400, 409 `slot_unavailable`/`session_full`, 422); same-key replay while the original create is active is defined as 409 `operation_in_progress` | C3, C6.4, C9, AC-19(b)(f′)(g); Approach §7 |
| REV3-05 replay validation compares against mutable `bookings.start` | medium | accepted — immutable `create_fingerprint` (event type, original start, invitee email) persisted in T1 and compared on replay; replay returns the row's current state independently of the comparison | C8, C9, AC-19(e′); Approach §3 |
| REV3-06 reload recovery persists only the key; constructor reset, hidden occupied slot, and elapsed-start gates block reconstruction | medium | accepted — unresolved-submission record `{ key, payload }`; `recovering` state replays before availability loads via `recoverBooking`, bypassing the elapsed/availability gates for the replay only; explicit "Try again" / "Start over"; full reload-after-commit scenario incl. elapsed start | C9, AC-19(h); Approach §7 |
| REV3-07 cancelled panel has no retry after a failed cancellation email | medium | accepted — cancelled panel keeps Reschedule/Cancel absent but renders delivery status and a Retry notification control when delivery is `pending`/`failed`, immediately and after reload, wired to the real notify handler (`{action:'cancel', expectedRevision}` / `{}`) | C12, AC-21(c)(h)(i); Approach §7 |
| REV3-08 duration-stepped slot generator cannot yield overlapping moves | medium | accepted — explicit reschedule candidate policy: `RESCHEDULE_SLOT_INCREMENT_MIN = 15` applied by the booking availability route and the reschedule validation (one generator), current start excluded; public routes unchanged; 09:00 → 09:15 proved as an actual overlapping move | C2, C6.6, C12, AC-8(d), AC-21(a); Approach §3 |
| REV3-09 consumed link rejected before the idempotency lookup; lost 201 becomes 410 | medium | accepted — identical replay on a consumed link reaches the C9 lookup and returns the original booking; a different payload maps to the existing 410; consumption idempotent per `consumedByBookingId`; retained fixture test still holds | C2, C3, C10, AC-22(g)–(i); Approach §7 |
| REV3-10 every `marloBookingId` item excluded regardless of store occupancy; host-moved event accepted over | medium | accepted — a managed item is excluded only when matched to its row's current interval or reservation; unmatched or displaced managed intervals are conservatively external busy; cancelled items never busy; host-moved event proved without synchronization | C7, AC-8(e); Approach §3 |

Founder locks P1–P3 are unchanged by every REV3 disposition: REV3-06/07/08 only make P2's change path and the booking page's recovery complete, and REV3-01/02/10 only make P3's real calendar event impossible to orphan, recreate, or double-book. No REV3 disposition reopens a LIVE-xx, REV1-xx, or REV2-xx closure; REV3-01 strengthens REV2-04/05, REV3-02/03 strengthen REV1-07/REV2-07, REV3-04/05/06 strengthen REV2-09, REV3-07/08 strengthen REV2-10, REV3-09 strengthens REV2-11, and REV3-10 strengthens REV1-03.

## Codex REV4 dispositions

| Finding | Severity | Disposition | Closed by |
|---|---|---|---|
| REV4-01 event-id fence relies on lifetime id retention and guaranteed 409 that Google's contract does not establish | high | accepted — **fence withdrawn**; replaced by **intended-id retention and reap**: an id whose insert attempt is `unresolved` and unobserved is *outstanding*; abandonment/cancel may proceed but must retain it in `unresolved_inserts`; anything that later lands under it is external busy from first sight (C7) and deleted by the next observing call (`If-Match` delete); the superseded worker decides by `events.get`, never by the insert status; ambiguous observation keeps `pending_op` and returns 503; the product guarantee is revised to "no double booking, no persistent orphan"; fakes and adapters model both `collisionMode: 'duplicate'` and `'land'` and every scenario runs under both | Goal 5, Non-goals, C6.2, C6.3, C6.3a, C6.4, C6.6, C6.7, C6.8, C7, C8, AC-8(e), AC-11(j), AC-12(f)–(h), AC-18(iv), AC-23(d); Assumptions; Founder smoke; Approach §4, §6 |
| REV4-02 existing-event mutations (patch/delete/compensation) are unfenced at Google; a paused patch lands after takeover and Google diverges from the booking | high | accepted — `google_event_etag` persisted; `pending_op.etag` snapshot; every `patch`/`delete`/compensation/reap carries `If-Match`; 412 is reconciliation (owner re-read → `events.get` → target/pre-state/foreign rule, at most one observed-etag retry), never a blind retry; **takeover's first Google mutation is a version bump** that invalidates every etag the predecessor holds before any reservation release, completion, abandonment, or delete; delayed move, delayed compensating patch, and delayed compensating delete interleavings proved | C6.3, C6.3c, C6.6, C6.7, C6.8, AC-12(i)–(k), AC-18(v); Approach §4, §6; Risks |
| REV4-03 a definite 4xx on a retry clears uncertainty from an earlier ambiguous attempt; cancel on a `calendar_state='failed'` row skips outstanding inserts | high | accepted — durable append-only `pending_op.attempts` written before every mutation and finalised only by the issuing worker's own response; takeover keeps it; a later attempt never resolves an earlier one; clearing rule: `pending_op` may be nulled only when every id with an `unresolved` insert attempt (other than the resulting `google_event_id`) is moved into `unresolved_inserts`; create T2′ retains accordingly; cancel runs the reap and `events.get` over intended ids on every row including `calendar_state='failed'`; the timeout → takeover-retry-403 → cancel → delayed-insert interleaving is proved | C6, C6.3b, C6.4, C6.6, C6.7, C6.8, AC-11(j)(k), AC-18(vi); Approach §4, §6 |
| REV4-04 notify rejects a stale `calendar_repair` forever; a repair whose insert timed out is unblockable without reschedule/cancel | medium | accepted — notify takes over a **stale** `calendar_repair` under the C6.3 ownership rules and resumes it at the observed step (`events.get` → T2, or insert → T2), then claims emails; non-stale repair and any reschedule/cancel op are still 409 `operation_in_progress`; repair T2 definite failure is 500 `repair_failed` with `pending_op` retained and no compensation, T2 unknown → C6.5; fresh-isolate notify retry after timeout and after T2 failure proved | C2, C5, C6.8, AC-18, AC-23(g)–(i); Approach §5 |
| REV4-05 `delivery.email` undefined when recipient rows are missing (commit-before-first-claim, death between recipients); cancelled panel may lack the promised retry | medium | accepted — `delivery.email` is computed against the required recipient set `{invitee, owner}` for the current pair: `sent` needs both explicit `sent` rows, a missing row is `pending`; one `summarizeDelivery` function serves every envelope on both stores; notify always claims every required recipient (missing → fresh claim, `sent` untouched); commit-before-first-claim and death-between-recipients proved for confirm and cancel, including a fresh cancelled-page load and a successful retry | C3, C5, C12, AC-13(h)(i), AC-21(j); Approach §5 |

Founder locks P1–P3 are unchanged by every REV4 disposition: REV4-01/02/03 only replace the *mechanism* by which P3's real calendar event is kept from being orphaned or double-booked with one Google's contract actually supports, and REV4-04/05 only make P3's email delivery and calendar repair recoverable from any isolate. REV4-01 revises one stated guarantee — "a late insert can never leave a live event behind" becomes "a late insert can never cause a double booking and never persists past the next observing call" — and says so in README. No REV4 disposition reopens a LIVE-xx, REV1-xx, REV2-xx, or REV3-xx closure; REV4-01/02/03 strengthen REV3-01 and REV2-03/04, REV4-04 strengthens REV3-02, and REV4-05 strengthens REV1-07/REV3-07. Rev 6 revises three REV4 mechanisms without reopening the findings — see the REV5 table's last column.

## Codex REV5 dispositions

| Finding | Severity | Disposition | Closed by | Revises |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| REV5-01 observing an event resolved every insert attempt for its id; a successful T2 discarded the ledger; cancel did not retain the live id; one deletion retired the id while another request could still execute | high | accepted — **observation completes ops, never attempts**: an attempt is `applied`/`rejected` only by its own request's response (409 `duplicate` → `rejected`); `unresolved_inserts` holds one entry per unresolved insert attempt `{ attemptId, eventId, opId, gen, issuedAt }`; every clearing update (T2, T2′, cancel T2, repair T2) copies every unresolved attempt, **including those naming the resulting `google_event_id`**; cancel T2 retires the live id so those entries become reap-eligible; a reaped present event retires exactly one entry (the oldest for that id), never the id; held-first-insert → successful retry → cancel → late-first-insert and two-unresolved-requests scenarios added | C6 header, C6.3a, C6.3b, C6.4, C6.6, C6.7, C6.8, C7, C8, AC-8(e), AC-11 criterion + (j)(l)(m), AC-18 (vi), Approach §6, Risks | REV4-01 ("the row's own live `google_event_id` is never listed" withdrawn), REV4-03 ("present resolves every attempt for that id as `applied`" withdrawn; clearing rule now copies attempts for the resulting id too) |
| REV5-02 a failed booking's `google_event_id` could be both reap-listed and repair-adopted; repair T2 did not remove it; even with removal, a paused reaper could delete the repaired event; reap needed no ownership | high | accepted — **mutually exclusive live/retired states, retired absorbing**: live iff `status='confirmed' AND id = google_event_id`; only retired ids are reap-eligible; the eligible set is computed in the same locked read as `status`/`google_event_id`; `google_event_id` only ever receives a fresh id, a cancelled row never returns to confirmed, repair targets only the current live id — **no id is ever promoted from retired back to live**, so a paused reaper can only delete an event nobody will adopt and needs no ownership; repair T2 removes nothing; sequential repair-then-read and paused-reader/paused-reaper interleavings added | C6.3a, C6.6 (fallback retires the old id), C6.8 (repair T2), C7, AC-8(e), AC-23 criterion + (j)(k), Approach §6 | REV4-01 reap procedure (eligibility now live/retired-based) |
| REV5-03 retaining an id cannot establish the promised absence of double-booked calendar intervals: cancel releases the interval, a new booking takes it, the delayed insert lands and overlaps | medium | accepted — **honest narrowing** (Founder's alternative — hold occupancy while an insert may still execute — would be unbounded, since Google bounds nothing about delayed execution; recorded as a Non-goal): the guarantee is (i) never offered as free, (ii) never creates/changes/rejects a booking row, (iii) never persists past the next observing call; a **transient overlap on the host's Google Calendar** is possible when a late insert lands after the interval was released and re-booked, and README says so in those words; new-booking-commits-before-late-insert interleaving added | Goal 5, Non-goals, C6.3a (guarantee), C7, AC-8(e), AC-12 criterion + (l), AC-16, Risks | REV4-01 stated guarantee ("can never cause a double booking" narrowed to "never offered, never a booking, never persistent; transient calendar overlap possible") |
| REV5-04 notify on a cancelled row was required both to make zero calendar calls (C5/C6.8) and to reap retained ids (C6.3a) | medium | accepted — **cleanup ≠ repair**: on a cancelled row notify runs the C6.3a reap of retired ids (get, `If-Match` delete of anything present) and then retries the cancel emails; it never inserts or patches; AC-13(g) reworded to zero insert/patch, AC-13(j) tests a cancelled notify with an actually landed retained event under both collision modes | C2 (notify row), C5, C6.8, AC-13 criterion + (g)(j), AC-23 criterion | REV3-02 / REV4-04 wording ("zero calendar calls on a cancelled row" → "zero inserts/patches; reap permitted") |
| REV5-05 claim-time revision validation cannot prevent an already-claimed confirmation from being sent after a cancellation; the absolute "never send stale content after a cancel" is unsupported | medium | accepted — **documented ordering, absolute claim withdrawn**: an already-claimed send may reach Gmail after a later revision's emails; ledger finalisation stays generation-conditioned; no pre-send re-validation is required or relied on; serialising sends with lifecycle changes is a Non-goal (no queue, no lock across Gmail); README sentence added and tested; AC-13(c) now pauses between the claim commit and the Gmail call and asserts the late send is issued with revision-1 content while the ledger is untouched | Non-goals, C5, AC-13 criterion + (c), AC-16, Risks | REV2-07 wording ("or send stale content after a cancel" withdrawn) |
| REV5-06 the owner-slug validator admitted application-owned roots: owner `b` collides with `/b/[token]`, owner `host` with the middleware-protected `/host` | medium | accepted — `RESERVED_ROOT_SLUGS` (`api`, `b`, `host`, `signin`, `tokens`, `components`) kept complete by a filesystem test over `app/` and `public/`; refused at every boundary: sign-in fails with `owner_slug_reserved` (no owner/token write, `/signin?error=owner_slug_reserved` with copy), both `OwnerStore`s refuse `upsert`, `resolveOwner` → 404 without a store read, `publicBookingPath`/`bookAgainHref` throw, the `[slug]/[event]` page is 404; route-level tests for `b` and `host` including the `middleware.ts` matcher | C1, C2 (booking page row), AC-2 criterion + proof, Approach §2, Assumptions | — (extends REV1-01's slug validation; `demo` stays covered by `owner_slug_taken`) |

Founder locks P1–P3 are unchanged by every REV5 disposition. REV5-03 and REV5-05 each **narrow a stated guarantee** rather than a product lock: P3's real calendar event and real emails are still created for every booking; what changes is that the PLAN no longer claims to prevent a transient overlap on the host's calendar from a Google-delayed insert (REV5-03) or a confirmation that reaches an inbox after a cancellation when its send was already claimed (REV5-05) — both are now stated in README in plain words. REV5-01/02/04 change mechanism only (attempt-level retention, live/retired ids, cleanup-vs-repair), and REV5-06 adds a refusal that no existing owner triggers. No REV5 disposition reopens a LIVE-xx, REV1-xx, REV2-xx, REV3-xx, or REV4-xx closure; the REV4 and REV2/REV3 mechanisms revised by REV5 are listed in the last column above.

## Codex REV6 dispositions

| Finding | Severity | Disposition | Closed by | Revises |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| REV6-01 C6.3a retired "the oldest unresolved entry naming the id" after a deletion without knowing which attempt created the event: a delayed insert response (B's event reaped, A's entry retired, B then retires its own → A executable but unlisted) and two concurrent reapers (2xx and 404 each retiring an entry) both retire attempts that may still execute | high | accepted — **durable attempt↔event attribution plus one idempotent cleanup rule**: every `events.insert` sets `extendedProperties.private.marloAttemptId` next to `marloBookingId`; a reaper that observed a present event deletes it with `If-Match` and, on 2xx/404/410, calls `retireAttempt(bookingId, event.marloAttemptId)` — exactly the attempt that provably executed, never the oldest, never an unidentified one; retirement is idempotent by `attemptId` and is the single rule used by the C6.3a reap, C7 reap-on-observation, cancel (which now `events.get`s the live id before deleting it when an entry names it), notify, and the issuing/superseded worker's own cleanup (its own 2xx → delete → 2xx/404/410 → retire *its own* entry; 412 → re-observe, a different `marloAttemptId` means its event is already gone); an attempt whose request may still execute is never touched by a deletion it did not cause; adapters/fakes carry and return `marloAttemptId`; delayed-response (both orderings) and concurrent-reaper interleavings added | Goal 5, C2 (notify row), C6.3a (reap procedure, superseded worker, adapters), C6.3b, C6.7, C7, AC-11 criterion + (n)(o), AC-12 criterion, Approach §4/§6, Founder smoke, Risks | REV5-01 ("one deletion retires one entry — the oldest for that id" withdrawn; a deletion retires the attributed entry, and a 404/410 after an observed present event retires it too, idempotently); REV4-01 superseded-worker cleanup (now through the shared `retireAttempt`) |
| REV6-02 the reap always took the first five eligible ids in list order while absent ids stay listed, so a sixth retired id is never inspected even after it lands; C6.7 required a definite observation of every retired id before cancel T2, which the five-id procedure cannot satisfy | medium | accepted — **persisted fair traversal**: each `unresolved_inserts` entry carries `inspectedAt`; the eligible set is ordered by smallest `inspectedAt` (`null` first) then `issuedAt`, the first `REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST` ids form the batch, and `inspectedAt = now()` is written for them in the same locked transaction before any Google call, so absent ids rotate to the back, concurrent reapers pick disjoint batches, and every eligible id is inspected within ⌈|eligible| / 5⌉ observing calls; C7 batches the observed items the same way; **cancel's T2 precondition is narrowed to the taken-over op's intended ids** (≤ 3), the live id's delete, and its bumps — the retired-id reap is bounded best-effort cleanup that is never a precondition (retired is absorbing, so a later observing call reaps any landing), and a row with six retained ids is cancelled in one attempt; >5-retained-ids scenario added | C6 header, Non-goals, C6.3a, C6.3b, C6.7, C7, C8, AC-11 criterion + (p), AC-12 criterion, AC-18 criterion, Approach §6, Risks | REV5-01/REV4-03 cancel precondition ("every retired id named by `unresolved_inserts` received a definite `events.get` answer" withdrawn from the T2 gate); REV4-01 "in list order" traversal |
| REV6-03 C6.4 requires the conflict check before create T1 commits, C7 requires live `events.list` data for it, C6 forbids the lock across Google calls, and AC-11 forbids any Google call before T1 commits — leaving no permitted place to obtain external occupancy (the shipped chain `booking.ts:335 → listAvailableTimes → slots.ts:42` reads the calendar before the row exists) | medium | accepted — **reads distinguished from mutations**: an **R0 external-availability read** (`events.list` per C7) runs before create/reschedule T1 outside any transaction and lock (fail-closed → 503, nothing written); T1 then re-checks the idempotency key (a replay never consults R0), rejects an externally busy interval with 409 `slot_unavailable` **without inserting a row, writing an op, reserving, or consuming the key**, and re-checks managed occupancy under the lock (authoritative); the C6 header states the rule (reads may precede T1; a mutation of the booking's own event only after the recording transaction committed; C7's reap of *other* rows during a read is unowned cleanup); AC-11 now forbids Google *mutations* before T1 commits; AC-4(e) proves external-busy rejection with zero rows, key not consumed, replay unaffected, reschedule equally; the snapshot window is an accepted, documented risk | C6 header, C6.4, C6.6, AC-4 criterion + (e), AC-11 criterion + (c)(d), AC-16, Approach §3, §8, Risks | REV1-10 / REV2-03 AC-11 wording ("No Google call happens before T1 commits" → "no Google *mutation*") |
| REV6-04 "at most one duplicate per recipient per 2-minute stale window" does not follow from claim generations: workers claiming generations at minutes 0, 2, 4, 6 that all resume at minute 7 deliver four copies in one window; generation-conditioned finalisation protects the ledger, not the send count | medium | accepted — **the real bound is documented, the false one withdrawn**: claim generations bound *acquisition* (one new generation per row per stale window, and only when a caller invokes notify or a lifecycle send — no background retrier), so `attempts` is the exact, audit-visible upper bound on copies; delivery *timing* is unbounded and stalled generations can deliver clustered copies; **no issuance-rate mechanism is added** — a local send deadline would narrow but never close the cluster (the stall can occur between the check and the Gmail call), so it would be a bound in name only; a wall-clock delivery bound is recorded as a Non-goal; README states the real bound; AC-13(k) proves four generations delivering four copies inside one window with a generation-correct ledger | Non-goals, C5, AC-13 criterion + (k), AC-16, Approach §5, §8, Risks | REV1-07 / C5 ("at most one duplicate per recipient per 2-minute stale window" withdrawn) |
| REV6-05 the mandated README sentence "the most recent email describes the booking's current state" is false in the AC-13(c) scenario it immediately follows, where the most recently received email describes a confirmed booking that is cancelled | low | accepted — the clause is **withdrawn**; README now says *"The order in which emails arrive does not tell you the booking's current state; the booking page linked in every email is the authoritative status."*; to make that pointer reachable, **every email body carries the absolute `/b/{token}` URL** (built from the request origin, no new env var) with one sentence that the booking page always shows the current status — the single product-visible addition of this revision, required by the finding's fix; AC-13(c) asserts both sentences and that README nowhere claims the most recent email describes the state, plus the link in all six bodies; AC-16's doc-presence test updated | C4, C5, AC-13 criterion + (c), AC-16, Approach §5, §8, Founder smoke, Risks | REV5-05 README sentence (second clause replaced) |

Founder locks P1–P3 are unchanged by every REV6 disposition. REV6-04 **narrows a stated guarantee** (the per-window duplicate bound becomes a per-retry bound with unbounded delivery timing) and REV6-05 adds one product-visible element to P3's emails — the booking-page link — without which the corrected README sentence would point recipients nowhere; REV6-01/02/03 change mechanism only (attributed retirement, fair traversal with a narrowed cancel gate, and a read-before-T1 with a locked re-check). No REV6 disposition reopens a LIVE-xx, REV1-xx, REV2-xx, REV3-xx, REV4-xx, or REV5-xx closure; the REV5/REV4/REV1 mechanisms revised by REV6 are listed in the last column above.

## Codex REV7 dispositions

| Finding | Severity | Disposition | Closed by | Revises |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| REV7-01 C6.3a's superseded-worker cleanup deletes the winner's live event: A's fallback insert response is delayed, B takes over, bumps, adopts A's event and later moves it; A's 2xx sees "the row no longer shows my target", deletes with its stale etag, gets 412, finds its own `marloAttemptId` with a new etag, and the prescribed observed-etag retry deletes B's live event | high | accepted — **response reconciliation and cleanup eligibility are two determinations from one locked read**: the response (200 / `operation_superseded`) is decided by "does the row show my target"; cleanup eligibility is decided **only** by the id's state — **live** (`status='confirmed' AND google_event_id = id`) → never eligible, whatever the interval (the winner adopted it and may have moved it under the same id); **intended id of an open op** → never eligible (the winner may adopt it); **retired and the worker's own attempt retained** in `unresolved_inserts` → eligible; retired but already retired-by-attribution → nothing to do. Only an eligible attempt with a definite 2xx enters cleanup, and the observed-etag retry after 412 exists only inside that eligibility, made safe by *retired is absorbing* (REV5-02). The rule "no worker ever deletes an event that is not its own owned live id or a retired id named by `unresolved_inserts`" now holds for the superseded worker too. Delayed-response → adoption → subsequent-move interleaving added (AC-12(m)), AC-11(n) realigned (a retired-but-not-retained attempt issues zero deletes), AC-12(f)/AC-18(iv)/AC-23(d) annotated with the eligibility that makes their deletes legal | Banner, C6.3a (superseded worker), AC-11(n), AC-12 criterion + (f)(m), AC-18 criterion + (iv), AC-23(d), Approach §4/§6, Risks | REV6-01 superseded-worker cleanup ("`google_event_id = id` and the row shows its target → success; otherwise → … it deletes it itself" withdrawn: the interval is no longer an input to cleanup; the observed-etag retry is confined to retired-and-retained ids); REV4-01 superseded-worker side |
| REV7-02 C6.4 orders R0 `events.list` before T1's idempotency lookup and returns 503 when R0 fails, so a completed booking whose 201 was lost cannot replay during a Calendar outage although no calendar work is needed — contradicting C9/AC-19's zero-Google-call replay | medium | accepted — **L0, a locked idempotency lookup before any Google traffic**: an existing key validates its fingerprint (422 on mismatch) and returns or resumes from the row with zero `events.list` calls (`pending_op` null → 201; non-stale create → 409 `operation_in_progress`; stale → takeover whose only Google calls are `events.get`/`events.insert` on its own intended id); only a **missing** key runs R0, followed by T1's **mandatory second lookup** (a concurrent same-key request can commit between L0 and T1), external-busy check, and managed occupancy check. Replay-with-`events.list`-throwing tests added for completed, non-stale pending, stale pending, missing key, fingerprint mismatch, and concurrent same-key requests | Banner, C6.4, C9, AC-4(e), AC-11 criterion, AC-19 criterion + (i), Approach §3/§4, Risks | REV6-03 ordering ("R0 → T1 with the lookup inside T1" → "L0 → R0 (missing keys only) → T1 with the second lookup"); REV6-03's "a replay of an existing row never consults X" is kept and now actually reachable without R0 |
| REV7-03 C6.6 reconciles a stale reschedule and then proceeds with the incoming request as a new op; a same-target retry after death between the patch and T2 completes the move and is then rejected `slot_unavailable` (C12 excludes the current start) for an operation that succeeded, and the client's slot-conflict branch does not reload | medium | accepted — **reconciliation is compared with the request**: (i) the taken-over reschedule **completed** and its `newStart` equals the request's `start` → **same-target recovery**: 200 with the completed outcome (current `booking`, the completing T2's `delivery`), no new op, no reservation, no availability check, the current-start exclusion not consulted; (ii) completed to a **different** start → 409 `booking_changed` with the current booking (the reconciliation stands; the caller's `expectedRevision` predates a move the guest has not seen; the page reloads per C12); (iii) abandoned, or a create/repair → proceed as a new op. Retry-after-death-between-patch-and-T2 tests added for same target, different target, abandoned + same target, and other-kind callers; page-level Try-again test added | Banner, C6.6, AC-18 criterion + (vii), AC-21(k), Approach §6, Risks | REV2-04 / C6.6 takeover sequence ("reconcile that op first, then proceed with this reschedule as a new op" is now conditional on what the reconciliation achieved) |
| REV7-04 the C5 claim SQL re-claims any `failed` row immediately while only `claimed` rows carry the 2-minute age condition, contradicting rev 7's acquisition bound, AC-13, and the mandated README sentence "a new retry is accepted at most once per stale window"; the stalled-generation test does not exercise the branch | medium | accepted — **honest documentation, no invented rate limit** (the Founder's preferred branch: it matches the implementation intent and keeps offline tests simple): the 2-minute window governs **takeover of a `claimed` row** only; a **`failed`** row is re-claimable **immediately**, and what keeps `attempts` an exact bound on copies is the **finalisation rule** now stated normatively — `failed` is written only for a **definite** Gmail refusal (4xx other than 429, `host_not_connected`), an ambiguous outcome (timeout/5xx/429/network/death) leaves the row `claimed` — so a failed generation delivered nothing. Both stores implement exactly the C5 predicate; the README sentence is replaced ("a retry of an email whose earlier send is still unresolved is accepted at most once per 2-minute stale window; an email that Gmail definitely refused can be retried immediately"), the unqualified rev-7 clause is withdrawn and its absence tested; repeated failed-claim retries inside one window (and the ambiguous-outcome contrast) added on both stores | Banner, Non-goals, C5, AC-13 criterion + (l), AC-16, Approach §5/§8, Risks | REV6-04 acquisition bound ("a new generation can be acquired at most once per 2-minute stale window" narrowed to `claimed` rows); REV1-07 / C5 README sentence (second clause replaced) |

Founder locks P1–P3 are unchanged by every REV7 disposition. None narrows a product guarantee: REV7-01 and REV7-03 fix mechanisms that could delete a live event or reject a move that happened; REV7-02 restores a replay path the rev-7 ordering had blocked; REV7-04 states the existing claim predicate precisely and adds the finalisation rule that makes it honest, without adding a rate limit. No REV7 disposition reopens a LIVE-xx, REV1-xx, REV2-xx, REV3-xx, REV4-xx, REV5-xx, or REV6-xx closure; the REV6/REV4/REV2/REV1 mechanisms revised by REV7 are listed in the last column above.

## Codex REV8 dispositions

| Finding | Severity | Disposition | Closed by | Revises |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| REV8-01 C9 treats a conflict response as sufficient to discard an unresolved submission while C6.4 leaves earlier requests unfenced before T1: A (key K, slot S) passes R0 and pauses before T1; S is taken; retry B with K finds no row → `slot_unavailable`; the client clears K and books T under a new key; the S booking is cancelled; A resumes, passes T1, and inserts the S booking — two bookings for one guest who followed the prescribed recovery. The locked lookup proves absence at an instant, not that an earlier request cannot commit later | high | accepted — **a terminal rejection is a durable fence**: the rejecting T1 writes `create_rejections (owner_id, idempotency_key, create_fingerprint, reason)` in the **same locked transaction** that answers 409 `slot_unavailable`/`session_full`; L0 checks the fence before any Google traffic and T1's mandatory second lookup checks it again, so the paused A's T1 finds the fence and answers the same 409 with zero `INSERT`, zero `pending_op`, zero `events.insert` (`create_fenced`); fence and row are mutually exclusive by the lock; an unknown fence commit resolves through C6.5 over both tables. The fence is per `(key, fingerprint)` so the C10 link path may still book another slot. The **no-key-consumption contract is restated precisely**: a terminal 409 consumes nothing the client can reuse but does consume the `(key, payload)` pair on the server, which is exactly why the client mints a new key; and the client **drops late responses for a key that is no longer its unresolved record** (`stale_response_ignored`). The alternative (retaining the submission until every outstanding attempt has a terminal outcome) was rejected: the client cannot enumerate server-side attempts, so it would have to hold the guest indefinitely. The full interleaving (pre-T1 pause → retry conflict → new submission at another slot → original resumes) is proved server-side in AC-11(q), through the form in AC-19(j), and for links in AC-22(j) | C3, C6.4, C6.5, C8, C9, AC-4(e), AC-11(q), AC-19, AC-22(j); Approach §3, §4, §7; Risks | C6.4 / C9 / AC-4(e) rev-6 "no key consumed" rejection (REV6-03) — a rejection now writes a fence row; the key itself is still not consumed |
| REV8-02 C6.3a's superseded-worker reconciliation and Approach §4 return **200** when a create worker finds `latest_action='confirm'`, but C3, C9, and AC-10 require 201 and the shipped client (`lib/api/client.ts`) treats only 201 as create success, so a delayed insert response whose booking the takeover completed sends the form into failure/recovery for a confirmed booking | medium | accepted — **reconciliation preserves the calling route's success status**: C6.3 and C6.3a(1) return "that outcome with the calling route's status" — **201** with the complete C3 envelope for create and create replay (owner-scoped, legacy, and C10 link routes), **200** for reschedule, cancel, and notify/repair; `reconcileSuperseded` returns `'success'`, not a status, and each route maps it. AC-11(r) proves the delayed create insert response through the real create handler **and the real client** (the form's own submission is the delayed worker and navigates on the 201), plus the 200 cases for reschedule and cancel | C6.3, C6.3a, AC-11(r); Approach §4 | C6.3 (REV2-04) and C6.3a (REV7-01) response wording "200 as if it had done the work" |
| REV8-03 C12's `pending` state permits one automatic retry but defines no transition when that retry also returns `operation_in_progress` (another caller took the stale op over immediately before it and renewed the window): no request in flight, no retry left, controls disabled at "Working on it…" forever; C9 has a manual retry after exhaustion for create, C12 had none | medium | accepted — **a defined exit: `stalled`**. When the single automatic retry is also answered `operation_in_progress` the page leaves `pending` for `stalled`: no request in flight, "Another change to this booking is still finishing.", and two explicit actions — **Try again** (the identical request with the **original** `expectedRevision`, re-entering `pending` with one fresh automatic retry; a booking changed meanwhile is reconciled by the existing `conflict` handling — `booking_changed` → reload → re-confirm — never by a silent revision substitution) and **Reload** (`getBooking` → `idle` with the fresh revision). The page can never remain disabled with nothing in flight. AC-21(l) proves two consecutive `operation_in_progress` responses followed by recovery through Try again (→ `booking_changed` → reload → success), through Reload, directly (the other op changed no revision), and with a second stall | C12, AC-21(l); Approach §7; Risks | C12 `pending` (REV2-10) — gains an exit; C9's manual-retry-after-exhaustion now has a C12 counterpart |

Founder locks P1–P3 are unchanged by every REV8 disposition. None narrows a product guarantee: REV8-01 closes a double-booking path the prescribed recovery flow could reach and restates the key-consumption contract precisely rather than weakening it; REV8-02 makes a confirmed booking reach the client as the success it is; REV8-03 gives P2's change path an exit it lacked. No REV8 disposition reopens a LIVE-xx, REV1-xx, REV2-xx, REV3-xx, REV4-xx, REV5-xx, REV6-xx, or REV7-xx closure; the mechanisms revised by REV8 are listed in the last column above.

## Codex REV9 dispositions

| Finding | Severity | Disposition | Closed by | Revises |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| REV9-01 C6.3a(1) decided a superseded create's success by `latest_action='confirm'`, so a same-key create replay whose insert response is held answers 409 `operation_superseded` if the winner's booking was rescheduled or cancelled before the held response arrives, although C9 requires that replay to return 201 with the original id/token and the current state, and the shipped client accepts create success only on 201; AC-11(r) covered completion without an intervening lifecycle change | medium | accepted — **create success is reconciled from immutable identity and completed business creation, never from `latest_action`**: the locked read must return the row whose `owner_id`, `idempotency_key`, `create_fingerprint` (written once in T1, REV3-05) and app-generated `id` are this worker's, with `pending_op` no longer naming the create's `opId` (cleared by T2, T2′, or a completing takeover — a create is never abandoned); both hold → **201** with the C3 envelope of the row's **current** state, exactly what a C9 replay returns (rescheduled → `start = T`, `revision = 2`; cancelled → `status='cancelled'`, `delivery.calendar='deleted'`); the same `opId` under a higher `gen` (takeover still running) → the non-terminal 409 `operation_in_progress` so the client replays into the completed booking; the identity-mismatch branch is defensive and also non-terminal for C9. **Cleanup eligibility stays the separate C6.3a(2) decision** and is unchanged: an intervening patch keeps the id live (zero deletes), an intervening cancel retires the id with the attempt already attributed and retired (zero deletes), a fallback reschedule leaves it retired-and-retained (the worker removes only its own event). `reconcileSuperseded` implements the rule for create via `createIdentityMatches`; the C9 replay and the superseded-worker path can no longer disagree about whether a booking exists. AC-11(r-ii) (intervening reschedule, incl. the fallback sub-variant), (r-iii) (intervening cancel), and (r-iv) (takeover still running → `operation_in_progress` → replay → 201) prove it through the real handlers **and the real client** (the form's own submission is the delayed worker and navigates on the 201), with the C9 replay envelope asserted deep-equal to the superseded worker's | C6.3, C6.3a(1)(2), C6.4, C9, AC-11(r-ii..iv); Approach §4; Risks | C6.3a(1) create test `latest_action='confirm'` (REV7-01/REV8-02) — replaced by the identity rule; C9 client list gains `operation_superseded`-on-create as non-terminal |
| REV9-02 the serialization and occupancy model used one `hostId` while the retained collective entry point assigns several: existing writers persist `booking.hostIds`, readers count secondary-host occupancy, and create acquires all assigned-host locks, but C8 has no durable host-assignment set and the PLAN supplied neither a collective guard nor a migration of this shared boundary — a collective booking organized by A with participant B could race a single-host booking for B under different locks or lose B's occupancy after a fresh-isolate read; C7 retains collective availability, so a Non-goal alone did not resolve the existing caller | medium | accepted — **the retained boundary is defined explicitly and honestly (C6.9)**. Memory mode: a collective row assigns every host in `hostIds`; every C6 operation on it (create, reschedule, cancel, notify/repair, reap) acquires the **same per-host mutexes** single-host ops use, one per assigned host, in **sorted `hostId` order** (deadlock-free; the existing `rescheduleBooking`/`cancelBooking`, which lock only `hostId`, are brought under the rule); C6.1 occupancy for host B counts every confirmed row that **assigns** B plus its retained old interval and reservation, through the single `hostOccupancy` (`extraBusyForHosts`/`listConfirmedBookingsForHost` become wrappers over it), so collective-vs-single-host overlaps conflict in both directions and a collective reschedule reserves its destination for every assigned host; calendar/email behaviour of the memory-mode collective path is unchanged. Pg/live mode: the durable schema carries one `host_id` and no assignment set, so rather than persist a collective row that would lose its secondary hosts' occupancy after a fresh-isolate read, **every collective operation is refused 501 `collective_not_supported` before any side effect** (before any lock, store read, L0/R0, `freeBusy`, mutation, or email; no fence, no key consumption — non-terminal for C9), no collective event type is materialized at sign-in or seeded, and durable collective support stays a Non-goal, restated so it fences rather than waves. AC-24 proves the collective-vs-single-host overlap in both directions and under interleavings (paused T1, reservation on both hosts, cancel in flight), the sorted acquisition of overlapping host sets, the lifecycle locks, and the pg refusal with an empty statement log | Non-goals, C3, C6 intro, C6.1, C6.9, C7, C9, AC-24; Approach §3; Risks | C6 intro / C6.1 single-`hostId` wording (REV1-08, REV2-01) — extended to assigned hosts in memory mode; Non-goals collective bullet (rev 1) — made precise. **Superseded in rev 11** by the Product bar lock: the memory-mode collective boundary is withdrawn as scope (collective and group are memory-only fixtures outside the C6 contract) and the pg refusal is re-stated as post-catalog-resolution (C6.9, AC-24; REV10 dispositions) |
| REV9-03 AC-15 required `/api/health` to report the schema as current/behind/missing "without touching the network" while C8 discovers migration state by querying `schema_migrations` on first store use; in a fresh serverless isolate whose first request is health no cached observation exists and `DATABASE_URL` cannot distinguish an empty database from a migrated one; a fake preconfigured as "behind" did not establish how production could satisfy both requirements | medium | accepted — **the bounded database readiness query is chosen (C13)**: in pg mode `/api/health` issues exactly one `SELECT version FROM schema_migrations ORDER BY version DESC LIMIT 1` per request, bounded by `HEALTH_DB_TIMEOUT_MS = 2000` (client deadline + per-query `statement_timeout`), and derives `schema` from that result alone — `current` / `behind` (against the shared `LATEST_SCHEMA_VERSION`, filesystem-asserted) / `missing` (zero rows or `42P01`) / `unknown` (error or deadline, with `db: "unreachable"`); it never calls `assertMigrated()`, reads no cached observation, caches nothing, and is therefore answerable as the **first request of a fresh isolate**; `ok` iff `schema ∈ {current, n/a}` and HTTP 200 iff `ok`, else 503, so an unmigrated or unreachable database fails the post-deploy check honestly. Google/Gmail calls and any other `fetch` remain forbidden; `calendar`/`email` are configuration. Memory mode performs zero I/O. The alternative ("unknown until a DB check occurs") was rejected because it would make the post-deploy health check pass on a database the store then refuses to serve. Offline `npm test` stays green: the health tests run on a fresh module instance per case against the pg fake `Queryable` (current, behind, `42P01`, zero rows, `ECONNREFUSED`, and a stalled query under the fake clock) with the fetch guard armed, and assert exactly one statement in the fake's log | Non-goals, C8, C13, AC-15; Approach §1, §3, §8; Verification smoke; Risks | AC-15 "without touching the network" (LIVE-09, REV1-11) — narrowed to "no Google/Gmail/`fetch`; one bounded DB query"; AC-15 proof (pg fake behind) — extended to the full C13 matrix on a fresh isolate |

Founder locks P1–P3 are unchanged by every REV9 disposition. (Rev 11: REV9-02's memory-mode boundary is superseded by the Product bar lock — see the REV10 dispositions; REV9-01 and REV9-03 stand.) No REV9 disposition reopens a LIVE-xx, REV1-xx, REV2-xx, REV3-xx, REV4-xx, REV5-xx, REV6-xx, REV7-xx, or REV8-xx closure; the mechanisms revised by REV9 are listed in the last column above.

## Codex REV10 dispositions (rev 11 — closed by the Product bar, not by new mechanism)

| Finding | Severity | Disposition | Closed by | Revises |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| REV10-01 C6.1 permitted only the booking's own occupancy exclusion and C6.4 rejected managed overlaps before the capacity check, so the second attendee of a capacity-3 group session was refused `slot_unavailable`, contradicting AC-8; the existing implementation instead excludes the whole event type and counts exact starts, which is unsafe under C12's 15-minute grid (a lone group booking moved to 09:15–09:45 is ignored by a public create at 09:30) | medium | **closed by scope-out (Product bar lock §1).** Group (capacity-N) sessions are dropped from LIVE-INT: the live path is single-host, one attendee per slot, and C6.1 occupancy is exclusive — no capacity-sharing rule, group exception, or group grid rule is defined for it; `session_full` is unreachable in pg/live mode; AC-3, AC-4, AC-8, AC-10, and AC-18 no longer require capacity-N behaviour. In memory mode the group type stays the existing fixture under its existing tests, outside the C6 contract (C6.9), and no interleaving involving it is claimed. The suggested group exception (shared capacity for identical event-type and interval, busy otherwise) is a valid design for a later group slice and is logged, not built | Product bar lock §1, Non-goals, C1, C3, C6.1, C6.4, C6.9, C8, AC-3, AC-4, AC-8, AC-10, AC-18, AC-24; Approach §3 | C6.1 "group count" wording (REV2-01); AC-8 group clauses (LIVE-05); the rev-10 C6.9 memory-mode boundary (REV9-02) — withdrawn as scope |
| REV10-02 Calendar HTTP 429 had conflicting outcomes: Approach §4 classified it ambiguous while C6.4 defined definite failure as "4xx other than 409" and C6.3b as "non-409 4xx", so a lifecycle step could mark a 429 attempt `rejected`, clear `pending_op`, and discard an attempt the adapter contract required to retain; a later cancel followed by delayed insertion then left an event no entry could reap | medium | **closed by unification (Product bar lock §2).** One shared classification, C6.0 (`classifyCalendarError`, `lib/google/errors.ts`), is the only status-code contract for C6.2–C6.8 and both adapters: 429 is `ambiguous`, never `definite`; an ambiguous outcome leaves the attempt `unresolved` and `pending_op` in place, writes no T2′, releases nothing, and answers 503; takeover keeps the ledger, the clearing update copies the attempt into `unresolved_inserts`, cancel T2 retains it, and the reap attributes and retires it if the insert lands. Every "definite"/"ambiguous" phrase in C6.2, C6.3a, C6.3b, C6.3c, C6.4, C6.6, C6.7, and C6.8 now names C6.0 and excludes 429 explicitly. AC-25 proves the classifier table, the create-insert 429, the 429 → stale → cancel-takeover → late-landing → reap interleaving (with a negative control showing a definite classification would lose the entry), patch/delete/bump 429, and mock/live parity. No new concurrency guarantee is introduced — the frozen retention/reap bar is applied to one more status code | C6.0, C6.2, C6.3a, C6.3b, C6.3c, C6.4, C6.6, C6.7, C6.8, AC-25; Approach §4; Risks | C6.2 "definite non-409 4xx" (REV2-03); C6.3a/C6.3b attempt resolution wording (REV4-03, REV5-01); C6.4 definite/ambiguous enumeration (REV1-10) |
| REV10-03 C6.9 required the collective refusal before any store read and AC-24(g) an empty SQL log, while C1/AC-3 require owner and event-type resolution from Postgres in a fresh isolate; the specified 501 with zero `SELECT`s could not be established on the production resolution path (an owner-scoped request either needs catalog `SELECT`s to learn the kind or finds no event and 404s) | medium | **closed by Product drop + catalog/side-effect distinction (Product bar lock §1; C6.9).** No `group` or `collective` type is seeded or materialized in pg mode, so the production path for such a slug is the ordinary 404 `event_type_not_found` after read-only catalog `SELECT`s. Catalog resolution (owner, event type, schedule; the token lookup for `/b/{token}`) is explicitly permitted and distinguished from booking side effects; the 501 `group_not_supported` / `collective_not_supported` refusal fires immediately after resolution and before any advisory lock, `bookings`/`create_rejections`/`notification_deliveries` write, `FOR UPDATE` read, L0/R0, `freeBusy`, `events.list`, mutation, or email, with no key consumption. The rev-10 "zero `SELECT`s" / "before any store read" wording is withdrawn as unsatisfiable. AC-24 now proves the 404 production path (statement log = catalog `SELECT`s only, exact SQL) and the 501 fixture-resolution path (fake catalog pre-populated directly, same zero-side-effect assertions), plus seed/materialization containing no such kind and the defensive `/b/{token}` branch | C6.9, C2 (404 codes), C3, C9, AC-24, AC-19 matrix; Approach §3 | C6.9 "before any store read" and AC-24(g) "empty statement log" (REV9-02) |

Founder locks P1–P3 are unchanged by every REV10 disposition. None adds product scope or a concurrency guarantee: REV10-01 and REV10-03 remove group and collective from the live path (the Product bar's own decision) and state the 404/501 boundary the production resolution path can actually satisfy; REV10-02 applies the already-frozen retention/reap bar to 429 through one shared classifier. Prior closures stand except where the table's last column names them; the only superseded closure is REV9-02's memory-mode collective boundary, withdrawn as scope. Any further finding that would widen the Product bar — a group capacity rule, a durable collective, occupancy held for a possibly-executing insert, ordered email issuance, or a 429 fast-fail — is `out-of-scope` for this slice unless the Founder reopens it.

## Codex REV13 dispositions (rev 14 — contract alignment inside the locked Product bar)

| Finding | Severity | Disposition | Closed by | Revises |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| REV13-01 C6.3a ordered the retained-id traversal by `inspectedAt` and stamped `inspectedAt = now()`, so under an unchanging clock six eligible ids collapse to one tie class after two calls and the `issuedAt` tie-break pins F1..F5 forever — F6 can land without ever being inspected again, contradicting AC-11(p)'s coverage assertion; the host lock makes the writes serial but does not make the timestamps strictly increasing. AC-11(p) also asserted disjoint concurrent batches, which six eligible ids and a batch of five cannot produce | medium | **accepted — ordering key replaced by a monotonic cursor; coverage wording corrected; no new mechanism or guarantee.** The reap's ordering key becomes **`inspectSeq`**, drawn from `bookings.reap_cursor bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` — a per-row sequence advanced once per batched id, in batch order, in the *same* locked transaction that selects the batch and before any Google call. Ordering is `inspectSeq` nulls-first, then `issuedAt`, then `eventId` (a total order); `inspectedAt = now()` is still written but is explicitly a diagnostic that **orders nothing**, so a frozen fake clock, a coarse timestamp, or a backwards NTP step can no longer give two inspections the same mark or pin a batch at the head. The coverage claim is restated as what holds: every eligible id is inspected within ⌈\|eligible\| / `REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST`⌉ consecutive observing calls, and **the union of any two consecutive observing calls** covers every eligible id while \|eligible\| ≤ 2 × `REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST`. Disjointness of two concurrent reapers is qualified to \|eligible\| ≥ `2 × REAP_MAX_PER_REQUEST` = 10; below it the batches overlap by construction (six ids, batch five → four shared), which is harmless — the reapers are serialized by the host lock, the second batch *begins* with the ids the first did not take, and a repeat `events.get`/delete finds the id absent and retires nothing (`retireAttempt` is idempotent by `attemptId`, REV6-01). AC-11(p) is rewritten against `inspectSeq` and gains **(p-ii)**: the clock frozen for the whole case, all six inserts held so every get is a 404, six consecutive `GET /b/{token}` calls asserting five fresh `inspectSeq` stamps each (`reap_cursor` 5,10,…,30) and no id skipped across any two consecutive calls, then F6 released (`'land'`) with the clock still frozen — the next call that batches it deletes it once with `If-Match` and retires exactly that attempt. The product bar is untouched: same batch size, same eligibility rule, same attribution, same transient-overlap and reap-is-never-a-T2-precondition statements; only the sort key and the coverage wording change | C6.3a fair-order clause; C6 header entry shape; C6.7 batch mention; C7 reap-on-observation; C8 `bookings` (`reap_cursor`, entry shape); Approach §6 `selectBatch`; Assumptions risk bullet; AC-11(p) + new (p-ii); Round budget; banner | C6.3a `inspectedAt` ordering and the "disjoint batches whenever more than five ids are eligible" / "within any two consecutive observing calls" wording (REV6-02) |
| REV13-02 C3's `delivery.calendar` union omitted `pending`, but create T1 persists `calendar_state='pending'` (C6.8) and AC-23(a) requires the envelope to mirror every row of that transition table; a token-authenticated read taken while a create is awaiting Google therefore had a persisted state the declared response type could not express, and neither the read contract nor the client contract said what such a read returns | medium | **accepted — `pending` added to the envelope and its rendering fixed; minimal rule, no new product UX.** `delivery.calendar` becomes `"created" \| "failed" \| "skipped" \| "deleted" \| "pending"`, so the envelope mirrors **every** state of `calendar_state` and AC-23(a)'s assertion is satisfiable for the create-T1 row. C3 defines the value precisely: it means *the calendar event is still being created and its outcome is not yet known*; it is returned **only** by the read surfaces (`GET /api/bookings/{id}` with the bearer token, and `/b/{token}`) on a row whose create T1 committed with the calendar outcome unresolved, and **never** on a create 201 (issued only after T2 → `created` or T2′ → `failed`) or on a reschedule/cancel/repair 200. Rendering is a **status line and nothing else** (C12): "Calendar invite is still being created.", `controlsState` stays `idle`, Reschedule/Cancel/Resend stay enabled (the C6 rules already refuse or take over an open create op — the page must not second-guess them), **no** repair or retry-calendar control (repair is reachable only from `calendar_state='failed'`, C6.8), no polling, no automatic retry; a reload re-reads the row. `pending` is never an error and never populates `delivery.errors`. AC-23(a) proves it end to end: insert held after T1 → both read surfaces return `pending` and render the line with zero extra Google calls; insert released and T2 completed → the same read returns `created`. No state machine, route, transition, or control is added, and the Product bar is untouched | C3 `delivery.calendar` union + new "Pending-create reads" paragraph; C6.8 mirror sentence; C12 calendar-status bullet; AC-23(a); Round budget; banner | C3 `delivery.calendar` union (REV1-06, REV3-02); AC-23(a) "mirrors each" |

Founder locks P1–P3 and the rev-11 Product bar are unchanged by both REV13 dispositions. Neither adds product scope, a concurrency guarantee, or new UX — Codex raised both as contract-alignment findings and the closures stay inside that: REV13-01 swaps one ordering key for a clock-independent one and states the coverage and disjointness claims at the strength they actually hold, leaving batch size, eligibility, attribution, retention, and the transient-overlap bar exactly as frozen; REV13-02 widens one response union by the one state the database already persists and pins its rendering to a single read-only status line. Prior closures stand. Any further finding that would widen the Product bar remains `out-of-scope` for this slice unless the Founder reopens it.

## Codex REV14 dispositions (rev 15 — contract alignment inside the locked Product bar)

| Finding | Severity | Disposition | Closed by | Revises |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| REV14-01 C6.7 lets a cancel take over a stale create whose event is absent and replace `pending_op` with its own cancel op before cancel T2, at which point `calendar_state` is still `pending` and `latest_action` still `NULL`. The shared create-success predicate C6.3a(1) tested only “`pending_op` no longer names my `opId`”, so the original create worker declared the creation complete and owed a 201 whose `delivery.calendar` could only be `'pending'` — which C3 forbids on a create 201, over a `(revision, NULL)` pair C5 cannot claim. The failure branch was worse: a definite delete refusal made cancel T2′ clear `pending_op`, leaving `confirmed` / `calendar_state='pending'` / `latest_action=NULL` / `pending_op=NULL` — C6.4 L0 answered 201 from it, notify could claim nothing, and C6.8 repair (reachable only from `failed`) could never fix it: a permanently unresolvable row | medium | **accepted — the success predicate is made to distinguish finalization from replacement, the inherited create is carried durably, and cancel T2′ restores it; no new product scope, no new concurrency or email guarantee.** (1) **C6.4a** names the durable, monotonic witness the contract already had but never used: the **nullness** of `latest_action` — written `NULL` exactly once by create T1 and non-null by every later writer, never back to `NULL` — so `latest_action IS NOT NULL` means “this booking's creation was finalized” and, unlike the equality test REV9-01 rightly rejected, a later reschedule or cancel cannot falsify it. Three invariants are stated and asserted: **CF-1** `calendar_state='pending' ⇔ latest_action IS NULL`, **CF-2** `pending_op IS NULL ⇒ latest_action IS NOT NULL`, **CF-3** `unfinished_create IS NOT NULL ⇒ latest_action IS NULL`. (2) **C6.3a(1)(ii)** becomes `latest_action IS NOT NULL` **and** the rev-9 clearing test; an identity match whose creation is still unfinished under another op (or a restored one) is answered the **non-terminal** 409 `operation_in_progress` — never 201, never `operation_superseded`, zero Google calls, zero deletes (the intended id is live). (3) **C6.4 L0 / C9 replay** is re-expressed against the same predicate, so replay and identity can never disagree: finalized → 201 whatever `pending_op` holds (the REV11-02 zero-Google-call exemption unchanged); unfinished with a cancel in progress → 409 `operation_in_progress`, non-terminal, zero Google calls; a create replay still never takes over a cancel at any age. (4) **C6.7 T1** carries the replaced create durably on the row — `bookings.unfinished_create` (C8) — written by exactly that one takeover (a repair never, because repair runs only from `failed`; C6.6's reschedule takeover never, because it *completes* a create, event absent included, before its own op begins), and **cancel T2** clears it while writing `latest_action='cancel'`, which is precisely why the REV9-01/REV11-01 cancelled-booking replay behaviour is preserved verbatim. (5) **Cancel T2′ recovery** restores the inherited create as `pending_op = {kind:'create', opId, gen+1, eventId, startedAt: <the original's>, attempts}` in the same owned conditional update — immediately takeable because that `startedAt` is already stale, and no longer completable by the original worker because `gen` advanced (C6.3c) — instead of clearing the row's last-operation state; CF-2 is therefore never violated by any committed row. (6) **C5** states the one case it could not express: a row with `latest_action IS NULL` has no notifiable pair, both notify forms are refused 409 `stale_revision` with `{ revision, latestAction: null }`, nothing is claimed or sent, and `delivery.email` reads `pending`. (7) **C3** records that the read/201 split is now a consequence of the predicate rather than a second rule. Codex's “preserve the existing successful cancelled-booking replay behavior” is honoured explicitly and proved | C6.4a (new), C6.3a(1)(ii) + the new non-terminal branch, C6.4 L0, C6.7 (T1 carry, T2 clear, T2′ restore), C6.8 transition table (new **cancel T2′** row), C6 column list, C8 (`unfinished_create`), C5 (no notifiable pair), C3; proofs **AC-11(r-v)** (cancel takeover of a stale create whose event is absent → the replay and the delayed create 2xx both answered 409 `operation_in_progress` with zero deletes → cancel T2 → the cancelled-booking 201 deep-equal to (r-iii) → the late event reaped) and **AC-11(r-vi)** (definite delete refusal → T2′ restores a resumable create, notify refused 409 `stale_revision` with zero ledger rows, the replay takes it over and finalizes it with one insert under the same id → 201 `calendar: 'created'`, notify then accepted, the guest's cancel retry succeeds; variants: a second cancel instead of a replay, and A's 2xx released against the restored op), plus AC-23(a)'s cancel-T2′ table row and the CF-2 invariant asserted over every committed row the suite produces | C6.3a(1)(ii) of REV9-01 (the clearing test is kept and **conjoined** with finalization, not replaced); C6.4 L0's `pending_op IS NULL → 201` (re-expressed against `latest_action`, same answer on every previously-defined input); C6.7's cancel T2′ (`pending_op = NULL` only when nothing unfinished was inherited); AC-11(r)'s `latest_action='confirm'` phrasing (nullness, not value). REV11-01, REV11-02, REV12-01, REV13-01, REV13-02 and every earlier closure stand unchanged |

Founder locks P1–P3 and the rev-11 Product bar are unchanged by the REV14 disposition. It adds no product scope, no concurrency guarantee, and no email guarantee: transient calendar overlap after a late insert, an already-claimed or stale email after a cancel, and 429-as-ambiguous remain exactly as frozen, group and collective remain scoped out, and the closure adds one nullable column whose only job is to keep a create the contract already promised never to abandon (C6.4: “a create is **never** abandoned”) actually reachable after the one takeover that replaces it. Prior closures stand. Any further finding that would widen the Product bar remains `out-of-scope` for this slice unless the Founder reopens it.

## Codex REV15 dispositions (rev 16 — contract alignment inside the locked Product bar)

| Finding | Severity | Disposition | Closed by | Revises |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| REV15-01 C5's rev-14 bullet required **both** notify forms to return 409 `stale_revision` whenever `latest_action IS NULL`, but C5's own closing sentence, C6.8's `kind='create'` → resume rule, and Approach §5 all require notify to **resume a stale pending create before claiming**. The restored create of cancel T2′ satisfies both conditions at once: AC-11(r-vi) asserted refusal while C6.8 required takeover and completion. Choosing unconditional refusal would also have removed the notify recovery AC-11(g) promises | medium | **accepted — one route-level ordering is made normative and a notification claim is distinguished from a lifecycle recovery; no new mechanism, no new guarantee.** `POST /api/bookings/{id}/notify` runs **three ordered phases**, each under the host lock, each re-reading the booking `FOR UPDATE` after the previous phase commits: **N-1 lifecycle recovery** — `pending_op.kind='create'` → non-stale 409 `operation_in_progress`, **stale → take over (C6.3 `gen+1`) and complete** per C6.4 (`events.get` → present → T2; definitely absent → one `events.insert` under the same intended id → T2; definite failure → T2′; ambiguous → 503), whose T2/T2′ writes the non-null `latest_action`, clears `unfinished_create` (CF-3), and **issues the `(revision, 'confirm')` claim itself**; `kind='calendar_repair'` → non-stale 409, stale → takeover and resume (REV4-04); `kind='reschedule'`/`'cancel'` → 409 `operation_in_progress` at any age. N-1 writes **zero** `notification_deliveries` rows and is the only phase that may take over another worker's op. **N-2 repair/reap** — C6.8's owned `calendar_repair` on a confirmed `failed` row with no `pending_op`, the bounded C6.3a reap on a cancelled row (unchanged). **N-3 the notification claim** — the two C5 request forms validated against the **re-read** `(revision, latest_action)` inside the claim transaction. **The NULL-pair refusal is scoped to N-3 and only N-3:** a claim that reaches it still carrying `latest_action IS NULL` is refused 409 `stale_revision` with `{ revision, latestAction: null }`, zero rows written, nothing sent — and CF-2 (`pending_op IS NULL ⇒ latest_action IS NOT NULL`) together with N-1 (which finalizes the create or returns first) makes that branch **unreachable on any committed row this PLAN produces**, so it is a **defensive invariant check**, logged `notify_no_pair`, asserted suite-wide never to fire. What an unfinished create actually gets from notify is N-1's answer, never `stale_revision` | C5 (the rev-14 bullet replaced by the N-1/N-2/N-3 ordering; the closing `latest_action IS NULL` sentence re-expressed), C6.8 (notify's `kind='create'` resume named as N-1; repair named as N-2), Approach §5 (`lib/notify/route-order.ts`, the three phases in order); proof **AC-11(r-vi)** split into **(r-vi-a)** the create replay arriving first (the rev-15 sequence, unchanged) and **(r-vi-b)** a notify arriving first — takes the restored create over at gen 3, one `events.insert` under the same id I, create T2 issues the `(1, 'confirm')` claim for both recipients, N-2 a no-op, N-3 finds both rows `sent` and adds no copies, **200** with `calendar: 'created'` / `email: 'sent'`, A's later 2xx answered 201 with zero deletes, the replay answered 201 with zero Google calls — plus the *variant* where notify lands inside (r-v)'s cancel window (409 `operation_in_progress`, zero ledger rows) and the suite-wide assertions that `notify_no_pair` never fires and no ledger row is ever written for a `(revision, NULL)` pair | AC-11(r-vi)'s rev-15 assertion that notify in either form is refused 409 `stale_revision` in the restored-create state (it now **recovers**); C5's rev-14 unconditional both-forms refusal (now N-3-only and defensive). C6.8's resume rule, AC-11(g), REV4-04, and every other REV14-01 clause stand unchanged |
| REV15-02 Approach §4 still prescribed `createIdentityMatches` **and** `pending_op` no longer naming the create op, saying explicitly "never `latest_action`" — precisely the predicate rev 15 replaced — and C9's remaining success text read the same way. Followed literally, AC-11(r-v) (a cancel replaces the unfinished create while `latest_action` is still `NULL`) returns 201 with `delivery.calendar: 'pending'`, contradicting C6.3a(1)(ii)'s required non-terminal 409 and C3's prohibition on `pending` in a create 201 | medium | **accepted — the rev-15 finalization predicate becomes one shared implementation and the rev-9 prohibition is narrowed to what it always meant; no new mechanism.** `createFinalized(row, op)` in `lib/booking/create-identity.ts` is the **single** implementation behind C6.3a(1)(ii), the C6.4 **L0** lookup, and the C9 same-key replay, so the three can never disagree: matching **immutable identity** (`owner_id`, `idempotency_key`, `create_fingerprint`, `id`) **and** `latest_action IS NOT NULL` (the C6.4a finalization witness) **and** the create op cleared (`pending_op IS NULL OR pending_op->>'opId' <> $opId`, the rev-9 test kept verbatim). **The prohibition is stated precisely:** what no predicate may do is compare `latest_action` against a **particular value** — `= 'confirm'`, which a later reschedule or cancel legitimately falsifies (REV9-01) — while testing the column's **nullness** is *required*, because nullness is monotonic (C6.4a) and is the only durable fact separating a create someone **finalized** from one a C6.7 cancel merely **replaced**; the rev-9 phrasing "never `latest_action`" is withdrawn as over-broad. C9's replay is re-expressed as three ordered branches against the same predicate — `pending_op.kind='create'` → resume (non-stale 409 `operation_in_progress`, stale → take over and complete, then answer from the finalized row); else `latest_action IS NOT NULL` → **201** with the row's current state whatever `pending_op` now holds (the REV11-02 zero-Google-call exemption unchanged); else → **409 `operation_in_progress`**, non-terminal, zero Google calls, nothing written — with CF-2 excluding the fourth combination. (r-ii)/(r-iii)'s intervening reschedule and cancel still replay as 201, exactly as REV9-01/REV11-01 require | Approach §4 (`reconcileSuperseded`'s create branch rewritten to `createFinalized`, both non-terminal branches named), C9 (the replay's three ordered branches; the "same identity rule answers a superseded create worker" sentence re-expressed as nullness-not-value); C6.3a(1)(ii) and C6.4a unchanged — they already stated the rev-15 predicate; proofs **AC-11(r-ii)(r-iii)(r-v)(r-vi)** all run against the one predicate | Approach §4's `createIdentityMatches` + "never `latest_action`" wording; C9's "otherwise 201" success text and its "never from `latest_action`" sentence. REV9-01's rejection of the **equality** test stands; REV11-01, REV11-02, REV14-01 stand |
| REV15-03 C2 required `Idempotency-Key` on legacy booking POSTs and C9 required 400 when missing, while AC-24 simultaneously required `tests/group-route.test.ts` and `tests/collective-route.test.ts` to pass **without modification** — and their request helpers send only `content-type` (`tests/group-route.test.ts:73–81`, `tests/collective-route.test.ts:85–93`) while their booking cases expect 201/409. Enforcing the header contract makes those preserved requests 400 before they ever exercise their fixture behaviour | medium | **accepted as the exemption Codex preferred — the header requirement is scoped to the live product's event kind, no test file is touched.** The `Idempotency-Key` check is **positioned** after the read-only catalog resolution and after the C6.9 kind gate on **every** create entry point (owner-scoped, legacy `demo`, C10 link), so a resolved `group`/`collective` kind never reaches it: **pg/live mode** answers C6.9's 501 `group_not_supported` / `collective_not_supported` first (the kind gate precedes the header check, so such a POST is never 400), and **memory mode** routes the row down its **existing** fixture path with no key, no `create_fingerprint`, no `(owner_id, idempotency_key)` record, and no `create_rejections` fence — no C9 guarantee (replay, recovery, fencing) is claimed or proved for a group or collective booking, which is exactly the "fixtures outside the C6 contract" boundary C6.9 already draws. This is an **exemption, not a request-helper change**: both test files must remain byte-for-byte unmodified. A `one_on_one` create without the header is still 400 `idempotency_key_required`, unchanged | C2 (the Book and Legacy row notes state where the check runs and that `group`/`collective` are exempt), C9 (new bullet scoping the requirement to `one_on_one` and naming the two preserved test files), C6.9 unchanged; proof **AC-24** summary plus new **(f)**: memory mode, header-less legacy POST → 400 for a `one_on_one` slug and the existing fixture outcomes for `group-4`/`collective-ab` with no key/fingerprint/fence row written; pg mode, the same header-less POST → 501 not 400; and `git diff --exit-code -- tests/group-route.test.ts tests/collective-route.test.ts` clean in the proof run | C2's unqualified "`POST` requires the same `Idempotency-Key` header" on the legacy route and C9's unqualified "the route requires it". AC-24(a)'s "pass without modification" is strengthened, not weakened; the Product bar's group/collective scope-out is unchanged |

Founder locks P1–P3 and the rev-11 Product bar are unchanged by the REV15 dispositions. Revision 16 adds **no product scope, no mechanism, no concurrency guarantee, and no email guarantee**: transient calendar overlap after a late insert, an already-claimed or stale email after a cancel, and 429-as-ambiguous remain exactly as frozen; group and collective remain memory-only fixtures scoped out of the live path, and REV15-03 is closed by holding that line rather than by extending C9 to them. All three closures state an **ordering** or a **boundary** the contract already implied — recovery before claiming, one finalization predicate, one place the header is checked — and the only thing withdrawn is wording that contradicted a rule already written down. Prior closures stand. Any further finding that would widen the Product bar remains `out-of-scope` for this slice unless the Founder reopens it.
